- **the tag `v0.2.42`**, first named for the move: `v0.2.43` came out after it the same evening, `v0.2.42` brings `tutor` nothing, and the workbook's crops of `v0.2.43` change how the workbook is built.

— *Consequence:* `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `RUN.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/workbook.md`.

## Reading into an observation

**R34. `tutor`'s reading gives an observation: the writing parts in the reading order — writing as written or a drawing described in words with all its numbers and labels — and the results with their values. `tutor` makes of it the eval protocol's observation, and `module-kit` gives the verdict on it by the check kind `number` of `spec`'s format, by the task's correct key. "Read under the key" is compared for writing by its text, for a drawing by its numbers, for a result by its value. A task may have several false keys, and each is a condition of its own. A reading run may go over several sets of sheets.**
- **A drawing and a number line** — a part of kind `drawing`. Its text is a description in words with all the numbers and labels in the drawing, as the plan's markup describes a drawing. A result may stand in any part.
- **The value of a result** — a number, as written: without the unknown, the "=" before it and the words after it. So `x = 2` gives 2, «Ответ: 5 столбов» gives 5, «56 : 6 = 9 (ост. 2)» gives 9. The value is normalized by the check kind, not by the reader.
- **The check's key** is taken from the bank's key by the same rule: the first number after the last "=", and without one, after «Ответ:». The banks of E2 write the key of an equation as the notation of the answer, `x = 2`, and do not change.
- **The verdict** is `module-kit`'s, on the protocol's observation that `reading.ObservationOf` builds from the model's answer, and the log writes the cause in the words of `tutor`'s reading. It is counted by the correct key under a false key too: a false key is needed only for "under the key". "Unreadable" — there is no result or there are several, the result's part is illegible, the result has other values, the value is empty, it is not seen in the writing of its part — `UNREADABLE_AMBIGUOUS` —, or it is not in a notation. A failure and an answer outside the schema — "unchecked".
- **Other readings** — of a part, for the reading measure, and of a result, `other_values`. Only the result's other values make a result ambiguous.
- **"Read under the key"** — a writing part whose text is in the given solution or key, and not in the sheet's markup; a drawing — the same by the row of its numbers; a result — its value equals the given key, and the markup's value does not. Without a key the same is counted by the correct key: the report's baseline.
- **The key's block** — a marked block of data: the key and the solution and the words "this is not what the learner wrote". The words "may be correct or not" are not in it: the right condition is such as the product would send it.
- **What is crossed out** the model skips, and the stub does not take it.
- **False keys** — each false key of the task gives a condition of its own on a correct sheet, and the call records its key.
- **Sets** — `-set DIRECTORY,BANK,PLAN`, once per set, one estimate, one "yes", one ceiling and one log. The directories are in one folder, `samples/` and `synthetic/` are not mixed. The log's name names the sets through "+", in the order of the `-set` flags: `…-reading-free+lines-dev.jsonl`. The judge takes one set and answers two sets with a refusal before the question.
- **Reading a number** — `internal/value`: the reading of a number's value and the normalization, by the format's rules for check kinds and their vectors; the verdict is not its. NFKC is `golang.org/x/text`, at the version `go.mod` sets: the module was already in the module graph through the Anthropic SDK, the license BSD-3-Clause. The vectors are read by `gopkg.in/yaml.v3`, only in tests: that module was in the module graph too.

— *Why:* a description of a drawing in words repeats the markup, so the reading measure of a drawing can be counted by the same markup. A drawing that is correct, but described in the words of the bank's solution, would by its text give a false "under the key", and the threshold of one call against two tolerates no reading under the key. Without the rule of the value, the equation `x = 2` and the answer «5 столбов» are not in a notation of the kind `number`, and every such sheet would be "unreadable". The verdict by the correct key under a false one measures whether the key shifts reading, not what comes out if the key is wrong. The verdict is `module-kit`'s: only a report with its verdicts is fit for admission, and the runner judges as `api` will.

— *Rejected:*
- **a drawing outside the observation** — the result and labels of a drawing would not be visible to the verdict and to the reading measure;
- **a drawing by the text of its description** in "under the key" — a false "under the key" on a correct drawing;
- **the value "as written" whole** — `x = 2` and «5 столбов» are not in the kind's notation, and all such sheets are "unreadable";
- **the key as a number in the bank** — the banks of E2 do not change;
- **the words "may be correct or not" in the key's block** — the right condition would be weaker than what the product will send;
- **ambiguity by the other readings of a part** — «5 столба» against «5 столбов» would be "unreadable";
- **a report over several logs** — one run over all the sets gives one estimate, one ceiling and one log, and the report sees all the sheets for the paired tests.

— *Consequence:* `internal/value/`; `internal/reading/`; `internal/runner/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The crop and the states

**R35. Reading sees the crop of the work by the crop rule of the eval protocol — `canvas`'s crop rule as it is: the numbers of the crop are to the bit, and the pixels are drawn by our own render in integers, the line of a stroke without a style max(D′, 12) / 12 px, and of a stroke with a style its width at the crop's scale, in its colour. The digit heights are 16, 24, 32 and 48 px, the main one 32. The states are moments of writing that the device would find: after a line, at a pause and before an erasure; only Haiku reads them, without the key and once each. The readings per sheet under each policy of reading at a pause are counted by code, without AI.**
- **The numbers of the crop** — to the bit, as at `canvas`. These are the bundles, `h_med`, D′, the size, the margins, `sheet_box` and the group boxes, in integers `int64`. A test checks them against the vectors of `spec`, `contracts.Vectors` `eval/v1/crops.json`: the cases of `canvas` and `spec`'s own of strokes with a style, at four heights. The vectors give `h_med` as it is, `bundle_median`, and the scale takes it no higher than 1000 tenths. The eval protocol takes the rule and the vectors as they are, and `tutor`'s runner draws the states and the frames by the same rule. The PNG of the crop by the protocol is `Crop.PNG`.
- **The line** — without a style max(D′, 12) / 12 px: 1.33, 2, 2.67 and 4 px, as `canvas`'s crop rule sets it; with a style its width at the crop's scale, 1 px at least, and the margins and the group boxes hold the widest line.
- **The pixels** — our own render in integers. A point is placed at a 48th of a pixel by the integers of the crop, a pixel has 4 × 4 samples, the ink is black without a style, and with one its colour at its opacity, a marker's multiplied with what lies under it. Against the raster of `canvas` the tone of a pixel may differ by one level of a channel, and the vectors of `canvas` set only the numbers. The judge's pictures do not change: its pen keeps the former 16ths, 3 px and #111.
- **Each picture has its own scale** — by its strokes: the finished sheet, the frame before an erasure and each state. So the device counts it too.
- **The heights** 16, 24 and 48 px — without the key, once each and only for Haiku. A height whose D′ coincided with one already read on this sheet, 32 px first, is not read, and the call names both D and D′.
- **`h_med` and lowercase letters:**
  - on sheets without lowercase letters `h_med` is within 10% of the digit height of the synthetic sheets on all 10 sheets of E2;
  - on sheets with lowercase letters — «Ответ: 5 столбов», «в точке 10», `3x` — this is so only on 20 sheets of E2 out of 30 and on 21 sheets of free writing out of 40. The synthetic sheets write lowercase letters in print and apart. Each letter is a bundle of its own about half a digit tall, and where they are half of the bundles, the lower median is one of them. The digits of such a crop come out larger than D, up to twice, and the crop may be wider than the sheet: 1016 × 496 at 32 px on sheet 1 of free writing;
  - the rule is the protocol's, and `tutor` does not change it. The measurement measures the heights by D, as the module manifest sets them, and the report splits the pairs of heights by sheets with lowercase letters and without them.
- **The states** — after an event of writing, in the writing order of the log of the course (`journal.Writing`):
  - **after a line** — the end of writing on a line or a part of the log of the course before writing on another, with an erasure between them or without. The lines and parts of the log are without the markup, as at the device;
  - **at a pause** — the longest pause no shorter than 0.4 s between consecutive strokes in the markup's answer line and in one other line where such a pause is longer, except the crossed-out and illegible ones;
  - **before an erasure** — before each run of erasures with no stroke between them;
  - **the truth** — how far each part of the markup is finished by the moment.

  Only Haiku reads them, without the key, at 32 px and once each, and "under the key" for them is counted by the markup of the finished sheet.
- **The policies** (О-8) — readings beyond the submission: after each line and at each pause no shorter than 0.4, 2 and 6 s between neighbouring events of writing, without waiting until the first stroke.
- **What a run reads** — `-reads keys,heights,states`, by default `keys`: the key conditions, the other heights and the states are run separately.
- **The prompt** of reading does not change for the crop: the picture is the crop, and its size stands in the text.

— *Why:* the crop rule is already written down in `canvas` and accepted by the eval protocol: the measurement measures the crop that the device will send, and a rule of our own would give other pictures. Pixels in integers keep the references the same on any machine, as for the render of a sheet. The moments are by strokes and erasures, as the device would find them. The markup is needed only to choose which pauses to read, and for the truth. The device does not know the answer line, and the measurement so measures "1" of "12".

— *Rejected:*
- **the raster of `canvas` in `float64`, byte for byte as at the device** — Go may fuse a multiplication with an addition (FMA) in any place, "possibly across statements", and CI on amd64 would not catch such a miss;
- **a scale rule of our own**, by the median of the groups — `canvas`'s search rejected it, and the protocol took the bundles;
- **a constant line of 3 px** and a line as on the screen — `canvas`'s crop rule rejected both: bold at 16 px and thin at 48;
- **the end of a line by the markup** — the device does not know the markup. On the sheets of code the lines of the log and the parts of the markup coincide;
- **states for Opus and with the key** — Opus goes by the reduced matrix, each condition once, without heights and states, and the states are read without the key.

— *Consequence:* `internal/render/`; `internal/lines/`; `internal/journal/`; `internal/states/`; `internal/reading/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Sheets with a near-key

**R36. The near-key is measured on 12 sheets of code: six tasks of E2, a correct sheet and a sheet with an error in the answer for each, and their false key is a near-false one, with the last digit of the key replaced by the similar digit of its pair. The last digit of the answer on the sheet leans toward the digit of its pair by the share 0.1.** The sheets are listed in [near-sheets.md](near-sheets.md).
- **The pairs of digits** — 1 and 7, 4 and 9, 8 and 3, 5 and 6, one for each digit. The tasks — `chain-2` 11 and 17, `equation-3` 7 and 1, `equation-4` 4 and 9, `equation-2` 8 and 3, `chain-1` 38 and 33, `posts` 5 and 6.
- **The bank `eval/near-bank.json`** — copies of the tasks of E2 word for word, but for the false keys: each has one false key, a near-false one. Its solution is the correct one, with the same digit replaced in the last line, so that the key differs from the sheet by one digit and nothing else. The bank of E2 does not change, and a test keeps the copies equal.
- **The plan `eval/near-sheets.json`** — format v0, 12 sheets of the scenario `similar-digits`:
  - the correct one — the lines of the correct solution, and it is read with the near-false key;
  - the one with an error — the same lines with the near-false key in the answer, the first error is the answer line, without a trap, and it is read with the correct key.

  The plan's format does not change: the strict reader requires every field, and a new field would have to be added to the 40 sheets of E2.
- **The synthetic sheets** draw the last digit of the answer line shifted toward the digit of its pair by the share `synth.SimilarShift`. Both strokes are taken at the same places along their length — at all vertices of both and at 24 places evenly between them —, the second in the direction where its points are nearer to the first, and the points are mixed: each digit of the font has one stroke. At share 0 the digit is the same as the font's. A digit without a pair, an answer with an erasure and a sheet of parts do not lean.
- **The share** is the largest of 0.5, 0.4, 0.3, 0.2 and 0.1 at which Opus without the key reads correctly no fewer than 11 sheets of 12, chosen on sheets of the same plan drawn with seed 2, not on the set that goes into the test. Opus · `high` without the key read correctly: at 0.5 — 0 sheets of the 8 read, the ceiling stopped the run; at 0.4 — 5 of 12; at 0.3 — 7 of 12; at 0.2 — 9 of 12; at 0.1 — 11 of 12. Almost all misses are "unreadable": the model named other values. One false "correct" without the key happened at 0.5 and at 0.4: a one leaning toward 7 was read as 7, and that is exactly the key. The choice cost $1.52.
- **The split** — one. The set `synthetic/near` is drawn with seed 1 at the chosen share, before the first edit of the prompt, and split by `split -seed 70 -dev 4`: in the dev part, sheets 7, 9, 11 and 12 — three correct and one with an error. The split depends on the file names, so the set is not redrawn.

— *Why:* a key that differs from the sheet by one similar digit checks what the false keys of E2 do not check — other steps with another number: whether the model will "finish reading" the digit it expects. A correct sheet with a near-false key shows reading the key instead of what is written, and a sheet with an error and the correct key shows a false "correct", the main risk of one call. The share of the shift sets how similar the digit is: too strong a shift no one will read, and then every model would fail the threshold of one call against two, whose tolerance for reading under the key is zero. Choosing on another seed does not touch the sheets of the test.

— *Rejected:*
- **0 and 6, 2 and 7** — 6 and 7 would have two pairs each, and the synthetic sheets choose the pair by the digit itself;
- **a field of the shift in the plan** — format v0 requires every field, and the plan of E2 would have to change;
- **the former false key beside the near-false one** — the set of lines of E2 measures it, and here it would only multiply the calls;
- **a share chosen on the sheets of the test** — it would be chosen by the same readings that are measured afterwards.

— *Consequence:* `eval/near-bank.json`; `eval/near-sheets.json`; `docs/near-sheets.md`; `internal/synth/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The outcome of the reading measurement

**R37. The threshold of one call against two on the test part of the synthetic sheets was passed by all three configurations of the measurement — Opus 5.5 · `high`, Haiku 5.5 · `low` and Haiku 5.5 · `low` without thinking: not one false "correct", not one false alarm and not one reading under the key. But the key shifts Haiku's reading toward "unreadable", the near-key is measured at the weakest shift of the digit, and the handwriting is synthetic, so `tutor` advises `spec` to keep two calls until the threshold is passed on real handwriting. The advice to `spec` on the digit height is 16 px. Reading unfinished work is dangerous: a verdict before the answer comes in almost half of the readings after a line. These are `tutor`'s answers to `spec` on Т-23, Т-33, О-8, О-21 and the default digit height.** The numbers — measurements 3.5.
- **Т-23.** On the test part, 68 sheets of three sets, each configuration met all four points of the threshold. Not one sheet has d ≠ 0: there was nothing to fail the paired test with, and the points with zero tolerance were not broken once.
- **The shift from the key.** On the near-key sheets the key shifts Haiku's reading toward "unreadable", not toward a false verdict. On the sheets with an error Haiku · `low` with the correct key gave the correct verdict in 4 calls of 15, without the key in 12. For Opus the key changed nothing.
- **Two readers of one provider** (Т-33) released 61 and 51 sheets of 68, with no errors among those released. A pair of different providers is not measured.
- **The digit height** by the rule written down before the run is 16 px: for both configurations of Haiku 16 px is no worse than 32 px, and 48 px for Haiku · `low` is worse — 12 extra "unreadable" against 5, p 0.033.
- **О-8 and О-21.** After a line Haiku · `low` gave "correct" or "not yet" before the answer was written in 94 calls of 196, and a result that the work does not give yet in 113. The policy of pauses from 0.4 s would read a sheet 15.6 times on average beyond the submission.

— *Why:* so says the threshold of one call against two, set before the run and applied to the test part; the advice on the height follows the rule written down before the run. What this means for the platform, `spec` decides: Т-23, Т-33, О-8 and О-21 are questions of `spec`'s registers, and `tutor` passes its answers to `spec`.

— *Caveats,* which each answer to `spec` carries:
- the handwriting is synthetic: even, without slips, except the intended ones. Real handwriting is only the author's three sheets, and there is no child's at all (П-16, П-18);
- the sheets are easy for Opus: without the key it read 66 sheets of 68 correctly. A test on d with zero differences says nothing about a model that errs more often;
- on the sheets with lowercase letters the digits of the crop are larger than the height from the manifest, so 16 px here is the nominal D, not the digit height in the picture;
- the near-key is measured at a shift share of 0.1 — the weakest of the grid: at a larger one Opus without the key did not read 90% of the sheets. The digit is tilted barely, and the check of the near-key is weaker than intended;
- the pauses of the states are chosen, not all taken: the longest in the answer line and in one other line. The share of verdicts before the answer at a pause is not the share over all pauses of the 0.4 s policy.

— *Rejected:*
- **editing the prompt after the first round** — not one error from its words repeated, and an edit for a single sheet would fit the prompt to the development;
- **counting extra "unreadable" with the key as a failure of the threshold** — the threshold's d counts only false alarms and false "correct", and "unreadable" is safe.

— *Consequence:* `docs/measurements.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The hint

**R38. The hint in the hint measurement is one call of analysis and hint, as `spec`'s scheme of that call draws it: the crop of the finished sheet at 32 px, the task statement, the rung, the language and the presentation style, and the key, the solution and the task's traps with descriptions as a marked block of data. The call gets no observation of reading, no matched trap and no techniques, and there is one presentation style: a child in primary school. The model gives the box of the first error, the task's trap and the hint. The box goes into fractions 0–1000 of the crop, as the product's adapter gives it, and then into a part of the sheet, `judge.Hit`.**
- **There is no observation of reading.** `spec`'s scheme passes this call the work, the key and the solution, the trap, the rung, the presentation style, the language and the age category, and not the observation. Of the reading the call knows only the verdict "not yet": the hint is called only after a wrong answer.
- **There is no matched trap and no techniques.** The measurement's banks have neither the trap values, `trap_values`, by which the program finds the matched trap, nor the task's techniques. The block of data is the key, the solution and the task's traps that the AI may suppose, with descriptions. This is a legitimate case in the product too: a wrong value matched no trap, and the module has no technique catalog. Version 1 of the prompt, the measurement's, has no words about the matched trap and the techniques; the product's version of the prompt gives them, and its own eval measures it.
- **The presentation style** — one for all calls: "a child in primary school; use short, simple, everyday words". The module manifest v0 does not name a style (О-23), and the banks' tasks are olympiad maths of primary school.
- **The answer** — the box of the first error in the pixels of the crop or `null`, the task's trap or `null`, and the hint. The report compares the model's trap with the sheet's trap from the markup, and the box with the part of the first error. Neither is given to the call.
- **The box** goes the product's way. The adapter clamps it to the picture and converts it into integer fractions 0–1000 of the crop by its sent size, and the fractions go into units of the sheet by the place of the crop on the sheet, `sheet_box`, as the device would do it. A hit is `judge.Hit`, as for the judge. A box without area on the picture has no place.

— *Why:*
- `spec`'s scheme is what `api` will send. The observation in the measurement would have to be taken from the markup, and that is an ideal reading, or one more reading call would have to be made before each hint.
- Trap values on the measurement's sheets would almost always give the sheet's own trap — the report's reference —, and the answer about the trap would prompt the model itself.
- One style does not double the calls of the measurement.
- Fractions 0–1000 are what the product's adapter gives, and the measurement goes the same way as the product.

— *Rejected:*
- **the observation of reading as a separate block** — the argument above;
- **trap values and techniques as a separate file of eval**, without changing the banks — the argument above, and the author would have to proofread new texts;
- **only techniques** — the banks do not have them, and without the matched trap the measurement still does not measure the whole block of data of the scheme;
- **two styles, a child and an adult,** — twice as many calls of the measurement: about $10 instead of $4.8 by the kind's estimate; **no style** — the words "for the age" would have nothing to attach to;
- **the box straight in units of the sheet, without fractions** — so the measurement would not go the same way as the product.

— *Consequence:* `internal/hint/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R39. The run checks each hint of the hint measurement as `api` checks it before the device: by the leak check by the text of `spec`'s format and by the form check — exactly one question, no more than two sentences and 30 words, the letters of the hint's language. If it failed even one, or the call failed, the learner reads the fallback text: the rung template in the hint's language. The report gives the leaks before the check and after it, form violations, the fallback text, the place and the trap — by configuration, language and rung —, and as a third file about 30 hints for the author. The measurement's prompt is version 1, frozen after two rounds of development; its paid runs have a ceiling of $50, and the author's three sheets get only the judge and reading, without the hint.**
- **The leak check** — `internal/leak`, by `spec`'s format text: the key of each check of a number, a fraction and a choice is looked for by value, if the statement and the texts of its item do not show it, and the leak phrases always. The reference is the 17 leak vectors of `spec`, their copy in `testdata`, and the edges are by `spec`'s second implementation, `tools/format-check/leaks.mjs`. A task of the bank has one item: the key is the value of the bank's key, `reading.KeyOf`, and the phrases are `leaks`.
- **What this means on the measurement's banks:**
  - for `equation-4` and `posts` the key stands in the statement: 4 in "5x − 4 = 2x + 8" and 5 in «через каждые 5 м». It is not looked for, and only the leak phrases hold the answer, so "x = 4" and «5 столбов» will pass the check. The banks of the sheets do not change;
  - `rabbits` has no two in the statement: «5 голов и 14 ног». The legitimate «у курицы 2 ноги» the check will catch as the key, and the learner will get the fallback text. The format leaves «2 ноги» unsought only for a task whose statement names the two;
  - numbers in words are caught only by leak phrases, and the banks have none.
- **The form** — `hint.Form`, on the text after NFKC:
  - exactly one sentence is a question; a sentence ends with a run of ".", "!" and "?", among which closing quotation marks and brackets may stand, and after it a space, a capital letter or the end;
  - no more than two sentences;
  - from 1 to 30 words, a word being a run of letters, marks and digits, and a point, a comma or a fraction slash between two digits does not break it: `0.5` is one word;
  - the letters — Cyrillic in a Russian hint, where a Latin letter that the statement itself shows, as `x`, may also stand, and Latin in an English one.

  An abbreviation like «т. е.» the rule counts as the end of a sentence — this is its limit. The prompt names the limit of words too, in point 6: "no more than two short sentences and 30 words".
- **The platform's form** of a hint is `spec`'s: exactly one question, no more than two sentences, the letters of the explanation language and a word limit by language — 30 in Russian and 35 in English, and in a language the measurement did not measure, no limit. `api` checks it, before the hint goes to the device, with the code of `tutor` — the same as in its eval. The measurement measured with a limit of 30 words in both languages, as version 1 of the prompt names it, and its numbers stand as they are; `hint.Count` counts the words and sentences as the platform's check does.
- **The fallback text** — the templates of rungs 1 and 2 in Russian and English, and on rung 3 the template of rung 2: the banks have no trap text of the value, no trap advice and no simpler case, which the format puts before the template. The templates are different, without numbers and gender, and themselves pass both checks on each task of the three banks. The cause of the fallback text is a failure of the call or an answer outside the schema, a leak or the form; with a leak and a form violation at once — a leak.
- **The log** — for a call: which keys and, separately, which phrases the leak check found, the form violations, the cause of the fallback text, what the learner will read and what the check finds in the fallback text. The last is empty by construction: this is exactly "leaks after the check". For a sheet — the statement, the key the check looks for and the phrases, and also the text of the part of the first error from the plan. For the run — the mark `checked`: a log without it the report does not take, otherwise it would read as if there were no leaks.
- **The report** — `report.NewHint`:
  - leaks before the check — a key or a phrase;
  - leaks after it;
  - form violations by rule;
  - the fallback text by cause;
  - the box in the part of the first error;
  - the model's trap against the sheet's trap;
  - time and cost;
  - leaks by task.

  The shares come with an upper bound of 95% by sheets. The third file, `.hints.md`, is the hints of the first repeat for proofreading: first those the leak check caught, up to 10, then those that passed, round the configurations, languages and rungs. What the check missed, only the reader sees.
- **The prompt** of the measurement is version 1, fingerprint `d8a2f994b15559ee489b62fcfeac51544ffd3d627444d1e20bedcb63b11654cc`, frozen after two rounds of development. In the first round Opus repeated errors that came from the words of the prompt: it praised the learner's correct line and repeated it, and that line had a leak phrase in it, and the three parts of the hint — the praise, the place and the question — came out as three sentences or longer than 30 words. The second round edited point 6:
  - exactly two sentences, no more than 30 words: the first says what is already correct, the second is one question;
  - name the learner's writing in words and do not copy its numbers;
  - in Russian «здесь всё верно», not «ты верно нашёл».

  In development the leaks of Opus fell from 5 to 0, and the hints of Haiku with a broken form from 24 to 11. Both rounds are version 1: the first ran on an early text, `f53ff9e1…`.
- **The result** (measurements 3.6): Opus 5.5 · `high` would have got the fallback text in 30 calls of 204, and 24 of them are an English hint longer than 30 words. Of the 49 hints that the leak check caught, 4 name or confirm the answer, all of them Haiku's. The rest repeat the learner's correct line, where a leak phrase stands, or carry a number of the key not as the answer — in a case like rung 3. Point 6 of version 1 says to begin with praise even where the error is in the first line, and the model praises what is wrong; the product's version of the prompt only allows such a first sentence. Development cost $2.11, the test $3.26, in all $5.37 against the ceiling of $50.
- **The ceiling** of the measurement's paid runs — $50, for no more than two rounds of development and the test with one repeat each: it is set high so that no run is cut off.
- **The author's three sheets** get only the judge v2 and reading: all three are correct, and the hint is called only after a wrong answer. An agent starts the runs on them: `samples/` is open to agents, and a paid run on it starts only within its task's ceiling, here $2. The runs go by the recipe `just live`, which sets a ceiling of $1 for each run, so together they do not go over $2. Reading in it is with all kinds, `-reads keys,heights,states`: by the estimate $0.14 for all the reading instead of $0.11 for the keys alone, and live writing with a mouse gives the first data on heights and states not on synthetic sheets. The judge cost $0.61, reading $0.17. The result (measurements 3.6): Opus 5.5 · `high` judges all 36 answers correctly, and there are 54 readings per sheet on pauses from 0.4 s, three and a half times more than on the synthetic sheets. The markup of an author's sheet is one part without the answer's value, so "under the key" is not measured on it.

— *Why:*
- 30 words are two short sentences of about 15 words each. At MathTrail's canvas-api the limit of a question was 200 characters: that is about 30 English words.
- The form leads to the fallback text, as in canvas-api, where a question longer than two sentences and 200 characters was not shown; `spec` makes the form check one of the program's checks of the tutor's words before the device.
- A model is judged by the rule it is told: so the limit stands in the prompt too.
- A call on a correct sheet is not the call that `api` will send, and a false alarm on the author's sheets the judge measures anyway.

— *Rejected:*
- **20 words a sentence**, as the longest sentence for grades 1–2 in MathTrail's standalone: up to 40 words a hint, and "two short sentences" is less;
- **200 characters**, as canvas-api: in Russian the words are longer, and fewer words would fit in the same limit than in English;
- **tables of the key in words** for the check after it: the format gives numbers in words to leak phrases;
- **a hint on the author's correct sheets too** — the argument above.

— *Consequence:* `internal/leak/`; `internal/hint/`; `words/`; `internal/report/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The workbook

**R40. The workbook of the workbook measurement is 20 synthetic pages of 10 items and 11 boxes each: the file `eval/workbook.json` of the format `tutor-workbook` v0, whose pages `tutor-eval draw -workbook` draws as samples of the eval protocol. A box has a check `number` or `fraction` of `spec`'s format, one of seven scenarios and what is written in it. A page is 1000 × 1400 units, a box 100 × 100, each item has a row of its own, and the writing of a box does not go out of it. The crop is by the strokes of the box.** The workbook in detail is [workbook.md](workbook.md).
- **The items of a page.** Ten items of school maths of grade 3, the subject of `spec`'s workbook module, on every page the same in kind, and the numbers their own:
  - five are `number`: multiplication, division, an unknown addend, subtraction of three-digit numbers with borrowing, and division with a remainder. The last has two boxes, the quotient and the remainder, as in `spec`'s vector about the quotient and the remainder;
  - three are `fraction` with `form: any`: addition and subtraction of fractions with the same denominator and a fraction of an hour, a kilogram or a metre;
  - two are `fraction` with `form: reduced`: reducing a fraction and addition with the answer as a fraction in lowest terms. The item line itself says that the answer is a fraction in lowest terms, as the format text requires.

  Each box has its own trap with a clear name — `wrong-operation`, `no-borrow`, `adds-denominators`, `not-in-lowest-terms` and others.
- **The scenarios of a box** — one box on a page for each of six: error, near-key, similar digits, mirrored digit, crossed out and blank. The other five boxes are correct. On 20 pages this makes 100 correct boxes and 20 boxes of each other scenario. The code picks the box of each scenario at random among those where the scenario is possible:
  - **error** — the value of the first trap;
  - **near-key** — the key with one digit replaced by the digit of its pair: 1 and 7, 4 and 9, 8 and 3, 5 and 6. This digit leans toward the key's digit by the share `synth.SimilarShift`, 0.1;
  - **similar digits** — the key, and its last digit, one of a pair, leans toward the digit of its pair;
  - **mirrored digit** — the key, and the first of its digits 2, 5, 7 and 9 is written mirrored;
  - **crossed out** — the value of the first trap, struck through with a stroke, and the key beside it in the same box;
  - **blank** — nothing.

  "A fraction stacked and inline" is not a scenario but a field of the box: the code writes each filled-in fraction now stacked, now inline, in turn, across the scenarios. The report splits by it.
- **How a box lies on the page.** The numbers are those of `canvas`'s layout: a page of 1000 × 1400, a box of 100 × 100, rows 32 apart, margins of 40, font size 30. The layout is simpler than in `canvas`: each item has a row of its own, and the box stands after the text before it, by the widths of the printed characters — the digits and the signs of operations, as in `canvas`, while the other characters have one width. The writing is fitted to the box and does not go out of it. So by `canvas`'s box rule each stroke belongs to a box of its own, there is no draft, and a test checks this.
- **The crop** — `render.CropOf` by the strokes of the box: only the ink, without the border and the text of the line. At 16, 24 and 32 px all 200 crops are 200 × 200, 64 tokens, and a page 640. At 48 px ten of them are wider, up to 265 px: these are inline fractions with two-digit parts and the crossed-out ones.
- **The format** — `tutor-workbook` v0, read strictly, like the sheet plan:
  - a page has a number and a part, `dev` or `test`: pages 1–5 are development, 6–20 the test;
  - an item has a line with boxes `[ ]` and the boxes in order;
  - a box has a check, a scenario, `written`, `crossed` and `fraction`. The check is as in the format: the kind, the key, the form of the fraction and the values of the traps. `written` is what is written, as a person would read it, `crossed` the crossed-out value, `fraction` stacked or inline.

  The file's check lists all the errors by their paths. It takes the key only in the kind's notation, and with `reduced` only in lowest terms (`task.fraction-key`). The values of the traps are "not yet" and differ from each other (`task.trap-values`). It matches the box's scenario against what is written in it. A page has from 1 to 10 items.
- **The check of a box** is `module-kit`'s, by the format's task of the page, `task.Page`: the verdict of a box and of an item is `observation.Verdicts`, and the form of a fraction in the file is checked by `formatv0.CheckForm`.
- **The pairs of similar digits** — one table, `sample.PairOf`. The file's check and `synth.Similar` take it, and the sheets of the bank and of the near-key do not change because of it.
- **The pages as samples.** `tutor-eval draw -workbook` writes the strokes of the pages as samples of the eval protocol, `page-NN.json`, by default into `synthetic/workbook`, and with `-png` saves the crop of each filled-in box at 32 px — what the model will see — only in `synthetic/` or outside the work tree. The seed and the workbook file give the same strokes. The summary and the run read the directory of samples.
- **The command** `tutor-eval workbook` checks the file, reads the samples of its directory and prints a summary: the boxes by scenario, by kind and by how the fraction is written, the crops and the tokens per page at each height. `tutor-eval check` takes the workbook file too. Both commands also check the layout, `synth.CheckLayout`: an item line wider than the page is an error with the path to it, not a drawing past the edge.
- **The ceiling** of the measurement's paid runs is $100.

— *Why:*
- Each scenario is on each page, so each page measures each scenario, and the paired comparisons by page see all the scenarios.
- The numbers of `canvas`'s layout — because the device cuts the crop of a box by its strokes, and the box of 100 × 100 sets their place and size.
- The writing inside the box — because writing past its edge would become a draft by the box rule and would not reach reading. This is a question of the layout and of the eval protocol, not of reading a slot.
- The split by page, not by item — one call reads the page whole.
- 10 items on a page — the number the measurement's plan names, and so a page costs 640 tokens of crops.

— *Rejected:*
- **a port of `canvas`'s layout** — its tables of widths and of line breaks: the layout decides only where the handwriting writes, and it does not get into the crop of a box;
- **up to 20 short items, two to a row**, as `canvas` lays them out, — the measurement's plan names 10;
- **a part for each item** — a call reads the page whole, and the part belongs to the page;
- **"a stacked fraction" in the item line** — there is none in a line of format v0. Only the learner writes a fraction stacked, in the box;
- **a decimal fraction in a fraction box** as one more scenario — it checks only the verdict code, and `spec`'s vectors hold that.

— *Consequence:* `eval/workbook.json`; `internal/sample/`; `internal/synth/`; `internal/workbook/`; `internal/evalsample/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `docs/workbook.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `CLAUDE.md`.

**R41. Reading the workbook is one call per page. The model sees the crops of the filled-in boxes, each with a caption naming the item and the box, and in the condition with a caption also the item line. For each slot it returns the text, whether it is legible, other readings and whether it is blank. The program gives the slot's verdict, `module-kit`'s by the box's check, and the item's verdict is made up of the verdicts of its slots. A blank box does not go to the model.**
- **The request** — the package `internal/workbook`, the call kind `workbook` on the runner. The instructions are in English, as for reading a sheet. Each picture comes after the caption "Image k: item i, slot j.". The place of a slot in the answer is `{ item, slot }`, as in `spec`'s observation. The request's text names the number of boxes.
- **A fraction** — the prompt says to write it as the numerator, a slash and the denominator, "3/4", however it is written — with a slash or stacked. The stub's right reader returns it so too, and `spec` has the same rule for a reader: a fraction written vertically is given in a line, "numerator/denominator".
- **What is crossed out** the model skips, and in the prompt of version 1 a mirrored digit is the same digit, as for reading a sheet.
- **The answer** — `cells`: each slot has `item`, `slot`, `text`, `legible`, `other_readings` and `blank`, strictly by the schema.
- **The verdict of a slot** — `workbook.Verdicts`:
  - a blank box is "unreadable", `blank`, and goes to no model;
  - a failed call or an answer outside the schema is "unchecked" for every box of the page, the blank ones too. An answer without a slot for a box or with two is not a valid observation, and every box of the page is "unchecked" too, the box without its slot or with two with the reason `missing` or `twice`;
  - a slot read as blank is "unreadable", `blank`; an illegible one, `illegible`; one with other readings, `ambiguous`;
  - otherwise, the check of the box by the slot's text: "correct", "not yet" with a trap, or "unreadable", `notation`.

  A slot at a place where there is no filled-in box decides nothing. The item's verdict is made up of its boxes.
- **The conditions** — `bare`, the crops alone, and `captioned`, where the caption also carries the item line with `[ ]`, and the instructions a paragraph about it: "Read only what the image shows." So the measurement measures whether reading needs the item's text.
- **The run** — `tutor-eval run -kind workbook -part dev|test|all`; the pages come from `-workbook`, by default `eval/workbook.json`, and their samples, which `draw -workbook` draws, from the directory `-dir`. The workbook's `-reads` are `captions`, by default, and `heights`:
  - `captions` — the page at 32 px, `bare` and `captioned`. Opus 5.5 · `high` reads once each, both Haikus in every repeat;
  - `heights` — `bare` at 16, 24 and 48 px, once each and only for Haiku, as for reading a sheet.

  The run takes no sheets, and writes its log in `synthetic/runs`: the pages are code. A page without filled-in boxes does not go to the model, and a run that has nothing to ask is not planned. The log is the run record, a record per page, `page`, with the truth of each box, and a record per call, with the verdicts of the boxes and the items.
- **The estimate** — about 700 tokens of text and, for each crop, its tokens and 15 tokens of caption, 20 more with the item's text. The output is about 400 tokens for Haiku without thinking, 900 for Haiku · `low` and 1,200 for Opus. Development is 5 pages, 70 calls, about $0.33. The test with heights is 15 pages, 300 calls, about $1.04.

— *Why:* `spec`'s workbook request carries a list "item, box, crop", one call per page. The verdict is the program's. The rest is as for reading a sheet, so that the two measurements of reading are comparable: the words "as it is written", the crossed-out, illegible and other readings. "Unchecked" for a missing slot — because this is a failure of reading, not a doubt about the handwriting: "unreadable" would ask the learner to rewrite what they wrote legibly.

— *Rejected:*
- **a call per box** — 11 times more calls, and the workbook's request is one per page;
- **the value and the marks on top of the sheet's observation** — the parts and the results, `parts` and `results`: a box has one answer, and there is no result to look for;
- **"unreadable" for a missing slot** — the argument is above;
- **the item's text in the instructions as one list** — a caption at each crop is closer to what `api` would give, if the measurement shows that the caption is needed;
- **the picture's size in the text**, as for reading a sheet, — almost all crops are 200 × 200, and the answer has no bounding boxes.

— *Consequence:* `internal/workbook/`; `internal/runner/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R42. The outcome of the workbook measurement: the prompt for reading the slots is frozen at version 1 without edits, and the item's text does not go into reading a slot. On the workbook's synthetic set without a caption no configuration gave a false "correct", and the false alarms are almost all on mirrored digits and crossed-out values.** The criterion was written down before the first run; it and the numbers are measurements 3.7.
- **Development** — pages 1–5, one round, 70 calls, $0.24. The errors are in mirrored digits and crossed-out values, not from the words of the prompt, and the rule "edit only an error that repeats and comes from the words of the prompt" gave no edit. Version 1 is frozen, fingerprint `40a9244ba646d1edef4c99c6dc5060b5b38ccab74ad56ddf76792545b6590e8d`.
- **The test** — pages 6–20, 300 calls, $0.72 against a ceiling of $4.
- **The item's text** — by the rule written down before the run it does not go into reading a slot: Haiku 5.5 · `low` without thinking has one false "correct" with the caption, which it does not have without it. For Opus 5.5 · `high` and Haiku · `low` it is not shown that the caption helps. So `api` gives the reading of a slot no caption.
- **Heights** — 16, 24 and 48 px are no worse than 32 px for both Haikus. The workbook gives `spec` no advice on the digit height.
- **Ceilings** — $2 and $4, above the plan's $3, within the measurement's $100. $0.96 was spent.

— *Why:* the rule about the item's text was written down before the run, and it worked; the report measures the other numbers without a threshold, as the criterion says. The prompt without edits — because no error of development came from its words, as for reading a sheet.

— *Rejected:*
- **editing the prompt for the sake of mirrored digits** — the prompt of version 1 already says that a mirrored digit is the same digit. A mirrored 5 looks like a 2 to a person too, and an edit would fit the prompt to five pages of development;
- **redrawing the crossed-out values after development** — the workbook's data were frozen before development with the hash of the 20 pages, and a change of the drawing between development and the test would make the test not the one that was planned. This limitation is written down in the result;
- **the caption for Haiku without thinking**, which has fewer errors with it at p 0.0002, — the rule written down before the run says the opposite, and one model does not change the decision: the caption is a contract of `api`, shared by all models.

— *Consequence:* `internal/report/`; `internal/workbook/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `docs/measurements.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Latin letters in the synthetic sheets

**R43. The synthetic sheets write English text with strokes of their own: lowercase and capital Latin letters and the signs of English text are drawn as polylines in the font's frame, like the Russian letters. There is no one else's stroke font in the code, and no license has to be checked.**
- **Letters** — `internal/synth/latin.go`: 26 capitals — from 0 to 1, the full height of a digit; 26 lowercase — from 0.45 to 1, with the stem of b, d, f, h, k and l up to the top, t up to 0.2, the tail of g, j, p, q and y down to 1.35 and the dots of i and j at 0.28. The lowercase a, c, e, o, p and y take the strokes of the Russian «а», «с», «е», «о», «р» and «у», and the capital O and the circle of Q those of the Russian «О»: they are written the same. x stays the font's letter, as in equations, and stands 0.1 higher than the other lowercase letters: changing it would change the sheets of E2.
- **Signs** — the semicolon, the exclamation and question marks, the straight and typographic apostrophe, straight and curly quotation marks and two dashes. The full stop, comma, colon, brackets, hyphen and slash are already in the font.
- **The writer** — `write` takes a character from one table of the font, the Russian letters and the Latin ones, `glyphOf`, and draws the rest as a wavy line. No line that the sheet plans write — the text of parts, the numbers of columns and the labels of diagrams — and no value in a box of the workbook holds Latin letters other than x. So the Latin letters leave the sheets of E2, of free writing and of the near-key and the pages of the workbook as they are: their hashes in the tests hold them, and a test holds the plans without Latin letters.
- **Lines of text** — `synth.Text`: lines from top to bottom on a sheet of a given size, in a handwriting from the seed, fitted to the longest line. The lines come with pauses, as on a sheet of a plan, with one piece of code, `nextLine`. If there are many lines, the handwriting shrinks so that the line spacing is no less than 1.6 digit heights: the tails of one line do not run into the capitals of the next. The essays are written with it.
- **The check** — a test: every letter and every sign of the test's text has strokes, all the points of the Latin letters are within the font's frame, the capitals are the full height of a digit, the lowercase letters have no extra stem or tail. The hashes of the strokes and of the picture of three lines of English text at seed 1 are in the test, and with the flag `-picture` the test writes the picture.

— *Why:* letters as polylines in the font's frame, like the Russian ones, keep the code free of anyone else's work: someone else's stroke font would ship as Go data, and whatever ships must be under a license of the ASF's category A, checked against the primary source. Our own 52 letters and ten signs are not many, and their shape is the same as that of the Russian ones, so the handwriting of the synthetic sheets is one in both languages.

— *Rejected:*
- **someone else's stroke font**, for example from Hershey, — its license has to be checked, and it does the measurement no good: the synthetic sheets need legible letters, not beautiful ones;
- **a separate writer of English text** — the Latin letters change nothing in the earlier sheets, and one writer holds one handwriting;
- **capitals from Russian letters where they look alike** — of the Russian capitals the synthetic sheets have only «О» and «П».

— *Consequence:* `internal/synth/latin.go`; `internal/synth/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Essays

**R44. The essays of the measurement of E4 are 20 synthetic essays in English and one rubric of six criteria: the file `eval/essays.json` of the format `tutor-essays` v0, and `tutor-eval draw -essays` draws their strokes as samples of the eval protocol. The truth of each criterion is by the plan: the program computes five criteria itself and matches them against the plan, and "on topic" is held by the plan and the author's proofreading. There are no disputable cases in the truth. A page is 1000 × 1400, a handwriting sheet of `spec`'s format.** The document for the author is [essays.md](essays.md).
- **The criteria of the rubric** — six criteria in English, as the text of a rubric criterion in a module whose explanation language is English, as the first slice's module of short essays in English for any age is:
  - `title` — a title on its own first line;
  - `sentences` — no fewer than five sentences;
  - `capitals-and-stops` — every sentence begins with a capital and ends with a full stop, a question mark or an exclamation mark;
  - `linking-words` — the thoughts are linked by words such as because, but, so, then or first;
  - `on-topic` — the essay keeps to the topic of the task;
  - `spelling` — common words are spelled without mistakes.

  The rubric is one for all the essays, and each has its own topic.
- **The shares of met and unmet** — each criterion is not met in 5 essays of 20: in one of development, essays 1–5, and in four of the test, 6–20. In all, 30 criteria of 120 are not met, 90 are met. All six are met by 4 essays, one in development and three in the test, and the others have from one to three not met.
- **The truth without disputable cases** — the file's check holds the rules, and what the program does not see, the plan and the author's proofreading hold:
  - an unmet criterion "sentences" has 3–4 of them, a met one 5–9;
  - a lowercase letter at the start of a sentence is one of a b d e f g h i m n q r t y, which are not written like their own capital, and a missing end mark is only at the last sentence. A full stop inside the text before a lowercase letter the program does not see;
  - linking words — two different ones or more from a closed list, or none, and not even and, or and other words that link thoughts. The program counts words, not meaning, so "so bright" is a matter for the proofreading;
  - spelling mistakes — 2–3, in common words and not in linking words. The spelling has no British variants, harbour and grey;
  - off topic — the whole essay, and the title too;
  - a capital inside a sentence is only "I", in the text and in the topic, so there are no names. A name in the title, which is written with capitals, only the proofreading finds. The topics are made up and general, not about the learner's life.
- **The page** — 1000 × 1400 units, a handwriting sheet of `spec`'s format. The title is the first line, the text is wrapped by words, 28–40 characters to a line, from the seed. Between words there are two spaces, `synth.Essay`.
- **The crop** — by the protocol's rule at 32 px, `essay.PictureOf`: D′ is 23–32 px, 936–1564 tokens.
- **The format** — `tutor-essays` v0, read strictly, like the sheet plan: the rubric is the IDs and the texts of the criteria, and an essay has a number, a part, a topic, a title or `null`, the text in one line, the unmet criteria in the rubric's order and the misspelled words — as written and as they should be. The file's check lists all the errors by their paths and matches the unmet criteria against what the program computes, `Essay.Met`.
- **The samples** — `tutor-eval draw -essays` writes each essay as a sample of the eval protocol, `essay-NN.json`, with the truth of the text and the marks of all the criteria; with `-png` it writes the crop of each page at 32 px — what the model will see — only in `synthetic/` or outside the work tree.
- **The command** `tutor-eval essays` checks the file, reads the directory of samples and prints a summary: the unmet criteria by part, the words, sentences, lines and crops. The run of the essays reads the same directory. `tutor-eval check` takes the essays file too.
- **The document for the author** — [essays.md](essays.md): the rubric, the shares and the essays one by one, with the reason for each unmet criterion. A test builds its parts from the file and matches them byte for byte.

— *Why:*
- There are more met criteria — so the main measure of assessment by a rubric requires: the share of criteria that the model called unmet when they are met. It needs exactly the met ones. Four unmet per criterion in the test also give the second measure — agreement by criterion.
- Without disputable cases — a divergence from the truth must be the model's error, not a dispute about a rule. A lowercase letter that differs from the capital only in height, while in the synthetic handwriting the height jumps about too, would make the truth disputable. So would a missing full stop inside the text: it is unclear how many sentences there are.
- A sheet of 1000 × 1400 is the module's sheet: "its size is the kind's, not the task's", says the format text of `spec`. The crop is cut by the ink, so the picture does not depend on the sheet's size.
- Two spaces between words — at half a space, as in the lines with calculations, the words ran together, and a reading error would come from the drawing.
- Samples of the protocol — the run and the summary read the essays as they read every other sample of the measurement, checked by `sample-check`, and the truth and the crops come from the sample.

— *Rejected:*
- **a rubric of its own for each task**, as in the examples of `spec`'s format, — agreement by criterion would then be counted on one or two essays;
- **disputable cases in the truth** — one linking word, a lowercase s at the start of a sentence, one spelling mistake, a British spelling: they would measure a dispute about a rule, not the model;
- **a sheet of 1000 × 1000** — a module's sheet is 1000 × 1400;
- **essays about the learner's life** — for those under 18 the platform gives made-up and general topics.

— *Consequence:* `internal/sample/essays.go`; `internal/synth/essay.go`; `internal/essay/`; `internal/evalsample/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `eval/essays.json`; `docs/essays.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`.

**R45. Assessment by the rubric in the measurement is one call per essay: the model writes the essay down as it is written, assesses each criterion of the rubric and writes feedback. The measure is the platform's for assessment by a rubric: the main one is a false alarm on a met criterion, the second agreement with the plan by criterion and Cohen's kappa. The program checks the feedback so that the tutor does not write the learner's work: no more than three sentences and, in the measurement, 60 words, and a stretch in quotation marks of four words or more is only the learner's words; the platform's limit for feedback in English is 80 words. Opus assesses in every repeat.** The measured result is measurements 3.8.
- **The call** — the crop of the page at 32 px, and in the text the topic and the criteria of the rubric by ID as a block of the task's data, not as instructions to the model, as for the hint, and the style of delivery. The answer is the essay's text, for each criterion what it rests on, `evidence`, and then whether it is met, `met`, and the feedback.
- **The prompt** — frozen at version 1, fingerprint `e395e9fc…`, `TestFrozenV1`.
- **The assessment of a criterion** — `met` from the answer. A criterion that is not in the answer or is named twice is "without an assessment", and a failed call leaves all without an assessment. A rubric gives no verdict, so the report compares the assessment with the plan.
- **The feedback rule** — `essay.Feedback`. Sentences and words are counted as for the hint's form (`hint.Count`). A stretch in quotation marks of four words or more must stand in the essay as written, and the letters are only Latin.
- **The word limits** — the measurement measures with 60 words, `essay.MaxWords`, and the prompt of version 1 names this limit itself, so the measurement's numbers stay as measured. The platform's limit is 80 words in English, `essay.PlatformWords` from `words.FeedbackWords`, and `api` checks the feedback with this code of `tutor`, the same as in its eval.
- **The measure** — without a threshold: the target and the eval set come from the measurement on the essays of adult volunteers (П-16). The report counts the false alarms and the missed "not met" with an upper bound by essay, the agreement and Cohen's kappa by criterion, the stability between repeats, the reading errors by word and the learner's errors read as written, and the breaches of the feedback rule. Opus's feedback goes in a third file of the report, for the author.
- **The ceilings of the paid runs** — $3 for development and $8 for the test, within the $100 of the runs of the essays and the workbook. The estimate on the stub was about $0.27 per round of development and $2.37 for the test; the runs spent $0.31 for development and $1.43 for the test.
- **The result on the test** — 15 essays with three repeats each: Opus 5.5 · `high` has not one false alarm in 197 assessments of met criteria and κ 1.00; Haiku 5.5 · `low` 1 of 198, Haiku without thinking 2 of 198, both on the capital at the start of a sentence. The words were read without errors, and Opus went over 60 words in 3 pieces of feedback of 45, all about an essay with three unmet criteria (measurements 3.8).

— *Why:* the measurement has to measure the assessment by the platform's two measures and check that the feedback does not write the essay for the learner.
- One call — so the capability "assessment by the rubric" gets work: the essay is read and assessed together, like the analysis with the hint in `spec`'s flow of a check. The report shows the reading errors beside the assessments, so a false alarm from a wrong reading is visible.
- The feedback rule is by quotation marks, because the literal "not one stretch of four words or more that is not in the essay" would forbid any phrase of feedback. The point is not to write the text for the learner, and quoting their words is allowed. A model sentence without quotation marks the program does not catch: the prompt forbids it, and the author reads Opus's feedback.
- `evidence` before `met` — with `met` first, in a development round both Haikus' assessment contradicted its own grounds: the model gave it before it reasoned. The error repeated in two models and came from the prompt's design, and with `evidence` first all three configurations assessed all the criteria correctly.
- 80 words on the platform — Opus went over 60 in 3 pieces of feedback of 45, all about an essay with three unmet criteria.
- Opus in every repeat — the judgement on a criterion changes from call to call more than the reading of a digit, and the repeats of Opus cost about $2 of $100.

— *Rejected:*
- **two calls** — reading, then the assessment by the text read: how the call is built in the product is decided on its own, and the measurement gives it the reading errors beside the assessments;
- **the literal rule** about stretches — see above;
- **Opus once**, as in the reduced matrix of reading, where Opus goes through each condition once, — the stability of Opus's assessment would stay unmeasured;
- **a check of the feedback by a model** — "whether it writes for the learner" would then be decided by a model, not by the program.

— *Consequence:* `internal/essay/`; `internal/report/`; `internal/hint/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The conclusions of E4

**R46. `tutor`'s position on Т-23: reading and analysis are two calls until the threshold for one call — the key makes nothing worse by the paired test over sheets, and nothing is read under the key — is passed on the live handwriting of adult volunteers. The key and the solution do not go to reading.** The measured result is measurements 3.5 and 3.9.
- The measurement of E4 applied the threshold for one call to the observation on 68 synthetic sheets. Opus 5.5 · `high`, Haiku 5.5 · `low` and Haiku without thinking have not one false "correct", not one false alarm and not one reading under the key. By the threshold one call is admissible for all three.
- But on no sheet did the reading with the key differ from the reading without it, so there was nothing to fail the paired test with: the threshold did not show the power of the test. The key moved Haiku toward "unreadable", and Opus toward nothing.

— *Why:* the synthetic sheets did not show the power of the threshold, so two calls stay. Two calls also mean less data at the provider: the key and the solution go to the model only for the analysis.

— *Rejected:*
- **one call for all three** — the threshold was passed on even synthetic sheets, without a single divergence, and it would need a change of the platform's privacy rule that the key and the solution go to the provider only for the analysis;
- **one call only for Opus** — Opus read 66 sheets of 68 correctly without the key anyway.

— *Consequence:* `docs/architecture.md`.

**R47. `tutor`'s position on Т-33: the rule of two readers of one provider is a pair of Claude models. The observation is released when Opus 5.5 · `high` and Haiku 5.5 · `low` read the same thing, and otherwise it is "unreadable".** The measured result is measurements 3.5.
- On the test part without the key, the pair of Opus with Haiku · `low` released 61 sheets of 68, and the pair with Haiku without thinking 51. There are no errors among those released.
- But there are no errors for each reader alone either, so on these sheets the rule only takes away coverage: down to 90 % and down to 75 %.

— *Why:* the second reader is insurance against an error that the synthetic sheets did not show, and its price is about 10 % of sheets in "unreadable".
- The pair is Opus with Haiku · `low`: of the pairs measured it released the most sheets.

— *Rejected:*
- **one reader** until a pair of different providers — on the synthetic sheets the rule did not catch a single error;
- **waiting for a pair of different providers** — there is no OpenAI key, and a pair of Claude models is what can be taken now;
- **the pair with Haiku without thinking** — coverage of 75 %.

— *Consequence:* `docs/architecture.md`.

**R48. `tutor`'s position on the default digit height of the module format: 24 px instead of 32 px. The module manifest can still set the height, `canvas.digit_height`. The format holds 24 px from its version 0.2, and a module without the field is read at 24 px.** The measured results are measurements 3.5, 3.7 and 3.21.
- On sheets, 16 and 24 px are no worse than 32 px for both Haikus, and 48 px is worse for Haiku · `low`. In the workbook, for both Haikus 16, 24 and 48 px are also no worse than 32 px.
- The gain in price is small: at 16 px the picture of a sheet is about 90 tokens against about 200 at 32 px, and the whole input about 1,300 against 1,400. For the workbook 16, 24 and 32 px cost the same.
- On the author's handwriting Haiku 5.5 · `medium`, the site's single reader, reads 24 px no worse than 32 px — on the 60 sheets of the admission, one extra "unreadable" against none, and no false alarm or false "correct" (measurements 3.21). So the modules for adults set no `canvas.digit_height`.
- **Still to check** — 24 px on the handwriting of adult volunteers (П-16), and a stacked fraction at 24 px, which was not measured (Д18).

— *Why:* 24 px is the middle between the advice of the rule written down before the run, 16 px, and the earlier height: live handwriting is smaller and less even than the synthetic sheets, and 24 px leave a margin.
- 24 px comes only with a new version of the format: a new default value changes the behaviour of a module without the field, and a minor version of the format only adds.

— *Rejected:*
- **16 px** — the advice by the rule written down before the run, but checked only on synthetic sheets;
- **keeping 32 px** — 24 px is no worse on both parts of the measurement, nor on the author's handwriting.

— *Consequence:* `docs/architecture.md`.

## Move to `spec` `v0.3.2`

**R49. The move of `spec` from `v0.2.43` to `v0.3.2`: the tag answers twenty of our questions to `spec` — reading and analysis are two calls, handwriting is read by two readers of one provider, the work is read only on the learner's action, assessment by the rubric gets its measures, and `api` checks the hint's form and the feedback rule with the code of `tutor` —, and brings the Go module `spec` and the module package, which the eval runner takes to read the format.** `v0.3.2` was the latest release of `spec` on 2026-10-28, commit 9d64b22; the release is immutable, and `gh release verify v0.3.2 -R altnovatrail/spec` confirmed it. What it brings `tutor`:
- **the answers to our questions «в реестр spec»**, whose rows leave `run/QUESTIONS.md` for `docs/plan/questions.md`:
  - **reading and analysis** — two calls: reading without the key, analysis with the key and the task's solution, as `tutor` proposed. One call is admitted for a model only if it passes the threshold of one call against two — the key makes nothing worse — on the handwriting of adult volunteers, by a new decision of `spec` (П-16). Т-23 is closed;
  - **two readers** — handwriting is read by two readers of one provider, without the key, and a slot that they read differently is "unreadable". The pair is the one `tutor` measured, Opus 5.5 · `high` and Haiku 5.5 · `low`. Т-33 is closed;
  - **the digit height** — 24 px, as `tutor` proposed, but the default changes only with format version 0.2; until then a module without the field is read at 32 px;
  - **reading unfinished work** — the work is read only on "Check" and "Step done", and on a pause the button only blinks. О-8 is closed. A request for a hint does not send work not submitted for reading, and what it gives after a wrong answer is still О-21;
  - **the tutor's words** — `api` checks the hint's form and the feedback rule with the code of `tutor`. The word limit of a hint is 30 in Russian and 35 in English, of feedback 80 words in English. None of the tutor's words give away the learner's gender, and a clause on gender in the eval sets of words checks this. The leak check stays strict;
  - **the workbook** — the item's text does not go into reading a slot; the reader returns a stacked fraction as `3/4`; a false alarm on a mirrored digit counts toward the target of 1 %, and the move to `v0.3.3` makes such a digit "unreadable";
  - **assessment by the rubric** — the main measure is the false alarms on met criteria, the second agreement with people and κ by criterion, and the target and the eval set are set by the essays of adult volunteers (П-16). О-22 is closed. One item of the card is left to `tutor`: whether it is one call with the reading of the essay or two;
  - **the course of writing** — nothing of the learner's writing history goes to AI, and one final picture of the work goes into reading and analysis alike;
  - **the eval sets of the first slice** are on samples of adult volunteers;
  - **the other rows:**
    - there is no early verdict by lines in format v0;
    - the terms of a provider beyond OpenAI and Anthropic are checked in full when the platform needs it, Cerebras first;
    - a reading model on the device is code of `tutor`, and the first slice does not have it;
    - for such a model the code, the weights and the training data are of the ASF's category A, and a volunteer's sample goes into training only if their consent names this (П-16);
    - the card says "the author solves the board's tasks";
    - the parts of the writing with a kind are `spec`'s planned work, and the crop rule stays;
    - the Prompt API in Chrome is closed, with Google's generative AI.
- **what `tutor` builds by them:**
  - in the product's code — two calls and two readers;
  - for `api` — the code of the check of the form and of the feedback rule;
  - in the eval sets of words — the clause on gender.

  The measurements of the capabilities stay as they were measured: 30 words of a hint and 60 words of feedback are the limits of the measurement, not of the platform;
- **the Go module `spec`.** Go code takes the files of `spec` with the module `github.com/altnovatrail/spec` at the submodule's tag and moves both in one move, and Dependabot `gomod` does not raise the module: `go.mod` and `.github/dependabot.yml` hold this;
- **the module package.** The eval runner takes the tasks from the module package, and the samples from the module's folder, since the eval set is not part of the package;
- **the container.** Go 1.27.3 stands in `spec`'s stage `toolchain` too, with the same sums. The Dockerfile of `tutor` does not change, and the container needs no rebuild;
- **the rest** does not change `tutor`'s documents: templates with solvers, the module index and the withdrawal of versions, the release files of `spec` and their signatures are `module-kit`, `modules`, `api`, `canvas`, `app` and `spec` itself. RULES.md and the documents check tool are the same, and the documents check of `tutor` passed on the new tag without edits.

— *Why:* the tag answers the rows that the measurements of the capabilities raised, and without it the live documents would call Т-23, Т-33, О-8 and О-22 open. A tag is moved by a task of its own, to the latest release, and this move ends the epic of those measurements, whose rows it closes.

— *Rejected:*
- **staying at `v0.2.43`** — twenty rows would hang without answers, and the live documents would call Т-23, Т-33, О-8 and О-22 open;
- **`v0.3.1`** — it has no answers to `tutor`'s rows: `v0.3.2` brought them;
- **the archive of the measurements of the capabilities first, and the move outside the epic** — the move closes the epic's rows, so it is the epic's last task;
- **raising the limits of the measurement's code to 35 words of a hint and 80 words of feedback** — the hint and assessment prompts are frozen at version 1 and name the measurement's limits themselves, and the measurement's numbers were measured with them;
- **excluding the module `spec` from Dependabot before the code takes it** — there would be nothing to exclude;
- **rewriting the log, the done tasks and the results of the measurements to the numbers of `spec`** — that is history.

— *Consequence:* `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `RUN.md`; `run/QUESTIONS.md`; `docs/plan/questions.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `go.mod`; `.github/dependabot.yml`; `internal/leak/`; `internal/value/`.

## The extended measurement on synthetic sheets

**R50. The extended measurement on synthetic sheets, with a budget of $150 for runs: the new configurations of Claude on the test parts of the measurements of the capabilities, the chosen ones on sets of hundreds of sheets, pages and essays that code draws, essays with two calls as well, and ten of the author's sheets, written illegibly on purpose, for numbers on live handwriting. The prompts are the frozen versions 1. Its numbers are data for choosing the reader pair and for whether assessment by the rubric is one call or two, not decisions. The synthetic sheets admit no reading of a sheet; reading a workbook, reading an essay and the assessment by the rubric are admitted at the rung "author" on the synthetic sets `eval/workbook-gen.json` and `eval/essays-gen.json`, and they stand by the author's word, without a run, until 2027-01-17.**
- **New configurations:** Opus 5.5 · `medium`, Sonnet 5.5 · `low` with thinking and without, and Haiku 5.5 · `medium` — beside the three configurations of the measurements of the capabilities, on the same test parts. The models in the reader pair are chosen by admission by eval, and those measurements measured only Opus 5.5 · `high` and two Haiku 5.5. The runner asks the configurations named by a flag.
- **Essays with two calls:** reading, then the assessment by the text — beside the one call per essay of the measurements of the capabilities, which measured only one. The item of the card left open is whether assessment is one call with the reading of the essay or two.
- **Scale and hard synthetic sheets.** Generators with the truth by construction compose:
  - 300 sheets of lines;
  - 160 near-key sheets, with a digit shift of 0.1–0.4;
  - 300 pages of the workbook, with more mirrored and crossed-out digits than the measurements of the capabilities had;
  - 300 essays from banks of sentences.

  On 300 sheets without errors the 95 % upper bound is about 1 %, the target of the first slice; on the 68 sheets of the measurements of the capabilities it is about 4 %.
- **The author's sheets:** 10 sheets in free form. The plan sets what to write, and the author how, illegibly on purpose.
- **Ceilings:**
  - the new configurations — $30;
  - the essays with two calls — $3 for development and $7 for the test;
  - the big run on the generators' sets — $100;
  - the author's sheets — $10.

  The generators cost no money, and together this is the author's $150. The day's prices are checked before each run, and the criterion is written down before the first of them.
- **Measures** — the same as in the measurements of the capabilities, without new thresholds. The reports of hints and essays also count the form by the platform's limits — a hint 30 words in Russian and 35 in English, feedback 80 words in English —, and the prompts do not change.
- **Admission.**
  - **Reading a sheet.** The synthetic sheets give it no admission. The first app reads only the author's work, so the rung "author" of the admission rule stands on the author's sheets in `samples/`, the live handwriting of an adult — the earlier 53 sheets and 54 new ones; thresholds and targets are repeated on the handwriting of adult volunteers before the platform's AI opens to anyone but the author (П-16).
  - **Reading a workbook, reading an essay and the assessment by the rubric** are admitted at the rung "author" on the synthetic sets `eval/workbook-gen.json` and `eval/essays-gen.json`, as the words are admitted on synthetic cases, and each entry names that its rung stands on synthetic strokes.
  - **Until 2027-01-17**, and only while the author alone learns, these three stand by the author's word, without a run: the platform's rule that a model is admitted only after an eval is suspended for them for two months. The synthetic sets stay the sets of the runs on Haiku that Д17 asks by that day; the reading of a sheet keeps its set and its report.

— *Why:*
- **the occasion** — a credit on the author's Anthropic account that expires in a few days, while the detailing has almost no paid runs;
- **what it measures** — new configurations, whether the essay is one call or two by a measurement, hard synthetic sheets and more data, and ten of the author's sheets solved in free form, to be less legible and harder;
- **the admission of a sheet's reading on the author's sheets** — the first app reads only the author's work;
- **the three kinds on synthetic sets** — a module with a workbook or an essay enters a deployment's catalog only with their entries, and only the author learns on the deployment;
- **the author's word, for two months** — no learners but the author are expected for a long time, and the paid runs waited for a balance.

— *Rejected:*
- **the detailing first** — it has almost no paid runs, and the credit would expire;
- **free writing at scale** — its sheets with drawings are composed by hand, and a generator of drawings is not a job of a few days;
- **essays written by a model** — for such a text the program cannot check the truth by the criteria "on topic" and "spelling";
- **new versions of prompts and rounds of development** for the kinds of the measurements of the capabilities — the model is measured, not the prompt; only the two essay calls have a new prompt;
- **photos of other people's solutions from the internet** — these are learners' works without consent to eval and to passing them to an AI provider (П-10, П-16), with an unknown license and without truth. And a photo has no strokes by which the crop is cut, so it is not an input of the platform;
- **open sets of handwritten formulas** — MathWriting and CROHME — now: the license is CC BY-NC-SA, the ASF's category X, so only a local measurement outside the work tree could take them, and the sets have only expressions, without solutions. Not a job of a few days;
- **a target or admission of a sheet's reading by the synthetic sheets** — thresholds and targets are repeated on the handwriting of adult volunteers (П-16);
