- **waiting for samples of volunteers** for the first app's admission — the first app would wait for the page of samples of `canvas` and for volunteers.

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `README.md`; `eval/workbook-gen.json`; `eval/essays-gen.json`; `admission/models.go`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`.

## Move to `spec` `v0.3.3`

**R51. The move of `spec` from `v0.3.2` to `v0.3.3`: the tag answers three of our questions from the conclusions of the extended measurement — the pair of readers stays, and Haiku 5.5 · `medium` is the first candidate for a check on the handwriting of volunteers; the near-key is an argument for two calls; a mirrored digit in a box of the workbook is "unreadable", and the learner rewrites it. For the last, `tutor` gives the workbook reading prompt version 2 and its report a row of mirrored digits, and measures the new rule, with a ceiling of $30.** `v0.3.3` was the latest release of `spec` on 2026-10-29, commit a58a323; the release is immutable, and `gh release verify v0.3.3 -R altnovatrail/spec` confirmed it. What it brings `tutor`:
- **the answers to our questions «в реестр spec»**, whose rows leave `run/QUESTIONS.md` for `docs/plan/questions.md`:
  - **the pair of readers** — the numbers of the extended measurement are recorded, and the pair stays: Opus 5.5 · `high` and Haiku 5.5 · `low`. Which models are in the pair in the product is decided by admission by eval, and Haiku 5.5 · `medium` is the first candidate for a check on the handwriting of adult volunteers (П-16). `spec` also recorded `tutor`'s argument: on the near-key at a shift of 0.4, Haiku · `low` and Sonnet without the key each gave one false "correct", and the pair with Opus would not have released them;
  - **two calls** — on the near-key the key for the first time shifted the reading toward a false "correct", and this is an argument for two calls. At a shift of 0.4 a false "correct" happens without the key too;
  - **the mirrored digit** — in `spec`'s decisions, its format text ("The value read"), its success criterion of the first slice and its glossary. The reader does not give a mirrored digit as a digit: the box is ambiguous, the verdict is "unreadable", and the learner rewrites the digit. So it stays until the measurement on children's handwriting. The rule replaces the one by which a mirrored digit is the digit itself. The target of the first slice — no more than 1 % false alarms — does not change, and a false alarm on a mirrored digit counts toward it. A row of the workbook's eval set measures how many mirrored digits go to "unreadable". The rule works only if the reader sees the mirroring, and no one had measured this: the models read a mirrored 5 as 2, and 2 as 5 (measurements 3.7). Crossed-out boxes — about 16 % false alarms in the big run (measurements 3.14) — `spec` recorded only as data for the target.
- **what `tutor` takes with the tag** — the reading prompt gives a mirrored digit as an ambiguous notation, and the workbook's eval set measures this with a row of its own:
  - the workbook reading prompt — version 2: the reader marks a box with a mirrored digit, and its verdict is "unreadable", ambiguous. Version 1, by which the measurements of the capabilities and the big run measured, stays in their logs;
  - the workbook report — a row of mirrored digits: requests to rewrite, readings as a digit and false alarms;
  - the measurement — a round of development on 20 pages of the generator, freezing version 2, and a test on 280 pages, for Opus 5.5 · `high` and Haiku 5.5 · `medium`, with a ceiling of $30 for everything, which is the confirmation a paid run needs. The round of development cost $0.57, the runner's estimate for the test for Opus 5.5 · `high` was $8.39, and the runner keeps a margin for the worst call;
  - in the product's code the reading prompt holds the same rule.
- **the rest** does not change: RULES.md, the documents check tool, the template and `.devcontainer` at `v0.3.3` are the same as at `v0.3.2`. The container needs no rebuild, and the documents check of `tutor` passed on the new tag.

— *Why:* the three rows come from the conclusions of the extended measurement, and without the tag `docs/architecture.md` would call a mirrored digit the digit itself. A tag is moved by a task of its own, to the latest release, and the move and the rule of the mirrored digit end the epic of the measurements, whose rows they close. Of three ways to bring the rule to code, the prompt of version 2 with a measurement is the one that measures whether the reader sees the mirroring.

— *Rejected:*
- **staying at `v0.3.2`** — three rows would hang without an answer, and `docs/architecture.md` would call a mirrored digit the digit itself, contrary to `spec`;
- **the rule of the mirrored digit only as an input of the product's code** — the measurement's prompt would stay version 1, and the row of the eval set that `spec` waits for would measure nothing;
- **the prompt of version 2 without a measurement** — the rule works only if the reader sees the mirroring, and this is not measured. The measurement costs about $7 of what is left of the author's credit;
- **rewriting the prompt of version 1** — the earlier measurements were measured by it, and their logs hold its fingerprint. A new rule is a new version;
- **a ceiling of $10** — with the runner's margin for the worst call, the test might have stopped before reading all the pages.

— *Consequence:* `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `RUN.md`; `run/QUESTIONS.md`; `docs/plan/questions.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `internal/workbook/`; `internal/report/`.

## The return of detailing

**R52. The detailing returns as the current epic after the measurements of the capabilities. Its open tasks — whether assessment by the rubric is one call or two, the measures for minors, the spending limit of an own key, voice, admission to AI and the detailing's outcome — stand in table 1 of RUN.md with the status `[ ]`, and their bodies in RUN.md, with what the measurements and `spec` have decided since the detailing was put off. The author runs the tasks one at a time, and each of them, but for the outcome, has its own planning. Assessment by the rubric goes first.**
- **the order** — assessment by the rubric, the measures for minors, the spending limit of an own key and voice, then admission to AI and the outcome. Assessment by the rubric opens the epic: its data are ready, and it needs no external sources. The spending limit and voice do not depend on each other;
- **the first task** is called «Оценка по рубрике: один вызов или два». This is how the card names its item once `spec` has decided the measures of assessment by the rubric (О-22 is closed);
- **the measurements of the capabilities** go to their archive, `docs/plan/E4.md`, and RUN.md keeps their row among the done epics;
- **the done tasks of the detailing** stay in its archive, `docs/plan/E1.md`;
- **the tag of `spec`** — `v0.3.3`, the latest on 2026-10-29. The move before this plan took it, so the plan needs no move.

— *Why:* a put-off epic returns after the epic beside it, its table to table 1 and the bodies of its open tasks to RUN.md, and the author starts them one at a time. The tasks of the detailing hold the measures for minors, the own key and admission to AI: these are the author's decisions, and partly questions to a lawyer (П-3–П-5), so the author leads each with the executor.

— *Rejected:*
- **the tasks of the detailing in a row, decided by the executor's recommendations**, as the extended measurement ran — their decisions are the author's, one at a time;
- **waiting until `spec` takes up its next epics** — the detailing would wait indefinitely, and those epics need `tutor`'s answers on Т-20 and П-15 ready;
- **dropping voice** — the item of the card has to be decided anyway, and the task may end with the boundary between `tutor` and the device and a deferred item;
- **writing the detailing out anew** — the tasks are written out, and their items are the same. What is new are the inputs, and they have been added.

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `run/EPICS.md`; `docs/plan/E1.md`; `docs/plan/E4.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `README.md`.

## Assessment by the rubric

**R53. Assessment by the rubric is two calls. The first is reading the handwriting of the essay: one reader, without the rubric, gives the text as it is written. The second is the assessment of the text read by the criteria of the rubric, without the picture: for each criterion, the grounds and whether it is met, and one piece of feedback on the whole essay. The measurement on the essays of adult volunteers (П-16) checks this path, and beside it measures one call too.**
- **The data** — the synthetic sheets and the rubric of the measurement of the capabilities, with six criteria:
  - on 300 essays of the big run (measurements 3.12), for four configurations together, the false alarms on met criteria are 26 for two calls and 28 for one, and the missed "not met" 13 and 11. By configuration, one call / two: Opus 5.5 · `high` 10 / 5, Haiku 5.5 · `low` 6 / 7, Haiku 5.5 · `medium` 2 / 2, Sonnet without thinking 10 / 12. On 15 essays of the measurement of two calls (measurements 3.11), for five configurations — 3 for two calls and 5 for one;
  - separate reading keeps the learner's errors more exactly. Words with an error read as written, of 178: Haiku · `low` 167 against 155, Haiku · `medium` 164 against 161, Sonnet 177 against 175, Opus 178 on both paths. Errors in words, of 17,683 — 12, 15, 4 and 1 against 23, 17, 5 and 3;
  - the price of two calls is $13.13 against $12.61 for 300 essays for four configurations, 4 % higher (measurements 3.12), and the time is 0–2 s more (measurements 3.11).
- **One reader.** Two readers of one provider read an answer slot whose check gives a verdict, and a slot they read differently is "unreadable". An essay has one answer slot — the whole sheet, and by this rule the learner would rewrite the whole essay because of one word. So a sheet under a rubric is read by one reader, and `spec` holds the same: two readers are for answer slots whose check gives a verdict. In the first call of the big run Opus read the text the same as Haiku · `low` in 287 essays of 300, as Haiku · `medium` in 284, as Sonnet without thinking in 295 (measurements 3.12). The texts were compared after squeezing spaces and line breaks, and without that the matches are 279, 278 and 280. Not one essay diverged only in letter case or signs.
- **The return.** If the measurement on the volunteers' essays shows that one call gives fewer false alarms, a new decision returns to the question.

— *Why:*
- on the synthetic sheets two calls are no worse than one by the main measure of assessment by a rubric — the false alarms on met criteria —, and the price and the time are almost the same;
- reading handwriting is an AI capability of its own, with its own prompt and eval set, as is assessment by a rubric. The path follows the platform's cycle: perception, observation, rubric, feedback. Reading and analysis of a task are built the same way, as two calls;
- the assessor does not see the picture: only the text read goes to it;
- the second reader insures the verdict: an answer slot read differently goes to "unreadable", not to a false verdict. A rubric has no verdict, and a false alarm from a wrong reading is counted by the main measure on the whole path, reading and assessment together.

— *Rejected:*
- **one call** — simpler and 4 % cheaper, and the measurement of the essays went that way. But it merges reading and assessment into one capability, and on the synthetic sheets it has no fewer false alarms;
- **waiting for the essays of volunteers** — the item on the rubric would stay open, and the product's code would have nothing to rest on;
- **two readers, as for an answer slot with a verdict** — a divergence in even one word would send the whole essay to be rewritten: on the synthetic sheets this is 5–16 essays of 300 — 5 for the pair with Sonnet, 13–16 for the pairs with Haiku —, and on live handwriting there will be more;
- **two readers with the divergences marked for the assessment** — a new design that no one has measured.

— *Consequence:* `essay.go`; `rubric.go`; `internal/essay/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Measures for minors

**R54. The measures for minors that OpenAI and Anthropic require are laid out by component: the disclosure that it is AI and the educational materials — `app`, parental consent and the age category — `api`, the filters — `tutor`, zero data retention at OpenAI for under 13 or under the age of digital consent — a deployment fact. Monitoring is only at the provider: the platform builds none of its own, and `tutor` and `api` flag nothing and store nothing. The content filter is without a separate moderation call: safety rules for children in all of `tutor`'s prompts and an item about safety in the eval sets.**
- **The providers' terms** (sources 1.11). The lists of measures of both providers are "may include": filters, monitoring with reports and age verification. OpenAI's list also has telling children about AI and about how to use it, and Anthropic's has educational materials for children and a child system prompt, which Anthropic "may provide". Said more strongly:
  - at OpenAI — "must take reasonable steps" so that what children get through the API is safe and suits their age; compliance with COPPA; parental consent (Services Agreement, para. 3.3(c)); without zero data retention — "should not" for under 13 or under the age of digital consent;
  - at Anthropic — the disclosure that it is AI ("must"), and compliance with COPPA.

  Anthropic's Usage Policy has told products for minors to follow the article on them ("must comply") since the edition of 15.09.2025. The Commercial Terms include the Usage Policy in the agreement, and the article for developers calls its measures those that "Anthropic requires". The article itself says "should implement" and "may include", so how mandatory its technical measures are, a lawyer decides.

  Audit: Anthropic "will periodically audit", OpenAI "reserves the right to audit".
- **The provider's monitoring.** At Anthropic the Trust & Safety automation works under zero data retention too, and what it flagged it may keep for up to 2 years. At OpenAI zero data retention excludes content from the abuse logs — except pictures and files suspected of CSAM —, and then the moderation and the reports that the law requires are on the customer. OpenAI's option of a check without storage writes records to the customer's own storage for 30 days, and the platform's server does not store works.
- **What is not met.** The organization's measure "monitoring and reporting" is not met by the platform's code: `tutor` and `api` monitor no content. Beside the provider's monitoring the platform adds an adult's "report a problem" at the tutor's answer and an escalation path with the operator. `spec` keeps the measures for minors as a list per provider with the status of each, and the operator declares `minor_safeguards` only once every measure of the provider's list is in place and the author has decided they are enough.

— *Why:*
- the provider's monitoring requires nothing of the code or of storage: the platform's rule of keeping data to a minimum stays as it is;
- the tutor's words are short, and their form and length are held by program checks before the device. These checks do not look at the content: the prompt holds it;
- the rules for children stand in all prompts, for all learners — so the provider does not learn that the learner is a minor, and a model that breaks them does not pass the item about safety in the eval set and is not admitted;
- Anthropic has no separate moderation API, by our reading of its documentation (sources 1.11), so a separate filter would be one more call of a model on each piece of the tutor's words.

— *Rejected:*
- **a flag without content** — the model, in the same call, flags alarming work with a code from a short list, `api` writes an event without content and tells the account owner, and the work stays on the child's device. It would meet the measure of monitoring without new storage, but it needs code in `tutor`, `api` and `app`;
- **storing flagged works** for review by a human — it needs a change of the platform's rule of minimal data and of the list of what it stores: the platform would start storing children's works. Here too belongs OpenAI's check without storage on its side, but with storage at the customer;
- **the provider's moderation** of the tutor's words. At Anthropic it is an extra call of a model. At OpenAI the separate moderation endpoint is free, and the scores can also be got in the generation request itself, though the page does not name the price of this (sources 1.11); it can be returned to when the platform needs OpenAI.

— *Consequence:* `docs/architecture.md`; the prompts in `internal/`; `eval/`.

## The spending limit of an own key

**R55. The spending limit of an own key is at the provider: the key's owner sets a monthly spending limit on an Anthropic workspace or a hard limit of an OpenAI project and takes a key issued in it — or the limit of the whole organization. The platform holds no limit of its own and accumulates no usage of an own key. A provider's refusal for the limit, the balance or the payment `tutor` gives as the outcome "limit reached" with a cause, separately from an AI failure, and does not retry it; the outcome says when access returns where the provider's terms say it, `Outcome.AccessReturns`, today the cap of Anthropic's tier alone. About the usage of each call `tutor` reports the same for platform AI and an own key: the model, the tokens and an estimate in dollars by its price table.**
- **The providers' terms** (sources 1.12):
  - at Anthropic the spending limit is monthly, per organization and per workspace, but not on the default workspace. A refusal for it is 400 `invalid_request_error`, and for the tier's cap 429 `rate_limit_error` with `enforced_spend_limit_reached`. Reading the usage and the limits the documentation describes only in the Admin API, and a workspace key does not fit it;
  - at OpenAI the hard limit is monthly, per organization and per project, and a spending alert does not stop requests. The refusals are 429 with a code in `error.code`, and "Retrying billing, spend, or quota errors won't restore API access". The limit does not trigger at once. The usage is given by the Administration API, and its examples have an Admin API key;
  - a limit per key or per day the documentation of neither names. Both SDKs by default retry a 429 answer twice themselves, so the adapter does not let them retry refusals for the limit.
- **What the learner sees:** at reading — "unchecked" and a neutral phrase, as wherever there is no reading to give a verdict on; at the analysis and the hint — the fallback text of the rung, as always when the model's question did not come; at assessment by the rubric — a neutral phrase, and the criteria stay without an assessment. If one of the two readers of handwriting was refused, the reading gives the refusal whole: the pair holds the verdict.
- **The usage message** — a record for each call of a model: the provider and the model, the tokens of input, cache and output, and of them those of thinking, whether the count is exact — otherwise the worst case, as in eval, — and an estimate in dollars by `tutor`'s price table with the date the prices were read. It has neither content nor identifiers. By it `api` counts the platform AI quotas, and on an own key it does not accumulate it.

— *Why:*
- the provider's limit is counted by whoever issues the bill, though at OpenAI it does not trigger at once. It is shared by all the devices and programs on the key and holds a leaked key too, and a leak is among the risks of an own key;
- the platform stores nothing new: neither a limit, nor a counter, nor a trace of the key, and on the server the key lives only in the memory of the request anyway. The limit is set by the key's owner, as `spec`'s glossary describes the own key;
- an own key in the first slice is only for adults (the recommendation of О-4), and the providers' documentation names no daily limit, but an adult decides for themselves how much to spend a month;
- `tutor` already keeps the providers' prices in the adapters, for the eval estimate. With an estimate in dollars `api` counts the quotas and the unit economics (О-14) without knowing the models' prices, and the models and their providers stay `tutor`'s business;
- with the time access returns `api` can tell the key's owner when to come back: the cap of Anthropic's tier is no limit the owner can lift, and its terms name the time.

— *Rejected:*
- **the provider and also a limit in `api`**, behind the profile of the key's owner, in dollars per day or month. The devices would be counted together, but `api` would store new data that the platform's privacy rules do not name, and the limit would rest on a price table that must be kept fresh;
- **the provider and also a limit on the device**, beside the key. The server stores nothing new, but the usage of several devices of one owner does not add up;
- **only the model and the tokens** in the usage message — then `api` would have to know the models' prices;
- **a limit in calls or tokens** — a reading call and an analysis call cost differently, and so do the tokens of different models.

— *Consequence:* `tutor.go`; `price.go`; `provider/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Voice

**R56. Voice: the boundary is decided, and the route waits for stage 3. The tutor's words reach the learner only as text that passed the checks of `api`: voicing comes after the checks, and speech to speech is closed for them. For now voicing is the device's speech synthesis, and `tutor` takes no part in it: the same text is voiced by `app`, and the model writes no separate text for speech. Recognition of the learner's speech and pronunciation assessment are the capability "voice" of `tutor`: the sound lives only in the memory of the request, what is recognized is an observation for the verdict of `module-kit`, and pronunciation is a rubric with feedback. The route of voice, the providers, the price and the eval sets are at stage 3 (Т-13).**
- **What the boundary rests on:**
  - voice is stage 3, and the first slice has no voice input;
  - "listen" on the text canvas is voiced by `app` on the event `speak` — by one speech synthesis for the whole app and with one set of rules for voices, and by the recommendation of Т-13 these are only local voices;
  - the tutor's words go to the device whole, once the whole text has passed the leak check, and not as a stream; a stream is only for reading, whose answer the program reads. The form of the hint and the feedback rule `api` checks in the same place;
  - `app` does not call AI providers, and `api` calls them only through `tutor`;
  - voice goes to AI for recognition and pronunciation assessment and is stored only in the memory of the request. Voice input, until the lawyer's answer, is only for adults (О-6; П-5);
  - pronunciation assessment is a rubric, and its result is feedback.
- **The boundary:** voicing by the device's speech synthesis — `app`; voicing by an AI provider, speech recognition and pronunciation assessment — `tutor`, the capability "voice", from stage 3; the persona's voice — `app` with the device's speech synthesis, and with voicing by a provider a call of `tutor`. An answer by voice is checked like work: doubt about what was recognized is a request to repeat, not "not yet", and the learner sees what was recognized. Recognition by the means of the browser or the system will not do: its model is not from a release of `tutor` and has not passed admission, and if it sends the sound to the network, the sound goes to a third-party service past `tutor`. This is `tutor`'s inference, and it concerns `app`.
- **What voice requires of admission:** the capability "voice" has no admitted models, and the function closes it to everyone, so until stage 3 it needs no new inputs. Voice input is only for learners 18 and older until the lawyer has answered on П-5, and if it is allowed to children — without storage and at a provider with zero data retention (the recommendation of П-5). Voicing itself does not depend on the age category — it is output, not data collection, but voicing by a provider passes the provider's admission by category, as any call does. The rules are the same for platform AI and an own key (Т-13). While "voice" is one capability, input cannot be told from voicing by the function's inputs: whether to split it, `spec`'s vocabulary settles at stage 3.
- **Waits for stage 3:** the route of input — a recognition API, recognition by a stream or on the device; voicing by a provider, if the device's speech synthesis is not enough; the eval sets of voice and the price; the providers' terms on sound, checked anew; a real-time channel (Т-25); voice input of children (П-5); one capability "voice" or two; the feedback rule for feedback on pronunciation — the rule was made for essays. To return — at the start of stage 3 or earlier, if a module with voice input or a step "pronounce" enters the plan, or if `app` chooses voicing other than by the device's speech synthesis.

— *Why:*
- the route of voice is chosen by eval and price (Т-13), and there are no eval sets of voice, as there is no module in the first slice that needs voice input: Spanish A1 with its step "pronounce" stayed a module for later. The providers' terms have to be checked anew by stage 3 anyway, before the decision on the route;
- the boundary rests only on what is decided, and does not depend on how `app` voices: the device's speech synthesis is not a call of a model, and voicing by a provider would become the capability "voice" of `tutor`. In both cases checked text is voiced;
- speech to speech would give the learner the model's words before `api` checks them for leaks and form and replaces a hint that did not pass the checks with a fallback text. And the model would itself decide what to say, rather than fit words to the rung the program chose. A stream goes only to a reading call, whose answer a program reads; recognition may have one by the same argument, as a program reads its answer too, but this is `tutor`'s inference;
- a text for speech that the model writes would be a second text beside the checked one, and a number written in words the key search does not find — only leak phrases catch it, if the author gave them;
- what voice requires of admission is needed already now, and it follows from the platform's privacy rules on voice and age and from admission by eval, without a decision on the route.

— *Rejected:*
- **the boundary and the route now** — checking the audio APIs of OpenAI and Anthropic and choosing the route before stage 3. Without eval sets of voice there is nothing to choose by, and the check would be out of date by stage 3;
- **deferring the item whole** — admission would be left without its input on voice, and the item on voice has to be decided anyway, if only as the boundary and a deferred route;
- **leaving Т-13 open whole** — speech to speech for the tutor's words would wait for stage 3, though the rules already in force — the words go to the device whole after the leak check, and a program checks their form — contradict it;
- **a separate text for speech from the model** — where a number is written in words, the leak would be held only by the author's leak phrases.

— *Consequence:* `docs/architecture.md`.

## Admission to AI

**R57. Admission to AI is one pure function of `tutor`. Its input is the AI capability, the AI route, the provider, the exact ID of the model and the age category; its rules are `spec`'s admission table, in two parts: "provider × AI route × age category" and "capability × age category". The deployment facts it gets as a value from `api`, which reads them from a file of its deployment configuration, edited by a pull request. The models it takes from `tutor`'s list of the admitted. `api` calls it before an attempt and, if AI is closed, gives tasks that are checked without AI, and `tutor` calls it before each call of a model with the learner's work. A refusal at a call is the outcome "not admitted": the learner sees the same as at an exhausted limit. Everything the function does not know is closed, and for minors everything is closed by default.** How it is built is in `docs/architecture.md`.
- **Before an attempt** `api` names the capabilities that the task's check needs, the route and the category, and for an own key its provider, without the key itself. The model is chosen by `tutor`: the first admitted one from the capability's ordered list, by the same choice at the attempt and at the call.
- **The checks** — in order, until the first refusal: the capability's rule for the category; the cell of the provider, the route and the category — closed, open, or open given facts; the facts that the cell names; the model — among those admitted for the capability and from this provider, since a model is admitted only after an eval. Facts are required only by a cell of platform AI: with an own key the agreement with the provider is the key owner's.
- **The answer** — "admitted" or a refusal with the code of its reason, the table's version and the cell, without content and identifiers: data are kept to a minimum.
- **The capability rule for voice** opens voice input to `adult` alone, until the question of children's voice input is answered (П-5; О-6). While "voice" is one capability, the rule closes voicing by a provider to minors too — our inference, until the capability is split.
- **Two readers** — for answer slots whose check gives a verdict; an essay is read by one. The function checks both readers before the first call, and a refusal to one is a refusal to the whole reading: not one call goes to the provider.
- **The facts** are those of the table's dictionary, each for the age categories declared: the processor agreement, minimal retention, zero data retention and the measures for minors. The operator declares the measures for minors only once the author has decided that the provider's measures are enough. With no file all the facts are "no": the cells open given facts are closed, an adult's own key is open, and `api` at start tells the operator that platform AI is closed. A file that does not parse is a start error. A self-hosting deployment writes its own file.
- **The eval runner and the board** do not call the function: they send synthetic sheets and adults' samples, with the platform's key for eval, not learners' work, and they measure models that eval itself admits. So the function guards only the requests with the learner's work. When children's samples go to AI, their route is checked anew.
- **The cells** are `spec`'s admission table, `spec/admission/`: what follows from the providers' terms and the privacy rules was `tutor`'s input to it, not a norm of `tutor`.

— *Why:*
- the rule on voice input is a rule on age from `spec`'s privacy rules, like the providers' rules, and it is changed by the lawyer's answer on children's voice input (П-5): its place is in the same normative part and its version. A table of providers with the capability as a fourth dimension would grow several times over, and almost all its rows would repeat one another;
- a learner to whom AI is closed should not write work that no one can read, and `api` knows this already before the attempt. The platform already has work without AI: an answer typed or chosen is checked by a program (О-5). A refusal at a call stays a safeguard, and the learner sees the same as at an exhausted limit: the hint does not need AI, it has a fallback text;
- a deployment fact — whether a processor agreement is signed, whether zero data retention is approved — opens to children the route to a provider. Its change must pass review and stay in the history, so it goes by a pull request, as the digests of the packages in `api`'s configuration do. But the digests are taken when the image is built, and the file of facts is not built into the image: it is read at start, and self-hosting takes the same build with its own facts, as `app` reads its deployment configuration at run time;
- the function is pure, and the tests check it with a case for each cell and for each fact.

— *Rejected:*
- **the capability as a fourth dimension of the table of providers** — several times more rows, and they differ by one row about voice;
- **the rule on voice in `tutor`'s code** — a rule on age would leave the normative part of `spec` for the code of one component;
- **only a neutral phrase at a refusal**, with the function called by `tutor` alone — simpler for `api`, but a child to whom AI is closed would write work in vain;
- **the facts in environment variables of `api`**, set outside git — simpler, but a change of a fact would not pass review and would not stay in the history.

— *Consequence:* `admission/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Move to `spec` `v0.5.4`

**R58. The move of `spec` from `v0.3.3` to `v0.5.4`, the latest release on 2026-11-01: the answers to `tutor`'s rows on mirrored digits, two readers and the rubric, the cross-repository plan, and the types of the module format.** `gh release verify v0.5.4 -R altnovatrail/spec` confirmed the release. What the tag brings `tutor`:
- **mirrored digits** — the numbers of `tutor`'s measurement are data of `spec`'s row of the mirrored digit, and the target of 1 % and the rule stay as they were;
- **two readers** — `spec` records the first false "correct" of one reader in the workbook, which the pair would not have released. Two readers are for answer slots whose check gives a verdict, and a sheet under a rubric is read by one;
- **assessment by the rubric** — two calls: reading the essay, and assessing the text read, without the picture. An essay that cannot be read does not reach the assessment. The card of `tutor` in `spec` has no open item about the rubric;
- **the cross-repository plan**, `spec`'s COORDINATION.md. It names the epic of `tutor`'s code: reading handwriting by two readers, analysis and hint, the checks of words — as a library in the process of `api`, its first release a tag of the Go module. The first app: the author solves tasks themselves, locally, in the devcontainer, Claude models read the handwriting, and the key lies in an environment variable, as for the check board; there are no children and no deployments. Without `spec`'s admission table `tutor` calls no AI, for an adult either. The plan names one move for each repository, to the line 0.6;
- **the types of the module format** — for Go, the packages `types/formatv0` and `types/formatv0/open` of the Go module `spec`; for TypeScript, the npm package `@altnovatrail/spec`, which also carries the types of the element of `canvas`. `tutor`'s Go code imports `types/formatv0`. Code that imports the Go module `spec` moves its version together with the submodule, and Dependabot's `gomod` leaves it alone: `tutor` imports it, and `.github/dependabot.yml` ignores it;
- **the rest** — the shared Go checks and the Go coverage of `spec` itself, and the paths of the shared release of `.github`. The shared rules add one thing: COORDINATION.md is in Russian. The documents check, the repository template and `.devcontainer/` are as before. `tutor`'s documents on two readers, the rubric and admission take the answers.

— *Why:* checking the outcome of detailing against `tutor`'s card in `spec` and proposing the next epic need the card's answers on the rubric and two readers and the cross-repository plan that names the epic of `tutor`'s code; `v0.3.3` had neither. So the move goes before that check, as a task of its own, ahead of the plan's one move to the line 0.6.

— *Rejected:*
- **the check against the card at `v0.3.3`**, with what the tags `v0.5` changed retold in words, keeping to the plan's one move to the line 0.6 — the check against the card and the plan would go by the old text;
- **staying at `v0.5.3`**, the latest release when the move began — not the latest tag;
- **splitting the check of the outcome of detailing** into the summary of how `tutor` is built with the request types, and the check against the card with the next epic — each shorter, but three tasks together with the move.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `RUN.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The outcome of detailing

**R59. The part "To decide when detailed" of `docs/architecture.md` holds only what is still to decide about `tutor`: a decided item leaves it, its decision is its entry of this log, and how it is built goes under "How it is built". Detailing decided every item but the route of voice, which waits for stage 3. "How it is built" describes `tutor`, and its request types by AI capabilities are brought together from the decisions already taken — the rubric, the measures for minors, the spending limit of an own key, voice, admission, one call or two, and `spec`'s two calls of reading and analysis, two readers, the checks of words and the rubric's two calls — and from the measurement of the hint, not a new design.**
- **The request types** — reading a sheet, reading the workbook, reading an essay, analysis and hint, assessment by the rubric and the words after a correct answer. Text reading comes with the text canvas, which the first slice does not have, and voice at stage 3. One prompt serves analysis and the hint.
- **"To decide when detailed"** names the items still to decide, each with when to come back to it, under their numbers. What was known about a decided item and what the options were stands with its subsection of "How it is built". An old citation of a decided item's number in the archives of `docs/plan/` leads to the subsection of "How it is built" on the same subject.

— *Why:* "To decide when detailed" is what the tasks still have to decide, and a text holds the current state: a stub of a decided item under its former number is history that every session reads. What was known and what was chosen from stays with how it is built.

— *Rejected:*
- **stubs of the decided items under their former numbers**, with a link to the new place — history in the part a session reads first; an old citation is led to how it is built instead;
- **deleting what was known about the decided items** and their options — what was known and what was chosen from would be lost;
- **renumbering the open items** — the citations of the open item of voice in the archives would point to the wrong place;
- **analysis and hint as two calls**, a prompt per capability — the measurement of the hint measured one call (measurements 3.6), and `spec`'s diagram of a check draws one.

— *Consequence:* `docs/architecture.md`.

## OpenSSF Best Practices

**R60. `tutor`'s project on OpenSSF Best Practices is 16171, at the passing level since 2026-11-01, and the badge stands in README. The answers to the 67 criteria of the passing level lie in `.bestpractices.json` at the root of the repository, in English, after the model of `spec`'s file: 60 met, 7 N/A, none unmet.**
- **The releases** meet `release_notes`, `version_semver` and `version_tags`, and give the justifications of `version_unique`, `release_notes_vulns`, `delivery_mitm` and `delivery_unsigned`. `release_notes` is mandatory: without releases there is no passing level. The text of the criteria was read on 2026-11-01 in coreinfrastructure/best-practices-badge, the branch `production`, `criteria/criteria.yml` and `config/locales/en.yml`.
- **Checked on 2026-11-01**, by the repository and the GitHub settings, only by reading:
  - the tests cover 95.1 % of the module's statements;
  - the rule of the branch `main` requires a pull request and ten checks, without bypass;
  - private vulnerability reporting, secret scanning and push protection are on;
  - SonarCloud has 0 open issues, CodeQL 0 findings.

  Scorecard has 6 open findings, and the file does not say that everything is fixed.
- **The file is kept up to date**, as `spec` keeps its own: a change that changes an answer changes the file too, in the same pull request, and the author saves the changed answers on the site by a link with `overrides`. The first fuzz test changes the justification of `dynamic_analysis`.

— *Why:* the answers are ready at once, and the project does not stand empty. A badge stands only when what it speaks of exists, so it stands with the passing level.

— *Rejected:*
- **releases for the passing level alone** — a tag of the Go module is recorded forever by the checksum database sum.golang.org, so releases come by a decision of their own, with the line and the files of a release;
- **N/A for `release_notes`** — the criterion allows N/A only to a project whose result is "not intended for reuse in multiple locations … AND employ continuous delivery", and `tutor` is a library of `api` and the eval runner of the CI of `modules`;
- **the badge before the passing level** — a badge stands only when what it speaks of exists;
- **the project with no answers** until the first release — project 16171 would stand empty.

— *Consequence:* `.bestpractices.json`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`.

## The return of epic E3

**R61. The epic of the eval protocol and the releases of `tutor` and `canvas` returns as the current epic after detailing. Its tasks stand in table 1 of RUN.md with the status `[ ]`, and their bodies in RUN.md, with what the measurements and `spec` have decided since the epic was put off. The release of `tutor` goes first; the move to a tag of `spec` with the eval protocol, the protocol in the code, the file of open views and the check against `canvas`'s synthetic sheets wait for eval protocol v0 in `spec`, format 0.2 and a release of `canvas` with synthetic sheets. The epic of the library's code waits after it.**
- **The conditions of return** of the deferred epic are not met when it returns. There is no eval protocol v0: `spec` writes it in an epic of its own, which has not started. `canvas` has no releases. `tutor` has no first consumer — neither code of `api` nor eval in the CI of `modules`. Checked with `gh release list` and by `main` of `spec`, read 2026-11-01. The conditions became the dependencies of the tasks, not of the epic.
- **The release first.** It waits only for the author's settings — immutable releases and a ruleset of the tags `v*`, set before the first release. The first release — a tag of the Go module — is awaited by the code of `api` and by the mandatory Best Practices criterion `release_notes`.
- **The move to a tag with the eval protocol, then the protocol in the code** — when eval protocol v0 comes out, after `spec`'s line 0.6. `spec`'s plan gives a repository one move to the line 0.6, and `tutor` takes it before the protocol, since the line comes out first; the move to the protocol's tag is a move of its own.
- **The file of open views** — after the release and format 0.2: the task's view in the file follows the task schema of the format. The samples page of `canvas` needs the file only until the first content of `modules`, and if that comes out earlier, the task is dropped.
- **The check against `canvas`'s synthetic sheets last.** Those sheets are samples by the eval protocol, and the runner reads them by the protocol, after the protocol is in the code.
- **The author runs the tasks one at a time**, by the rules of RUN.md, and each has its own planning.
- **The tag of `spec`** — `v0.5.4`. `v0.5.5` changes only the RUN.md and `.bestpractices.json` of `spec` itself. `v0.5.6` decided what checks the signature of a release, but the rule and the recipe `release-verify` come to the repositories with the one move to the line 0.6, and `spec`'s plan rejects a move beyond it. The bodies of the release and of the check against `canvas`'s sheets say in words what `v0.5.6` means for them.

— *Why:* the library's code is not taken yet, so the epic of the protocol and the releases returns, though none of its conditions of return is met. Its tasks were written out when it was put off, and their items are the same; what is new are the inputs, and they are added, as at the return of detailing. Of the epic's tasks only the release can go now, so it goes first.

— *Rejected:*
- **returning only the release**, and leaving the other tasks deferred: a deferred epic waits whole, in its own file, and the rules of RUN.md know no partial return;
- **writing the epic out anew**, with new numbers: the items of the tasks are the same, and the log, the archives and `spec` refer to the tasks by their numbers;
- **the file of open views at once**, by the task schema of the format at `v0.5.4`: the samples page of `canvas` needs the file no earlier than the eval protocol, and by then format 0.2 changes the schema.

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `run/EPICS.md`; `run/DEBT.md`; `docs/plan/E3.md`.

## The release of `tutor`

**R62. A release of `tutor` comes out by the shared `release.yml` of `.github` after each green merge into `main` that changes what the module is built from. A release is the tag `vX.Y.Z` of the Go module `github.com/altnovatrail/tutor`, which `api` takes as a library, and three files with a signature: the archive of the commit's sources `tutor_<version>_source.tar.gz`, the open views of the measurement's tasks `tutor_<version>_open-tasks.json`, a copy of `eval/open-tasks.json`, and `SHA256SUMS`. The line is `0.1`, `RELEASE_LINE` of `.github/workflows/release.yml`, and the first release is `v0.1.0`. There are no binaries of `tutor-eval` in a release until the CI of `modules` takes them.**
- **Why with files.** The shared release of `.github` releases only with files: its step "Build the files" stops the run if `dist/` is empty — "release-files wrote nothing into dist/, or dist/ is a link" (`.github/workflows/release.yml` of `.github` at `v0.1.11`, commit c1b8487, read 2026-11-01; `v0.1.24` keeps the step). The module's tag is what `api` takes, and the source archive is the code of the same commit under a signature.
- **The recipe `release-files`** (justfile):
  - checks the number, as the recipe of `module-kit` does;
  - refuses a folder with `.git` and a tree that has a folder that `.gitignore` names at the root, other than `dist/`. The shared release calls it in a tree that `git archive` wrote, and only such a tree holds the commit's files and nothing more. A working copy also holds what git does not track, and among it is `samples/`, the author's handwriting, which never enters git or a release. The refusal by `.gitignore` also covers a folder that git will start to ignore later, for example the samples of volunteers (П-16);
  - writes `dist/` anew: the archive with the folder `tutor_<version>/`, without `dist/` and the empty folder of the submodule `spec/`, the copy of `eval/open-tasks.json`, and the `SHA256SUMS` of both. The archive is reproducible: names in order, without an owner, one time for all entries, gzip without a name and a time.

  The recipe `release-files-test` first checks that each file that the code embeds by `//go:embed`, and `eval/open-tasks.json`, stands in `paths` of `release.yml` — otherwise an edit of an embedded file, for example of a prompt, would give no release. Then it checks `release-files` on its own tree in a temporary folder. `ci-test` calls it first — so the check runs in CI, in the job "Go / test".
- **The calling file** `.github/workflows/release.yml` — `.github`'s template, pinned by the SHA of the tag of a release of `.github` that `release-verify` accepts, the version beside it. The pinned release builds the stage `toolchain-core` of the Dockerfile of the released tree, not `toolchain`, with no cache, and the recipes of the release need nothing beyond the core; it signs and verifies the files eight at once, with no new input. To the list of `paths` of the kind go are added the files that the code embeds by `//go:embed` — `internal/gen/topics.json`, `internal/board/page.html` and `internal/board/record.html` — and `eval/open-tasks.json`, and code in `testdata/` is excluded. An edit of the documents and of the data of eval gives no release, but for `eval/open-tasks.json`. An edit of `justfile` and of the Dockerfile gives one, even if the module's code is the same: so the template says, since they hold the recipe of the release and the Go it is built with.
- **The number** is computed by the recipes of `spec` `release-version` and `release-refusal` from the submodule: the next patch of the line. The shared release sets the tag itself, and tags are not set by hand.
- **Settings**, set before the first release and checked by reading the API: immutable releases are on, and the ruleset of the tags `v*` is `v-tags`, 26325840: `update`, `deletion` and `non_fast_forward`, without bypass, as at `module-kit` 25559103.
- **The signature.** The shared release signs each file by `cosign sign-blob`, and the bundle is `<file>.sigstore.json`. A consumer checks the signature by the recipe of `spec` `release-verify` where the file enters NovaTrail, and the recipe accepts only the signature of the shared `release.yml` by the SHA of a release of `.github` no lower than v0.1.7. `api` checks the Go module not by the signature but by the checksum database, by `go.sum`.
- **A module is for good.** The Go proxy builds the module's zip from the tag, and sum.golang.org records its hash for good; a version can then only be retracted by the directive `retract`. So a merge that changes what the module is built from releases a version for good.
- **The API within the line.** The library's API changes within the line `0.1`: before `v1` semantic versioning allows it, and `api` takes the tag at an exact version and moves to it by a task of its own. So the module's root builds no adapters of providers: `tutor.Config` takes them by the field `Adapters`, and the service connects `provider/claude` and `provider/gpt` itself.
- **`.bestpractices.json`.** The releases meet `release_notes`, `version_semver` and `version_tags`, and give the justifications of `version_unique`, `release_notes_vulns`, `delivery_mitm`, `delivery_unsigned` and `crypto_published`. A change of the release that changes an answer changes the file in the same pull request.
- **Where it is described.** The section Releases of `docs/README.md` names the files of a release, their bundles and how a release and a file are checked; README holds only the badges Release and of the release number, and no command. What the release is built from is in `docs/architecture.md`.

— *Why:*
- Nobody needs the binary `tutor-eval` yet: `api` takes the library, and the CI of `modules` takes the eval runner from stage 2.
- A binary also carries the code of the dependencies, and their licenses require a notice in each copy. `golang.org/x/text` has "Redistributions in binary form must reproduce the above copyright notice, this list of conditions and the following disclaimer in the documentation and/or other materials provided with the distribution", and Anthropic's SDK "The above copyright notice and this permission notice shall be included in all copies or substantial portions of the Software" (the LICENSE files of the modules `golang.org/x/text` v0.42.0 and `github.com/anthropics/anthropic-sdk-go` v1.84.0, read 2026-11-01). A release with a binary would also carry a file of these notices. `module-kit` has no dependencies, and LICENSE and LICENSE-go are enough for it.

— *Rejected:*
- **only a tag, without files** — the shared release would not release it;
- **`tutor-eval` for Linux**, amd64 and arm64, and **for six platforms**, as at `module-kit`: there is no consumer until stage 2, and with a binary comes a file of the dependencies' notices;
- **one LICENSE in the release** — a file for the sake of a file, without the release's code;
- **a trial run of the shared release with `publish: false` on a pull request** — its signature would stay for good in the public log Rekor, as with the trial in pull request #33 of `.github`. The recipe is checked by its test and by a run on a tree of `git archive` before the pull request;
- **a merge without the author's word** — the merge releases a version for good.

— *Consequence:* `.github/workflows/release.yml`; `justfile`; `.gitignore`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/README.md`; `README.md`; `.bestpractices.json`.

## Move to `spec` `v0.6.2`

**R63. The move of `spec` from `v0.5.4` to `v0.6.2`, the latest on 2026-11-02: `tutor`'s one move to the line 0.6, which `spec`'s plan gives each repository, made before the eval protocol, which `v0.6.2` does not have; the move to a tag with the protocol is a move of its own. The tag brings format 0.2 — a default digit height of 24 px — and the rule of signatures: a release file of NovaTrail enters code and CI only through the recipe of `spec` `release-verify`.**
- **What the tag decided for `tutor`:**
  - **the signature** — the shared rule of release files. At `tutor` this is the archive of `canvas`'s synthetic sheets in CI. `tutor`'s own release meets the rule: `release.yml` pins a release of `.github` that the recipe accepts, the ID `tutor` is recorded in `tools/release/repositories.mjs` of `spec`, and the recipe `release-verify` at `v0.6.2` accepted both files of `v0.1.0` (checked 2026-11-02);
  - **format 0.2** — there are no other edits besides the default digit height: 24 px instead of 32. The schemas changed only `$id`. The vectors of the check of a value and of the leak check, which `tutor` copies into `internal/value` and `internal/leak`, did not change, and the copies match byte for byte. The file of open views names the format of the submodule's tag. The main height of the measurements, `render.Digit`, stays 32 px: the measurements were taken by it;
  - **the first content of `modules`** is postponed, so the samples page of `canvas` needs the file of open views for longer;
  - **the Go module `spec`** — `spec.Format` with format 0.2, and `types/formatv0` without edits of the API. `tutor` imports the module for the open views of the measurement's tasks, the type `open.Task`, at the version of the submodule's tag, and Dependabot's `gomod` leaves it alone.
- **What did not change.** The repository template, `.devcontainer`, the documents check and `tools/toolchain/toolchain.sh` are the same as at `v0.5.4`. `tutor`'s card in `spec` changed in one place: the signature of `canvas`'s synthetic sheets is checked by `release-verify`.
- **The rule of language of RUN.md** takes the template's text, with the normative texts for authors among the texts in English.

— *Why:* the line 0.6 came out before the eval protocol, and the file of open views and the library's code wait for format 0.2. The move takes the latest tag at the time of the move.

— *Rejected:*
- **waiting for the protocol, so that the move to the line is the move to the protocol** — `tutor` would stand at `v0.5.4` until the protocol, which waited for work of other repositories, and the file of open views and the library's code would wait for format 0.2;
- **the move as the first task of the epic of the library's code** — that epic waits;
- **`v0.6.1`** and **`v0.6.0`** — not the latest tags of the line. `v0.6.2` differs from `v0.6.1` only by illustrations in `spec`'s glossary and in `spec`'s RUN.md, and `v0.6.1` from `v0.6.0` only by `spec`'s RUN.md and COORDINATION.md.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `RUN.md`; `.bestpractices.json`.

## The open views of the measurement's tasks

**R64. The open views of the measurement's tasks come out in a release of `tutor` as the file `tutor_<version>_open-tasks.json` — a copy of `eval/open-tasks.json` in the format `tutor-open-tasks` v0. It holds 56 tasks of the measurements: the bank of the measurement of the course of writing, the free tasks, the pages of the workbook and the essays. Each task is an open view of a task of the module format, the type `open.Task` of the Go module `spec`: the statement, the canvas and the items with answer slots, without the key, the solution, the traps, the leak phrases and the checks. The file is built by the command `tutor-eval open`, and a test checks it against the banks byte for byte.**
- **The file.** At the top are `format`, `version` and `module_format_version` — the format of the submodule's tag, since a v0 reader reads only the current version —, and then the banks in order. A move to a tag that changes the format rewrites the file by `just open-tasks`; where the task schema changes only `$id`, only the line of the format version changes, and the digests of the open views of the 56 tasks stay. A bank has:
  - `bank` — the path of its file in the repository;
  - `digest` — the digest of its bytes, `sha256:` and the SHA-256, as the format writes a digest;
  - `language` — the language of its texts, a BCP 47 tag;
  - `tasks` — the open views of its tasks, in its order.

  There is no module manifest: the file is not a module, and in the types of the element of `canvas` the module manifest may not come at all (`types/canvas` of `spec`). The language that a module manifest would give stands at the bank.
- **The kinds of tasks** — as `spec`'s module format has them:
  - **a sheet** — the bank of the course of writing (`eval/bank.json`, 10 tasks) and the free tasks (`eval/free-bank.json`, 6). The statement is one paragraph. The canvas is `handwriting_sheet`, and there is no grid in the file: the format knows no sheet without a grid, and the default grid stands, squares. One item, one answer slot, `anywhere`: the answer is where AI finds it, as the judge and the measurement's reading find it;
  - **a page of the workbook** (`eval/workbook.json`, 20) — `workbook-01`…`workbook-20`. The workbook's file has no statement, and all the pages have one, new: «Впиши ответы в окошки.» An item is a line `line` from the item's text: `[ ]` is a box, `binding: box`, and the pieces of text are trimmed at the edges, as in the vectors of `spec`;
  - **an essay** (`eval/essays.json`, 20) — `essay-01`…`essay-20`. The statement is the topic, the canvas is `handwriting_sheet` with the grid `lines`, one answer slot, `sheet`: the work as a whole. The rubric is a check, and it is closed, and the title and the text of a synthetic essay are not the task. The language is `en`, and of the other banks `ru`.
- **How a sample names a task:** the tag of the release of `tutor` and the sha256 of the file — the CI of `canvas` pins them —, the bank and its `digest`, the task's ID. By them the runner at the same tag finds the bank and the key. The digest of the open view, by which a sample of the eval protocol names the task, is not in the file: the protocol defines it, and `module-kit`'s `openview.TaskDigest` computes it from the view.
- **The types are `spec`'s.** This is the import of the Go module `github.com/altnovatrail/spec`, at the version of the submodule's tag. The module is excluded from Dependabot `gomod`, and the test `TestSpecVersion` checks the module's version in the build against the tag that CLAUDE.md names, and where the submodule is present also against the submodule's tag. CI has no submodule, and there CLAUDE.md holds the version. The line `go` in `go.mod` is `1.27.0`: the module `spec` requires it.
- **Checks** — the package `internal/openview` and its tests:
  - `Read` takes a file only if it is byte for byte as `Marshal` writes it, and without fields that the types do not have. So a closed field of a task is never read;
  - `Check` checks the rules of the format that the types do not have: the pattern and the uniqueness of IDs, a non-empty statement and items, the answer slots of a sheet and the lines of the workbook, the patterns of the language and of the digest. It also holds the file to what `Build` writes: the measurements' tasks have no pictures, `givens`, `unknown`, grid step, item text, zones or choice, and the file never has them — `Check` does not repeat the rules of the format for them;
  - `TestPatterns` checks what `Check` takes from the format — the format version, the patterns of the ID, the language and the digest, the canvas kinds, the grids and the slot bindings — against the schemas from `spec.Format` of the module `spec`. The test will not let through a move to a tag that changes them;
  - the test of leaks — by the format's leak check. The texts of a view have neither the key, nor the solution, nor a trap, nor a leak phrase, nor the false key of a sheet. They have neither the key, nor a trap value, nor what is written in a box. They have neither the title, nor a phrase of the text, nor a criterion of an essay. Only what the bank itself does not show is counted, and the texts of a view equal the texts of the bank;
  - once, when the file was first built: 56 tasks with stub closed fields passed the task schema of format 0.2 by the check `format-check` of `spec`, and the open view that it builds by the marks `x-open` matched the file.
- **The release.** `release-files` copies the file into `dist/` and writes the `SHA256SUMS` of both files. `eval/open-tasks.json` stands in `paths` of `release.yml`, so its edit gives a release. If a bank changed, the test fails until `just open-tasks` writes the file anew.

— *Why:* while `modules` has no content, the samples page of `canvas` takes the measurement's tasks from `tutor`'s release, and `spec`'s order puts that release first. `spec` asks for the view by the task schema of the format and for reading it by `spec`'s types. All the tasks of the measurements: the samples page will collect the samples of volunteers (П-16) for each kind of reading that the measurements of the capabilities measured, and the build of `canvas` already checks that the workbook's tasks from the file fit on the page.

— *Rejected:*
- **only the bank of the course of writing or only sheets** — the samples page collects samples for each kind of reading that was measured;
- **own copies of the types of the open view** — `spec` asks for the open view to be read by its types;
- **a file that `release-files` builds right in the release** — it could not be read in a pull request, and the release recipe would need a Go build;
- **a module manifest with a made-up module ID** — the file is not a module, and a module ID is a publisher's namespace;
- **a digest of the open view at each task** — the eval protocol defines it, and a definition of our own could diverge from it;
- **a field of tools** — the format does not have it: every task has the pen and the eraser.

— *Consequence:* `internal/openview/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `eval/open-tasks.json`; `justfile`; `.github/workflows/release.yml`; `.github/dependabot.yml`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/README.md`; `.bestpractices.json`.

## Move to `spec` `v0.9.3`

**R65. `spec` is at the tag `v0.9.3`, the latest on 2026-11-06: the first tag with the eval protocol, which `tutor`'s row of `spec`'s cross-repository plan waited for. The tag brings the eval protocol — the sample file, the markup, the eval sets and the runner's report —, the type of the observation, the API contracts, the admission table and format 0.3. The open views of the measurement's tasks move to format 0.3, and the rest are inputs of the protocol in the code, of the check against `canvas`'s sheets and of the epic of the library's code. buf stands in `tutor`'s devcontainer for `spec`'s recipe `sample-check`.**
- **What the tag decided for `tutor`:**
  - **format 0.3** — the schemas changed only `$id`, and what is new in the format is the version comparison of a module and one dialect of Starlark. But while the format is v0, the platform reads only the current version (`spec`'s vector `previous-minor`), and the file of open views names `module_format_version: "0.3"`. This is `ModuleFormatVersion` in `internal/openview` and the file that `just open-tasks` writes anew; the tasks in it are the same. The code and the file are in `paths` of the release, so the merge releases the next patch;
  - **a second tag.** From the line 0.7 a release of `spec` also sets `contracts/<tag>` on the same commit, the version of the nested Go module of the contracts. `git describe` of the submodule names it, and `TestSpecVersion` checks the module against the tag that starts with `v`;
  - **the rows «в реестр spec»** — `spec`'s session itself reads the tables 3 on `main` of the repositories before each task, and the question of a row opens with the mark «В реестр spec»; the author does not pass the rows on. RUN.md's rules follow the template;
  - **`tutor`'s earlier questions** — `spec` recorded them and closes them by its own list. They leave `run/QUESTIONS.md` for [docs/plan/questions.md](plan/questions.md), and its part «Закрыты» names where each answer went;
  - **the eval protocol** — an input of the protocol in the code. The runner reads samples strictly, by the Go module `spec/contracts`, the markup by the field `markup`, writes the report as the message `Report`, and CI checks samples by `spec`'s recipe `sample-check`. `canvas`'s synthetic sheets are the same samples of the protocol, an input of the check against them;
  - **the library's code** — inputs of the epic of the library's code: the admission table `spec.Admission` instead of the admission cells of `docs/architecture.md`, the observation of each reader without the join, the own key check by a separate call, the prices of the call kinds and the output limit, what of the presentation style reaches the words.
- **buf** stands in `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, `BUF_VERSION`: `spec`'s recipe `sample-check`, by which CI and the runner hold the samples of the protocol, builds by buf the image of the contracts of the submodule, and `tutor` may not write a check of its own. `tutor` writes no contracts and does not generate their code: the code comes generated, as the nested module `spec/contracts`. The version is the one that `spec`'s devcontainer installs at the submodule's tag, checked by the sums `sha256.txt` of buf's release; the license is Apache-2.0 (GitHub, `bufbuild/buf`, read 2026-11-07).
- **What did not change.** The documents check of `spec` is the same. `spec`'s devcontainer got buf 1.75.0 for the contracts and the base image `3.0.12`; `tutor`'s devcontainer is its own, and Dependabot raises the base image. The template at `v0.9.3` gives no gopls, so `tutor`'s own step builds it.

— *Why:* the eval protocol came out, and `tutor`'s row of the cross-repository plan waits for this move; the runner on the protocol waits only for it.

— *Rejected:*
- **the file of open views on format 0.2** — its open views would name a version that the tag's reader rejects, and `TestPatterns` checks the file's version against `spec.Format`;
- **`v0.9.2` and the tags before it** — not the latest. The sample file, the markup and the runner's report were already in `v0.7.9`, the admission table in `v0.8.0`, but the tag of the line 0.9 also carries format 0.3, and reading one version of the format with `module-kit` and `canvas` is simpler than moving twice;
- **a check of the samples of `tutor`'s own instead of buf** — the protocol forbids a repository other than `spec` to write one.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `internal/openview/`; `eval/open-tasks.json`; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `RUN.md`; `run/QUESTIONS.md`; `docs/plan/questions.md`; `.bestpractices.json`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Coverage in Codecov

**R66. The coverage of the tests goes to Codecov: `ci-test` writes the Go profile `coverage/go.out`, and the job "Go / coverage" of `.github`'s shared `go.yml`, at the release that `.github/workflows/ci.yml` pins, sends it by OIDC, without a secret, as the artifact `go-coverage`. The profile is by packages, without `-coverpkg`. `codecov/patch` is no less than 90 % of the new and changed lines, and the coverage badge stands in README.**
- **What is set:**
  - `ci-test` — `go test -race -count=1 -covermode=atomic -coverprofile=coverage/go.out ./...`, after `release-files-test`, as the agreement of `go.yml` requires: a recipe that wrote no report fails "Go / test";
  - the job "Go" in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` gives the wrapper `id-token: write`: a called workflow only lowers rights, and the token is asked for by its one job `coverage`. Dependabot raises every reference to `.github` together, in one pull request, the group `shared-workflows`;
  - `.github/codecov.yml` — as at `module-kit`: `patch` 90 %, `project` only reports, a comment only when the coverage changes;
  - the coverage badge — after the Go version badge; `/coverage/` — in `.gitignore`;
  - the answer `test_most` in `.bestpractices.json` names the coverage and Codecov.
- **The coverage on 2026-11-06** — 95.2 % of statements. Every package had no less than 86 %, except the provider port `provider` — 54 %.

— *Why:* the shared `go.yml` has the job `coverage`, and without the right to the token and without `coverage/go.out` it fails a pull request, so its reference cannot be raised without the report. The shared rule that the merge waits for `codecov/patch`, at least 90 % of the changed lines, holds only where the coverage goes to Codecov.

— *Rejected:*
- **`-coverpkg=./...`, as at `module-kit`.** Each test binary then writes all the packages, and the profile grows 25 times: 149,151 lines over 5,967 blocks, 10.7 MB instead of 0.4 MB. The tests take 2:48 instead of 1:15, and the coverage is 96.6 % instead of 95.2 %. At `module-kit` one test of the vectors runs the code of other packages, while at `tutor` each package has its own tests, and a package's line is counted by them;
- **a Codecov secret** instead of OIDC — the shared `go.yml` does not take it.

— *Consequence:* `justfile`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `.github/codecov.yml`; `.github/dependabot.yml`; `.gitignore`; `README.md`; `.bestpractices.json`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The eval protocol in `tutor`'s code

**R67. `spec`'s eval protocol — the package `novatrail.eval.v1` — enters `tutor`'s code as the package `internal/protocol`: reading and writing the sample `Sample` and the report `Report` by the types of the Go module `github.com/altnovatrail/spec/contracts`, the reduction of the writing history, and the protocol's rules of the workbook's boxes and of snapping. The tests of groups and crops run on `spec`'s vectors, `contracts.Vectors`, and the copies of `canvas`'s vectors are gone. The runner, the synthetic sheets, the record mode and the author's sheets stand on it, and the runner's verdicts are given by `module-kit`.**
- **What stands on it:** `draw` writes samples of the protocol, and the runner reads them, held by `sample-check` in a run and in CI; the runner's report `Report`, per reader and pair, is a file beside the log, with measures by its entries; the record mode and the author's sheets are on the protocol, and `tutor-sample` v0 is read only by `internal/convert`, so the in-memory model has no pressure.
- **The verdicts are `module-kit`'s.** The runner takes the Go module `module-kit`: the digest of the open view is `openview.TaskDigest`, and the verdict by the markup's truth and by what was read is `observation.Validate`, `Join` and `Verdicts`. `spec`'s eval protocol admits only a report with `module-kit`'s verdicts.
- **The contracts module** — at the tag `contracts/<tag>` on the commit of the submodule's tag, like the module `spec`. It is excluded from Dependabot `gomod`, and `TestSpecVersion` checks its version against the version of the module `spec`. With it the runtime of protobuf came into the build, `google.golang.org/protobuf`, BSD-3-Clause; the Connect of the contracts module does not enter the build — the packages `eval/v1` and `observation/v1` do not import it.
- **Reading and writing:**
  - the reader first looks at the field `protocol` and names a file of another protocol so — `*sample.FormatError` with the path `protocol` —, and then reads the file by `protojson.Unmarshal`, strictly: an unknown field and a field twice are a refusal;
  - the writer writes `protojson` with the protobuf names and all the fields, and then indents by two spaces and ends with a newline, like `spec`'s examples. `protojson` deliberately puts spaces differently from build to build, and the indentation makes the bytes the same — a sample drawn from a seed is the same file;
  - there is no check of our own of the protocol's rules of the sample file and of the strokes: "A repository other than `spec` … MUST NOT write a check of its own". The recipe `sample-check` gives it.
- **The reduction of the history** — `protocol.Fold`: by IDs only, as at `spec`; a cut takes off the strokes it names and adds its pieces, strokes written. `protocol.Ink` gives the history to `tutor`'s code — `sample.Stroke` and `sample.Erasure`: all the written strokes in order, with their styles, and an erasure at the moment when the sheet last lost a stroke that is not visible at the end. What was erased and returned by undo does not enter the erasures, and an undo of writing does. The IDs of strokes stay strings of `tutor`, a number in decimal digits, `protocol.StrokeID`.
- **Vectors:**
  - `groups.json` — the 11 cases of `canvas`'s copy, and `spec`'s own with erased, undone, cleared and cut strokes, with the group boxes and the groups of an erased stroke. `lines.Groups` passes them all without an edit;
  - `crops.json` — the cases of `canvas`'s copy, with the same numbers, and `spec`'s own of strokes with a style. `bundle_median` is h_med as it is, and the scale takes it no higher than 1000 tenths;
  - `boxes.json` and `snaps.json` — 20 and 14 cases — pass `protocol.BoxOf`, `protocol.Table` and `protocol.Snap`. The box serves the workbook, and snapping the first measurement that marks a place on the sheet.
- **The PNG of a crop** — `Crop.PNG`: the Go encoder writes a grey image, and an opaque RGB one of strokes with a style, as the protocol requires — 8 bits, colour type 0 or 2, without interlacing, only IHDR, IDAT and IEND —, and a test holds this by parsing the chunks.
- **`spec`'s examples** lie in the zip of the contracts module, but are not embedded, and CI has no submodule. The tests take them from the module cache, by the build's version, `protocoltest.Examples`. Every example is read, written and read again into the same value, and the groups, the numbers of the crops and the colour type of their PNG of every sample are those that `lines.Groups`, `render.CropOf` and `protocol.Table` give.

— *Why:* the basis is checked by `spec`'s vectors and examples, without a run and without an edit of the commands, and the rest of the eval code on the protocol stands on it. Only a report with `module-kit`'s verdicts is good for admission.

— *Rejected:*
- **the whole move of the eval code onto the protocol in one step** — more than one check, without a basis that `spec`'s vectors alone check;
- **verdict code of our own** in `internal/value`, with our own digest of RFC 8785 and `VERDICT_CODE_RUNNER` in the report — less work, but the report would be good only for a measurement, not for admission, and the digest could diverge from `module-kit`;
- **stroke IDs `int32` in `internal/sample`** — an edit of twenty packages and of the author's sheets for the sake of the form of an ID;
- **an adapter of the protocol's sample into `sample.Sample` in the basis** — the outcome and the trap are a verdict by the key, and the protocol does not carry the scenario;
- **the examples from the submodule `spec/`** — CI does not have it; **a call of `go list`** — a command from `PATH`;
- **a comparison of bytes** with the examples — `spec`'s check compares values;
- **a PNG encoder of our own** — the Go encoder already writes what the protocol requires;
- **the output of `protojson` as it is** — its bytes change from build to build.

— *Consequence:* `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `.github/dependabot.yml`; `internal/openview/`; `internal/protocol/`; `internal/lines/`; `internal/render/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R68. The tasks of `tutor`'s measurements are tasks of `spec`'s format with checks, the package `internal/task`: each task of the banks of sheets, each page of the workbook and each essay has a `formatv0.Task`, by which `module-kit` gives the open view, its digest and the verdicts. The traps of the banks of sheets are the field `traps` of the task, without values; the open view of the file `eval/open-tasks.json` is built by `module-kit`; `internal/value` has no checks, and `module-kit` is outside Dependabot.**
- **A task of the format** (`internal/task`):
  - a sheet — the statement as a paragraph, the canvas `handwriting_sheet`, one item with an answer slot `anywhere` and the check `number`, the key is the value of the bank's key, `sample.KeyOf`, as the verdict of reading takes it. The solution is a paragraph per line, and the leak phrases are the bank's `leaks`. A bank whose key gives no number `task.Read` rejects. The value of an answer — `ValueOf` and `KeyOf` — lies in `internal/sample`, and `internal/reading` only calls it: so `internal/task` depends only on `sample` and `value`, and the runner's kinds take the tasks of the format without an import cycle;
  - a page of the workbook — `workbook-01` and on: each box of a line has as its check its `BoxCheck` — `number` or `fraction` with the form rule and trap values. The workbook's file has no solution, and the schema requires one of a task with a verdict: the solution is the items' lines with the keys in the boxes. A page with an item that has not as many boxes as there are "[ ]" in the text is not a page of the file, and `task.Page` fails on it;
  - an essay — `essay-01` and on: an answer slot `sheet` with the check `rubric`, the texts of the criteria in the order of the rubric. The format gives no criterion ID, and the protocol's marks name a criterion by its place in the rubric, from 1: the order of `sample.Criteria` is this number;
  - **the closed fields that the measurements do not have** — the schema requires `skills`, `band` and `difficulty` of every task. Nobody chooses a task of `tutor` by them, and the values are constant: the skill is the kind of task (`linear-equation`, `calculation`, `free-solution`, `workbook`, `essay`), the band is `measurement`, the difficulty is 1. They do not enter the open view and do not change the digest;
  - all the files of tasks in `eval/` have tasks of the format, and so do the generated ones, each valid by `task.schema.json` as `module-kit` takes it. The file `eval/open-tasks.json` holds four banks.
- **The traps of sheets are the task's `traps`**, that is the traps that AI may name by the kind of error in the work. The banks of sheets have no trap values, so the check of a sheet has no `trap_values`, and a wrong final answer is `not_yet` without a trap. So does `spec`'s example of the report, `report-reading.json`. The trap mark of a sheet for the measurement of the hint stays in the plan of sheets, `tutor-sheet-plan`. The banks do not change.
- **The open view is `module-kit`'s.** `internal/openview` takes the tasks from `task.Read`, and the open view of each from `openview.Task` of `module-kit`. There is no building of the view by hand, and `TestFile` gives `eval/open-tasks.json` byte for byte: the view of `module-kit` matches the release file.
- **The digest is checked against `spec`.** The digest of the open view is `openview.TaskDigest` of `module-kit`, which itself is checked against `spec`'s second implementation. A test of `internal/task` checks it against five examples of `spec`, which name the tasks `equation-1`, `grasshopper`, `workbook-01` and `essay-01` of `tutor` `v0.1.1`: the tasks give the digest each names.
