The truth of each example is a valid observation of its task, `observation.Validate`; the verdicts by it, `observation.Verdicts`, are those of `spec`'s report, and the essay is an `assessment` with the marks of the criteria of its rubric. `internal/evalsample` checks the digest against the format's vectors of the digest.
- **`module-kit` gives every verdict.** The checks of `internal/value` and their copy of the vectors `testdata/verdicts` are gone, and so are the tests that held them equal to `module-kit`'s verdicts; the reading of a number and the normalization for `leak` and `reading` stay in `internal/value`: they are not a verdict.
- **`module-kit` is outside Dependabot `gomod`**, like the modules `spec` and of the contracts. Its release requires `spec` of its own tag, and a new patch may require a `spec` newer than the submodule: then a PR of Dependabot would raise the module `spec` above the submodule, and `TestSpecVersion` would fail. The version of `module-kit` is raised by hand, together with the move to a tag of `spec`. `ignore` silences security updates too: in the reference of the Dependabot options the section `ignore` is marked "Version updates" and "Security updates" (docs.github.com, "Dependabot options reference", `ignore`, read 2026-11-07). Dependabot still updates the dependencies of `module-kit`, and `ci-vuln` checks everything that `tutor` calls on each PR and on `main`; the answer of `.bestpractices.json` about security updates stays true for the other dependencies. With it came into the build `github.com/santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema/v6` (Apache-2.0), `go.starlark.net` (BSD-3-Clause) and `go.yaml.in/yaml/v4` (Apache-2.0 and MIT). The licenses are from the list of `.github`'s `go.yml`.

— *Why:* `module-kit` takes a task of the format, and only by it does a sample name a task by a digest, and the runner derives the outcome and the trap by the verdict by the truth. `module-kit` gave the same verdicts, reasons and traps as `tutor`'s own checks on each box of both workbooks — what is written, crossed out, the key and the trap values — and on the final answer of each sheet of the plans with a number in it: 432 sheets, of them 217 correct — `correct`, 215 with an error — `not_yet` without a trap.

— *Rejected:*
- **the trap values of sheets from the final answers of sheets with an error** in the plans, added to the banks — a final answer does not always show a trap unambiguously, and the bytes of the banks, their digests and the release file would change;
- **`module-kit` in Dependabot** — its PRs that raise `spec` would be red until the move to the tag;
- **a digest of our own by RFC 8785 in the test** — the digest could diverge from `module-kit`'s;
- **keeping the building of the open view by hand beside `module-kit`** — two buildings of one view could diverge, and `TestFile` checks the file byte for byte anyway;
- **checking the digest of a bank against the examples** — the examples name the banks of `v0.1.1`, and an edit of a bank would break the test for no reason; a task is recognized by the digest of the open view.

— *Consequence:* `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `.github/dependabot.yml`; `internal/task/`; `internal/openview/`; `internal/sample/`; `internal/reading/`; `internal/value/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The synthetic sheets and the runner on the protocol

**R69. The synthetic sheets of `tutor-eval draw` — the sheets of lines, the free sheets and the near-key sheets — are samples of `spec`'s eval protocol, `Sample` of the package `novatrail.eval.v1`, and the summary, the split and the runner read only samples of the protocol. The runner refuses a sample by the protocol's refusals, the rules of the sample file and of its crops are held by `spec`'s recipe `sample-check` — in a run and in CI, on the samples of `testdata` —, and buf for it stands in the devcontainer.**
- **The sample of a sheet** — `internal/evalsample`:
  - `PURPOSE_MEASUREMENT`, `WRITER_ADULT` — the synthetic hand stands for an adult —, `synthetic` with the seed of `draw` and the writing history. The ID is `synthetic-<seed>-<plan>-<NN>`, from the seed, the name of the plan's file and the number of the sheet, and with a near-key shift other than the default also `-shift-<thousandths>`: the protocol takes the ID of a synthetic sheet from the seed, and the plan, the shift and the number tell apart the samples of one seed — the sets of one reading run and two fractions of shift, which give different files. The writer code is 8 hexadecimal digits of the SHA-256 of the string `tutor-synthetic:<seed>`, in capitals: the protocol requires it of a measurement sample, and one seed is one "hand". `pointer` is `pen`;
  - `canvas_version` is `0.0.0` and `text_set_version` is `none`: the protocol has a file that the samples page did not write name them so, and `sample-check` requires them of synthetic sheets;
  - the task is the source `file`: the path of the bank from the root of the repository — the nearest folder with `go.mod`, so that the path is one wherever the bank is read from, and a bank outside the repository has just its file name, without the folders above it —, and the `sha256:` of its file in `file_digest` and `bank_digest`, the task's ID and the digest of the open view of `module-kit` by `task.Sheet`;
  - the groups and the crops are by the strokes, as `protocol.Ink` reads them, with points to a tenth: `sample-check` computes them anew. The test of the examples of `spec` calls the same code, `evalsample.Groups` and `evalsample.CropSet`, and gets their numbers — the box of the points, h_med, the crops and the group boxes; pixels are not compared;
  - the truth is the value of the answer or the reason, and for a sheet with a crossed-out part the mark `crossed_out`. A plan where the answer is the whole solution, as at the author's sheets, `draw` does not draw: an answer without a number will not become a truth, and such a sheet is written by the author's hand;
  - the file name is `NN-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS-mmm-task-scenario.json`: by it the catalog finds the plan's sheet, and with it the scenario, the planned erasure and the trap, which the protocol does not name. The split and `split.json` keep their form.
- **Reading back** — `evalsample.Read`: the strokes and the erasures are `protocol.Ink`, the lines are the parts, the outcome is the verdict of `module-kit` by the truth and the task's key, the first error is from the markup, the trap is from the plan, for the outcome "error", and the planned erasure is the last one whose strokes stood where the plan erased them from. `draw` reads back each file it writes, and does not write what the runner would refuse or what diverges from the plan.
- **The refusals** — by the reasons of the report, `evalv1.Refusal`:
  - **unfit** — `sample-check` names it: `internal/samplecheck` calls the recipe of `spec` from the root of the repository and writes no check of the rules of its own, since the protocol holds its file rules by `spec`'s code alone. Also unfit is a file that is not read strictly, and markup not by the task — a truth that `observation.Validate` of `module-kit` does not accept or by which there is no verdict, and marks of criteria at a task without a rubric;
  - **without markup**; **another task** — not the source `file`, another bank or its digest, no task, a task that is not of the plan's sheet, another digest of the open view; **a child sample** — until an agreement with a provider that admits children, nothing of it goes to AI (П-3).

  A run names a refused sheet before the estimate, with the reason, and asks nothing about it; the rest go. The run log carries the IDs of the samples and of the refused ones, and the runner's report `Report` names the refused. The summary names a refused sheet with the reason and the line numbers that `sample-check` named — without its words: they could quote the text of a part. A check that cannot pass — no submodule, no buf, a path outside the root — stops the command. `draw` does not call the check: it reads the catalog only to know which sheets are already there.
- **Only the protocol.** A file with the key `protocol` is a sample of the protocol; a file of `tutor-sample` v0 is a problem of its sheet, which names `tutor-eval convert`, the one reader of v0. `tutor-eval check` checks no sample of the protocol and names `sample-check`.
- **CI.** The recipe `sample-check` is `just -f spec/justfile sample-check` on the samples of `internal/evalsample/testdata`, one of each kind that `draw` draws — nine samples: five scenarios of the plan of lines, a free sheet with a drawing and an erasure and a near-key sheet, 848 KB, and a workbook page and an essay. The test `TestDrawn` holds them to what `draw` writes with seed 1, byte for byte, and `go test -update` writes them anew. `go.yml` does not take the submodule, so `ci-sample-check` is run by its own job "Samples" of `ci.yml`, like the shared `documents.yml`: a checkout with the submodule and the stage `toolchain`. buf is at the version of `spec`'s devcontainer at the submodule's tag, an `ARG` of `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`.
- **Checked 2026-11-07:** `draw` with seed 1 drew the sets `lines`, `free`, `near`, `lines-gen-1` and `near-gen` — 192 sheets —, and `sample-check` accepted all. Runs of the judge, reading and the hint on the stub by the protocol's samples of the set `lines` gave the same reports, line for line, as by the same set in `tutor-sample` v0.

— *Why:* by the protocol a synthetic sample writes the markup, the runner refuses a sample for four reasons, and the rules of the file are checked by the code of `spec`. Samples in `testdata` give a Go test something to compare what `draw` writes with.

— *Rejected:*
- **CI draws the samples itself** and checks them in `synthetic/` — nothing in git, but the job is more complex, and a Go test has nothing to compare what `draw` writes with;
- **the source `tutor`**, where the bank is in the file of open views, — `go run` does not know the tag, and the sample would name a tag that the working tree does not have; and it does not fit the banks of the generator and of the near-key sheets;
- **a check of our own of the protocol's file rules** in the runner — the protocol forbids it;
- **the words of `sample-check` in the summary** — the check could quote the text of a part, and the agent sees the summary;
- **values of the trap or the outcome in the sample** — by the protocol the runner derives the outcome and the trap by the verdict by the truth;
- **an ID from the seed and the sheet number** — for the sets of one seed and for two fractions of shift the IDs would coincide, and the files would not;
- **`draw` draws the plan of the author's sheets** with the truth "no answer" — the outcome would not match the plan, and such a sheet is not synthetic: the author's hand writes it.

— *Consequence:* `internal/evalsample/`; `internal/samplecheck/`; `internal/sheets/`; `internal/render/`; `internal/protocol/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; `justfile`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `docs/architecture.md`; `.bestpractices.json`.

## Move to `spec` `v0.9.4`

**R70. The move of `spec` to the tag `v0.9.4`, the latest on 2026-11-07, and of the Go modules `spec` and of the contracts to `v0.9.4` and `contracts/v0.9.4`. The tag changes only `spec`'s plan and log: it has no code and no rules for `tutor`.**
- **What the tag brought** (`gh api …/compare/v0.9.3...v0.9.4`, read 2026-11-07): the recipe `sample-check` of the eval protocol will have a mode of a module's eval set: the module ID and the files of its folder `eval/`, and beyond the rules of the file, the refusals that need neither a package nor a key: the purpose `PURPOSE_EVAL_SET`, there is markup, the source is `modules` with this ID, the IDs of the samples do not repeat. `spec` writes the mode, and the CI of `modules` calls it. The check of a task against the package and of the digest of the open view stays with the runner of `tutor`, as its other refusals do. The rest is `spec`'s plan, matched to `main` of `module-kit` and `modules`;
- **what was done:** the submodule — `v0.9.4`, commit e803736, `gh release verify v0.9.4 -R altnovatrail/spec` confirmed the release by its attestation; `go.mod` and `go.sum` — `spec` and `spec/contracts` `v0.9.4`, and `TestSpecVersion` checks them against the submodule and CLAUDE.md; the tag — in CLAUDE.md, README, the start of RUN.md and the links to RULES.md in `.bestpractices.json`. buf of the devcontainer of `spec` at this tag does not change, and `sample-check` accepts the samples of `testdata`.

— *Why:* the tag is the latest, and the submodule and the Go modules of `spec` move together, in one move.

— *Rejected:* **staying at `v0.9.3`** until the workbook and the essays are samples of the protocol — the tag breaks nothing, and a move as a separate edit is simpler to check than inside a task of code.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `RUN.md`; `.bestpractices.json`.

## The workbook and essays on the protocol

**R71. The pages of the workbook and the essays are samples of `spec`'s eval protocol too: `tutor-eval draw -workbook F` and `draw -essays F` draw them as files `page-NN.json` and `essay-NN.json` into their own directory, by default `synthetic/` and the file name, and the summaries `tutor-eval workbook` and `essays` and the runs of the workbook and the essays read the directory `-dir`, checked by `sample-check`, with the protocol's refusals. From the workbook file or the essays file the runner takes what the protocol does not carry, by the task ID of the sample. It cuts the crops for the model by the sample's strokes. Only `draw` draws in memory, and `-seed` leaves the run and the summaries: the seed is the samples'.**
- **The file beside the samples** — a sample finds the page or the essay of the file by the ID of its task, `workbook-NN` or `essay-NN` (`task.Page`, `task.Essay`). The truth and the crops come from the sample, and the box's scenario, the written and the crossed-out values, the writing of the fraction, the errors in the words and the part come from the file, as a sheet takes its own from the plan. The protocol does not carry them: by it the truth of a mirrored box is `UNREADABLE_AMBIGUOUS` with the mark `mirrored`, a rewritten one has only the mark `crossed_out`, and the writing of the fraction, the errors in the words and the part are not in it.
- **The samples** — `internal/evalsample`: as for a sheet, a measurement, an adult, `synthetic` with a seed, the writer code, `0.0.0` and `none`, the writing history and the task source `file`. A page has the surface `workbook`: each box at the place of `synth`, and the filled ones by the box rule, `protocol.Table`, with crops by the box's strokes; `draw` fails if the rule gave other boxes than code drew. An essay has a sheet with groups and crops, the truth is the title, a line break and the text, and the marks are all the criteria of the rubric.
- **Reading back** — `evalsample.ReadPage` and `ReadEssay`, with the protocol's refusals, as for a sheet, and beyond them, for a page, parts and marks, for an essay, parts, a criterion outside the rubric and marks on an unreadable one. A sample without a refusal but not by the file is a problem by path, without values, and a run with it will not start, as with a sheet that does not pass the check. An undrawn page of the part stops the run too; a refused one is named before the estimate and is not asked.
- **The directory** — `page-NN.json` and `essay-NN.json` by the number in the file, other files are named and not read. The default directory is `synthetic/<file name>`: `synthetic/workbook`, `synthetic/essays`, `synthetic/workbook-gen`. It is in `synthetic/`, and `samples/` is a refusal: code's handwriting does not lie beside the author's handwriting. `draw` writes outside the working tree too, but the summary and the run will not read such a directory: `sample-check` takes only paths of the repository. Samples of two seeds in a directory are a refusal: the run and the report name one seed, and they would not tell a mix apart. `draw` with another seed into a directory where samples are refuses, as `draw` with another `-shift` does.
- **The crops for the model** — by the sample's strokes, by the crop rule, as for the sheets. A test holds that they are byte for byte those that were drawn in memory: the points of the history to a tenth shift neither the numbers nor the pixels. The runner takes the PNG of no sample, `canvas`'s either, where the PNG may diverge by a grey level: it cuts their crops by the strokes too, and a workbook page of `canvas` is read marked up anew by the sample's truth, `evalsample.ReadMarkedPage`.
- **Commands.** `draw -workbook F` and `-essays F` with `-seed`, `-dir` and `-png` draw only those not in the directory, read each file back, and with `-png` save the crops of those drawn at 32 px — `page-NN-item-NN-box-N.png` and `essay-NN.png`, in `synthetic/` or outside the working tree; a sample file is written after its crops. `tutor-eval workbook` and `essays` — `-file` and `-dir`, without `-seed` and `-png`: the summary, and with it a line about the samples — read, not drawn, refused with a reason, not by the file. A run — `-dir` instead of `-seed`; the run record names the directory, `dir`, and the seed is the samples'.
- **`testdata`** — page 5, with all seven box scenarios and both writings of the fraction, 142 KB, and essay 16, the shortest, 701 KB; `TestDrawn` compares them with `draw` byte for byte, and `ci-sample-check` holds the rules.
- **The verdicts** of the boxes come from `module-kit`, as a sheet's; a rubric gives an essay no verdict.
- **Checked 2026-11-07:** `draw` with seed 1 drew 20 pages and 20 essays, and with seed 7, 300 pages of `workbook-gen` and 300 essays of `essays-gen`; `sample-check` accepted 40 samples of `eval/`. The crops of `-png` matched those drawn in memory byte for byte, the summaries line for line, plus the line about the samples. Six runs on the stub — the workbook with captions and without, at all heights, essays with one call and with two, `workbook-gen` and `essays-gen` — gave the same logs, without time and `dir`, and the same reports, Markdown and CSV, as the runs drawn in memory.

— *Why:* the synthetic sheets of `canvas` and the eval sets of modules are sample files, and the runner reads the workbook and the essays the same way as sheets, with the protocol's refusals. The runner reads what `sample-check` checks, and the protocol tells the runner to hold the rules with the same code.

— *Rejected:*
- **everything from the sample, without the file** — the protocol does not carry the box's scenario, the written value of a mirrored one, the crossed-out one, the writing of the fraction, the errors in the words and the part, and the reports stand on them;
- **drawing in memory further on**, and writing files only for CI, — the runner would read not what `sample-check` checks;
- **`-dir` is required** — every call and recipe would have to name it, and by default the directory is in `synthetic/` anyway;
- **the sample's PNG for the model** — the sheets' crops are cut by the strokes, and it would diverge from them;
- **an undrawn page goes past the run** — a sheet without a file stops the run, and a page does the same;
- **the summary and the run without `sample-check`** — the protocol tells the runner to hold the rules with the same code.

— *Consequence:* `internal/evalsample/`; `internal/protocol/`; `internal/synth/`; `internal/sample/`; `internal/workbook/`; `internal/essay/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `docs/architecture.md`; `CLAUDE.md`.

## Move to `spec` `v0.9.5`

**R72. The move of `spec` to the tag `v0.9.5`, the latest on 2026-11-07, and of the Go modules of `spec` and of the contracts to `v0.9.5` and `contracts/v0.9.5`. By the tag's eval protocol a file that the samples page did not write writes `canvas_version` `0.0.0` and `text_set_version` `none`, and `sample-check` requires them of synthetic sheets. `tutor` already wrote them, so the code does not change.**
- **What the tag brought** (`git diff v0.9.4 v0.9.5` of a clone of `spec`, read 2026-11-07): the rule of the two versions — in the protocol's text, the comments of the fields of `sample.proto` and the check `tools/contracts/sample.mjs`, and `spec`'s examples now write `0.0.0` and `none`; what `tutor` and `canvas` take from it; the gate of `sim`, where `tutor`'s part of `spec`'s plan does not change;
- **what `tutor` takes:** `draw` writes `0.0.0` and `none`, and all nine samples of `testdata` pass `sample-check` of the tag; the board's record and the converter of old sheets write them too;
- **what was done:** the submodule — `v0.9.5`, commit 86f442a, `gh release verify v0.9.5 -R altnovatrail/spec` confirmed the release by its attestation; `go.mod` and `go.sum` — `spec` and `spec/contracts` `v0.9.5`, and `TestSpecVersion` compares them with the submodule and CLAUDE.md; the tag — in CLAUDE.md, README, the start of RUN.md and the links of `.bestpractices.json` to RULES.md; the comments of `internal/evalsample` name the protocol's rule.

— *Why:* the tag is the latest and settles the versions that `tutor` wrote; the submodule and the Go modules of `spec` move together, in one move.

— *Rejected:* **a separate branch after the workbook and the essays on the protocol** — the move goes in the same branch, as a commit of its own, and is checked separately.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `RUN.md`; `.bestpractices.json`; `internal/evalsample/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The runner's verdicts — `module-kit`

**R73. `module-kit` gives every verdict of the runner by the format's task of `internal/task`: the verdict of reading a sheet and the boxes of the workbook is `observation.Verdicts` on the protocol's observation, which `tutor` builds from the model's answer by the protocol's table of reasons, and the verdict of the pair of readers in the reading report is on `observation.Join` of two observations. The log writes the verdict and the trap of `module-kit`, and the reason in `tutor`'s words of reading, where reading gave it. `internal/value` has no checks and no copy of the vectors; the reading of a number and normalization stay in it.**
- **The protocol's observation** — `reading.ObservationOf` for a sheet, the only answer slot `anywhere` (`task.SheetItem`), and `workbook.Verdicts` for a page, each box in the order of the task. No answer — `UNREADABLE_NO_FINAL_ANSWER`, several — `UNREADABLE_SEVERAL_FINAL_ANSWERS`, an illegible part — `UNREADABLE_ILLEGIBLE`. Other readings, a mirrored digit and a value that is not in the writing of its part — `UNREADABLE_AMBIGUOUS`: the protocol calls so a value "that the reader cannot point to in the writing". Empty after the reading of a number, empty or only spaces — `UNREADABLE_BLANK`, as is an empty box, which is not shown to the model.
- **The words of the log** — the verdict and the trap are `module-kit`'s, and the reason is the word of reading, where reading gave it: `no_result`, `results`, `illegible`, `ambiguous`, `not_written`, `mirrored`, `missing`, `twice`. Where the check gave the reason, it is a word of `module-kit`, `blank` or `notation`. So the logs of earlier and new runs read the same, the reference files of `internal/report/testdata` hold byte for byte, and the workbook report recognizes a mirrored digit by the word `mirrored`. The runner's report `Report` translates the reason words of the log into the words of `module-kit`, reading the observation from the call record, `reading.RecordObservation` and `workbook.RecordObservation`.
- **The pair** — the verdict of the pair in the reading report is `module-kit`'s on the joined observation, as `api` judges it, and a sheet is released when both read one value: the check compared them as equal and gave them one verdict. A sheet where both have one value but the verdict "unreadable" is not released; `0.5` and `1/2` under `number` are one value. The report takes the observation of each reader from the call record — the verdict, the reason and the value, `reading.RecordObservation` — and does not read the answer anew: so the pair stands on the rules of the run that also recorded the errors of each reader beside it. A test holds that the record and the answer give one observation.
- **A workbook answer without an answer slot of the box or with two** — the observation is not valid, and by the protocol this is like an AI failure: all the boxes of the page get `unchecked`, the empty ones too, and the box without an answer slot or with two has the reason `missing` or `twice`. In the same way, on a failure of the call an empty box is `unchecked`, not `unreadable`.
- **The truth of a box in the log** — the verdict of the written value of the file, `verdict.OfSlot` by the box (`sample.BoxCheck.Verdict`): the sample's truth for a mirrored one is `UNREADABLE_AMBIGUOUS`, without a value, and a false alarm on it is counted by the value. The check of the workbook file is on `module-kit` too, and the check of the format's box is `sample.BoxCheck.Format`. The form of a fraction — `formatv0.CheckForm`. A check that `module-kit` does not take — a placeholder of a template in a trap value — is named itself, not the key; the boxes of a page with such a check are "unchecked" without a reason.
- **The expected verdict of a sheet** the report takes from the outcome, which `module-kit` gives by the sample's truth (`evalsample.Read`). An essay has no verdict: a rubric gives none.
- **`internal/value`** — `Normalize`, `Read` without units, `Rational` and `Lowercase`: by them reading finds the value in the writing of a part, the leak check the number of the key, and the reports compare what was read with what was written.
- **The cost** — the reading report adds up the cost of a reader's call over the sheets in their order: in the order of a map a cost at the edge of rounding was printed now one way, now another — for the log `near-tune-20` $0.00026 three times of ten, and $0.00027 seven.
- **Checked 2026-11-07:**
  - four runs on the stub before the edits and after — the reading `keys`, `heights` and `states` of 152 sheets of four sets, 4999 calls, the workbook with captions and without, at all heights, and 300 pages of `workbook-gen` — gave the same logs, without time, and the same reports, Markdown and CSV;
  - the reports of 32 logs of reading and of the workbook of past runs, `synthetic/runs` and `samples/runs`: in 16 reading logs only the lines of the pairs changed, 42 lines. There are 247 fewer released, exactly as many as there were extra "unreadable" among the released, and the false verdicts, the errors of each reader and the p of the hybrid are as many as before;
  - each answer of these logs is judged anew by the new code: in reading, 15,318 answers have the same verdict and reason; in the workbook, 602 answers of prompt version 2 are the same, and 1062 calls with a failure of the log `limited-t95-workbook` got `unchecked` for an empty box too. Their report does not change: it takes the recorded verdicts. The 1768 answers of prompt version 1 the current reading does not read, and they were not compared.

— *Why:* `spec` admits a model only by a report with the verdicts of `module-kit`, and the runner measures what `api` will do: it joins two readings and judges the joined one. `tutor`'s words of reading keep the logs of earlier and new runs readable alike.

— *Rejected:*
- **the words of `module-kit` in the log** — `no_final_answer`, `several_final_answers` and `ambiguous` instead of the words of reading: the reference file of the reading log would change in 66 lines, the report would read both vocabularies, and a mirrored digit would have to be marked by a separate field;
- **releasing a sheet where both readers give one verdict by `verdict.Same`**, an "unreadable" one too, — the report would measure a rule by which no one decides;
- **keeping a box without an answer slot alone `unchecked`** — `module-kit` does not take such an observation, and the runner would diverge from `api`;
- **the truth of a box by the sample** — for a mirrored one it has no value, and a false alarm on it would not be counted.

— *Consequence:* `internal/sample/`; `internal/task/`; `internal/reading/`; `internal/workbook/`; `internal/report/`; `internal/gen/`; `internal/value/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The runner's report `Report`

**R74. The runs of reading sheets, of the workbook and of essays give the runner's report `Report` of the package `novatrail.eval.v1` by `spec`'s rules of the runner's report: `tutor-eval report` writes it from the log, beside the log, — for each reader alone and for each pair, — and the measures of those rules, counted from its records, as a section of Markdown and a CSV file of their own. The run log carries for it the IDs of the samples, the refused ones, the verdict by the truth and the marks, the tag of `tutor` and the version of `module-kit`. The other tables of the reports stay.**
- **Both capabilities** — `CAPABILITY_HANDWRITING_READING` of the sheets and the boxes of the workbook and `CAPABILITY_RUBRIC_ASSESSMENT` of the essays.
- **Which Reports** — a Report for each reader alone, with all its repeats, and for each pair of the report — the one that reads once, Opus, with each that reads in repeats. `module-kit`, `observation.Join`, joins the pair, repeat to repeat, and a pair has the smaller of the two numbers of repeats. From the log what the platform will ask is taken: a sheet without the key, a workbook page without captions, finished work, digits of 32 px. For an essay the reader and the assessor are one model, in one call or in two.
- **The measures** — `report.MeasuresOf` counts them over the records of the Report, all repeats together, by `spec`'s rules of the runner's report — for reading, the false alarm, the false "correct", the extra and the kept "unreadable", another trap, "unchecked" by cause, and all of this also separately for the mirrored and the crossed-out; for essays, the extra "unreadable", the false alarm, agreement, the marks `NONE` and Cohen's kappa by criterion. The Markdown section "The runner's reports of the protocol" is a line per Report, and the CSV is a file of its own, `<log>.protocol.csv`, so that the other CSVs stay as they are. The other tables — the key, the heights, the states, the captions, the pairs of two different readers — stay: the Report does not carry their data.
- **The name of the set** — by the run's sets: `capability_set.name` is the sets joined by "+" and the part, `lines+free+near-dev`, for the workbook and the essays the file name and the part, `workbook-test`, `essays-all`. `spec` leaves the capability's set and its place to `tutor`.
- **The files** — `<log>.<reader>.report.json` and `<log>.<strong>+<fast>.report.json`, through `protocol.MarshalReport`; they are written once, as Markdown is, and all or nothing.
- **One set, one Report** — `tutor-eval canvas` reads the sheets and the pages of a set by a reading run and a workbook run, and joins their reports of one reader or pair into one Report of the set, `report.Joined`.
- **The log** carries what the Report is made up of:
  - the run record — `runner`, the tag of `tutor`, and `module_kit`, the version of `module-kit`, from the build of the run, `runner.Versions`: another build may assemble `report`. A build not from a tag writes `v0.0.0`, as `spec`'s examples do: it is not a release. Also `refused`: the refused samples, their set, the ID when the file names a readable one, the file, the reason and the detail;
  - the record of a sheet and of a page — `sample` and `truth`: the sample's ID and the verdict of `module-kit` by the truth of each answer slot that the model reads — for a page, the filled boxes —, with the marks `mirrored` and `crossed_out`; the record of a box also has `trap_values`, so that the report assembles the page's task for `Join` without the workbook file;
  - the record of an essay — `sample`, `people`, the people's marks, and `unreadable`, the reason of the truth without text;
  - the call record — `invalid`, an answer outside the schema.
- **The reading of a repeat** — the verdict of `module-kit` in the words of `module-kit` by the observation from the call record: `reading.RecordObservation` and `workbook.RecordObservation`, and for a pair their `Join`. "Unchecked" comes with a cause: `UNCHECKED_CAUSE_INVALID` for an answer outside the schema and for a workbook answer without an answer slot of the box or with two; `UNCHECKED_CAUSE_CHECK` for an answer slot whose check gives no verdict on a valid observation — a cause of the task, not of the model; `UNCHECKED_CAUSE_FAILURE` for any other failure, for a call that was not asked, and for a check that `module-kit` does not take; `UNCHECKED_CAUSE_LIMIT` for a repeat the run did not reach when the limit `-max-usd` stopped it, and when it stopped otherwise — interrupted or without a record of the end — a failure. The runner does not tell the provider's limit or quota from another failure: an exhausted spend limit answers `400 invalid_request_error` (sources 1.12), and such a call in the Report is a failure. For an essay a criterion "unchecked" is `CRITERION_ASSESSMENT_NONE`, not a single mark is `UNCHECKED_CAUSE_NO_MARK`, and with two calls a reading outside the schema makes the assessment that was not made `INVALID`.
- **An empty sheet** — a sheet without strokes does not go to the model, and the Report measures it without answer slots, as an empty page; `spec`'s rules measure it so too.
- **The reader** — `Model`: the provider by the code of `spec`'s admission table, `anthropic`, and the model with its setting as `tutor` names it — the ID, the effort and `no-thinking`: `claude-haiku-5-5 low no-thinking`.
- **A log without sample IDs** — of past runs and of the author's `tutor-sample` v0 sheets — gives no Report, and its report for people is the same, byte for byte. The file of a refused sample without a readable ID is named only in Markdown.
- **The check.** A test of `tutor` reads its Report strictly, `protocol.ReadReport`, and holds `spec`'s rules of the report itself; `spec`'s recipe `report-check` holds the Reports of `canvas`'s synthetic sheets.
- **Checked 2026-11-07:**
  - six runs on the stub — the reading of 152 sheets of four sets, the workbook with captions and without, at all heights, 300 pages of `workbook-gen`, essays with one call and with two — gave the same call records, and the records of the run, the sheets, the pages and the essays with the new fields; the other Markdown and CSV line for line, plus the section of the protocol. Reports — 22: five each for reading and for the workbook with captions and without, one for `workbook-gen`, three each for the essays;
  - all 22 were accepted by the check of `spec` itself — `checkReport` and `sampleBreaches` of its `tools/contracts/report.mjs` against the samples of the run, called directly;
  - a copy of the workbook directory with a child sample, without markup and with a file that does not read: the Report named them `REFUSAL_INVALID` — `sample-check` calls the child's invalid — and `REFUSAL_NO_MARKUP`, and the file without an ID is named in Markdown;
  - the reports of 32 logs of reading and of the workbook and of 13 logs of essays of past runs are byte for byte the same, and they have no Report;
  - the examples of `spec`'s report give the measures counted by hand from their records.

— *Why:* the report `Report` is read by the CI of `modules` and the admission rule, and only a Report with the verdicts of `module-kit` is fit for admission. A Report for each reader alone keeps the measure of each model in the protocol, not only in the other tables; a section and a CSV of their own keep the other tables and CSVs as they are; names from the run's sets say what was measured.

— *Rejected:*
- **the essays' Report later, apart** — one runner's report covers both capabilities at once;
- **a Report only for pairs** — the measure of each model would stay only in the other tables;
- **recounting the other tables by the Report** — their numbers would change, and the logs of past runs without sample IDs would stop reading;
- **the run writes the Report itself, at the end** — the report for people and the Report would be made up by different paths, and the log of an interrupted run would give none;
- **constant names of sets** — the name would not say what was measured.

— *Consequence:* `internal/evalsample/`; `internal/sheets/`; `internal/runner/`; `internal/reading/`; `internal/workbook/`; `internal/essay/`; `internal/report/`; `internal/protocol/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `docs/architecture.md`; `.bestpractices.json`.

## The record mode and the author's sheets on the protocol

**R75. The board's record mode writes samples of `spec`'s eval protocol, `Sample` with a writing history — the events `write`, `erase`, `undo` and `clear`, with `pointer` and without pressure, — and the `tutor-sample` v0 sheets in `samples/` and the set `synthetic/free` are moved to the protocol by `tutor-eval convert`, into directories `<set>-v1` beside the old ones. Only the converter reads v0, `internal/convert`: pressure is gone from the model in memory, and with it the commands `synth` and `check` of v0 samples.**
- **The board's record and the converter** come together: the author's new sheets and the converted ones are on the protocol at once.
- **Where the converter puts the files** — a neighbouring directory: `samples/sheets-v1`, `samples/free-v1`, `samples/free-messy-v1` and `synthetic/free-v1`, with the same file names and a copy of `split.json`, so that the splits of past measurements name the same sheets. The converter does not change the old directories. Sheets from `samples/` it writes only into `samples/`, and a person's sheet into no directory of `synthetic/`, wherever it comes from; a sheet that is already in the new directory it leaves: a second launch writes nothing. `sample-check` checks each file before it gets its name, when the directory is in the working tree. The board's record and the recipes — `record`, `free-record`, `messy-record`, `live` and `free` — and the default `-dir` of `sheets`, `split` and `run` are the `-v1` directories.
- **The author's writer code** — the file `samples/writer-code`, beside the samples, outside git, and in it 12 digits and capital Latin letters. The first recording or conversion makes it random, and after that both read it, `evalsample.WriterCodeAt`. A file without a code is an error, not a reason to rewrite it: the saved samples name the code that was in it. For a directory outside `samples/` — a test of the board or of the converter — the code file by default lies beside the directory, so that the test's samples do not name the author's code or make it; for a directory in `synthetic/` there is no code: a person's sheet is not written there. A code file in the working tree outside `samples/` is not taken.
- **A stroke outside a line** — the board does not save the sheet: the check names a visible stroke that did not fall into any line or part — the protocol puts every visible stroke in one part —, and the author moves it into a part or removes it. The converter refuses such a sheet: among the author's 14 sheets there are none such.
- **The sheets of code in `samples/sheets`** — converted with a check: 39 of the 40 sheets of lines were drawn by code, with seed 1. The converter writes them as `draw` writes them, `evalsample.Of`, byte for byte, only if the current `synth` draws the sheet with the same seed the same way; otherwise a refusal "drawn with another seed or code". So the set of 40 sheets and its split read on the protocol.
- **The truth of a free solution** — for a plan sheet of one part, which the author writes whole: the value of the key for a sheet marked correct, and the number after the last word «ответ» in the text of the markup for a sheet with an error — a separate word, not inside «ответа». A correct sheet whose text names another number, a sheet with an error without a number or with the key's number, and a sheet with another outcome without the mark "illegible" are refused. The rule is one for the board and the converter and only for a person's sheets: code does not draw such sheets. Otherwise `sample.ValueOf` gives no number from such markup, and for one sheet of the author's — «в 3 раза дороже» — it gives the wrong one.
- **The pointer of a v0 sheet** — `mouse` when the pressure of every point is 0.5, as for a mouse in Pointer Events, and `pen` otherwise.
- **The ID of a person's sample** — random, 128 bits in 26 lowercase letters and digits, `evalsample.NewID`.
- **Clear, Undo and Start over.** Clear is the event `clear`, as for the element of `canvas`: the sheet goes on, with the same clock, from the second press. Start over, a button of its own, starts the sheet anew, with a new history. Undo is the event `undo`: it removes the last visible stroke and returns nothing. Undo and Clear are slips: erasure by the scenario is only a movement of the eraser, and the server recognizes them by the events of the history, `protocol.ErasureEvents`.
- **`spec`'s check on saving** — the board writes the file under a temporary name and runs `sample-check`; the file gets its name only if the rules hold. A directory outside the working tree — a test of the board — is not checked, and the mode says so at launch: `sample-check` takes paths of the repository.
- **Pressure in `synth`** — `synth` draws the random numbers of pressure and does not write them: otherwise the same seed would draw other sheets. The hashes of the tests of `synth` are of sheets without pressure, with the same points and times, and the 40 sheets of `synthetic/lines` hash as the files drawn with pressure do without it.
- **The other v0 sets in `synthetic/`** — the logs of past runs — the same command moves when they are needed.
- **What the sample knows from the board's markup.** The erasure by the scenario that the author chose on the page, and its text, the protocol does not hold: the reader takes for it the last movement of the eraser on a line or part of the plan, `evalsample.PlannedErasure`, and the text from the plan. Undo and Clear do not count. The board holds the author's choice to this erasure: another choice is a 422 with a hint which one the reader takes, — so that a saved sheet reads as the board checked it.
- **The proposed erasure.** On a sheet of lines the board proposes the same erasure as the runner takes — the last movement of the eraser that stands on a line of the plan, `evalsample.PlannedErasure`. Several movements give a remark, and the sheet is saved with the last one: it is worth rewriting only if that movement did not remove the whole planned error. If the movement removed a stroke from a line of the plan but stands on another line, the board proposes nothing and says why: the runner would not take it. On a sheet of parts the board proposes the movement that removed the most strokes, and of equal ones the earlier: until the author has joined the groups, the number of a group is not yet the number of a part of the plan, and `evalsample.PlannedErasure` compares exactly that.
- **Checked 2026-11-07:**
  - the conversion — 40 sheets of `samples/sheets` (39 of code and the author's sheet), 3 sheets of `samples/free`, 10 of `samples/free-messy` and 40 of `synthetic/free`: no refusals, `sample-check` accepted all 93. The outcomes by the verdict of `module-kit` are those marked: 21 correct and 19 with an error, 3 correct, 6 and 4, 20 and 20. A repeated launch writes nothing. The 40 sheets of `synthetic/free-v1` are byte for byte the sheets of `draw -seed 1`;
  - the summary `sheets` over the four `-v1` directories — the same numbers as over the old ones, the mouse on the same sheets, the split the same; only the line about the mouse differs, which names the pointer;
  - runs on the stub, the judge and the reading `-reads keys,heights,states`, over the four directories: the judge's reports and all the CSVs are byte for byte the same, and the Markdown of reading is the same, but for the set name with `-v1`, plus the section of the protocol. Reports — 20, and the check of `spec` itself, `checkReport` and `sampleBreaches`, accepted each against its set;
  - the record mode with a directory outside the working tree: the proposal, the check and the saving of a sheet from the history of a `testdata` sample; `sample-check` accepted the saved file, and `sheets` read it;
  - the page script in `node:vm` on a DOM stub: strokes with a pointer and a number, `undo`, `clear` from the second press, and Start over is a new history.

— *Why:* the samples of the measurement must read after the runner moved to the protocol, and the old sets keep their files, names and splits, so that past runs and their logs stay as they are. The board proposes the erasure that saving accepts: a proposal that saving rejects stopped the author on a sheet of `eval/author-sheets.json` erased in ten movements of the mouse.

— *Rejected:*
- **the board's record later, apart from the converter** — the author's sheets would come on the protocol in two steps;
- **a subdirectory `protocol/`** and **the same directory with another ending** — a set would live inside a set, or the split would name the wrong files;
- **the code in `~/.config/tutor/`** — it would not travel with the sheets to another machine; **a flag with the code** — a typo would split the author's sheets between two writers;
- **a stroke outside a line as a part of its own or into the nearest one** — the markup would diverge from the plan or silently take the wrong part;
- **only the author's sheets** — the set of 40 sheets and its split would stay without the protocol;
- **removing the calls of pressure in `synth`** — the same seed would draw other sheets, and the sets of past measurements would diverge from their logs;
- **the page computes the surface itself** — groups and crops twice, in JS and in Go, where the protocol has one rule;
- **proposing on a sheet of lines the movement that removed the most strokes** — the board would propose what saving rejects.

— *Consequence:* `internal/convert/`; `internal/evalsample/`; `internal/protocol/`; `internal/sample/`; `internal/synth/`; `internal/sheets/`; `internal/board/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `justfile`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/sheets.md`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `.bestpractices.json`.

## Compatibility with `canvas`'s synthetic sheets

**R76. The CI of `tutor` reads the synthetic samples of `canvas` from a pinned release — the tag and the sha256 sum in `justfile` — and checks, without a key and without spending, that the runner reads them: each sample passes `sample-check`, is read by the task that it names — the bank is taken at the tag of `tutor` that the sample named — and goes into a run on the stub with a report `Report` for the whole set, which `spec`'s recipe `report-check` holds too. The check is red when a sample is refused other than as a child sample or is not read.**
- **The release** — `canvas` v0.1.6, released 2026-11-08: the archive `synthetic-samples_v0.1.6.tar.gz` of 16 samples `synthetic-101` … `synthetic-116` and the table `synthetic-samples_v0.1.6.tsv` — the ID, the purpose, the writer and the scenario in words. The signature of both files was checked by `spec`'s recipe `release-verify` at pinning — "canvas v0.1.6, signed by the shared release.yml of .github v0.1.13", — and from then on the pinned sum holds the file, as for every release file of NovaTrail. A new tag of `canvas` is pinned the same way: two lines of `justfile`, `release-verify` of the new archive and a green `just canvas-check`.
- **The bank at the sample's tag** — the samples of `canvas` name the task by the source "a release of `tutor`": the tag, the digest of the file of open views of this release, the bank and its digest, as the eval protocol names a task. The runner takes the bank with the keys at this tag from its own git: the recipe `canvas-fetch` lays out `eval/` of each tag that the samples name, by `git archive`, into `synthetic/tutor-trees/<tag>` — a missing tag it fetches without `--depth`, so that a full clone does not become shallow —, and the runner compares the digest of the tag's `eval/open-tasks.json` — a copy of the release's file of open views — and the digest of the bank with those that the sample names (`evalsample.Releases`). So an edit of `tutor`'s banks does not break the check of `canvas`, and samples made at an old tag read with its bank. So the eval protocol tells: the runner takes the task of the capability's set "at the source and the digests that the sample names".
- **The capability's set.** The synthetic sheets of `canvas` are a set of the reading capability, `tutor`'s: "samples of any purpose, of tasks of any source", in the eval protocol's words. Eval-set samples are not refused for the source `tutor`; a child's are refused until П-3, and the check counts this as expected.
- **How a sample is read:**
  - **a sheet without a plan sheet** — its plan is taken from the sample itself, `evalsample.ReadSheet`: the task from the reference, the parts and lines are the parts of the markup, the answer is the first part with a final answer, the outcome is the verdict of `module-kit` by the truth, the first error is from the markup, the trap from the same verdict, and there is no scenario: the protocol does not carry it. The scenario in words from `.tsv` the runner does not read;
  - **the final geometry** — eval-set samples and child samples have no history: a sheet and a page are read by the visible strokes, without erasures and without time. `Read` and `ReadPage` for the measurement take only the history;
  - **a workbook page of `canvas`** writes its own values, not the values of the workbook file: the page is marked up anew by the sample's truth, `evalsample.ReadMarkedPage` — the checks and keys of the file, the values of the sample, a crossed-out one is "rewritten", a mirrored digit is the key with a mirrored digit, as `tutor` draws it. Markup that the workbook run cannot pass on — a value without a stroke, an empty box with strokes, a box "illegible" — the check names a problem of the sample, and does not read it silently. The same for a second page with the same number: the run and the report of the workbook know a page by its number, and the check would sooner turn red than mix two;
  - **an empty sheet** — a sheet without strokes has no crop by the eval protocol's crop rule, and so nothing that goes to the model: it does not go to the model, as an empty box of the workbook, and the report measures it without answer slots, as `spec`'s check of the report expects. The rule is shared, and the author's sheets have it too;
  - **the PNG of the crops** of `canvas`'s samples the runner does not take: it cuts the crops by the strokes, `internal/render`, and `sample-check` holds the PNG by the protocol's rules of crops;
  - **one report per set** — the sheets and the pages go in two runs, of reading and of the workbook, each with its own log and reports, and the command joins the reports of one reader or pair into one Report of the set, `report.Joined`: the protocol's report is "what the eval runner measured in one run of an eval set, for one capability";
  - **`spec`'s `report-check`** — `canvas-check` runs it on the reports of the whole set together with its samples after `--samples`, and on the reports of the sheets and of the pages, each about a part of the set, without samples. The run logs are in `synthetic/runs/canvas-check`, anew at each check. `tutor-eval canvas`'s own comparison stays: that the report measures each read sample and that it has no "unchecked" is the rule of this decision, not of the protocol;
  - **red on a spoiled sample** — the recipe `canvas-check-test`: a copy of the set, the first sample with zeros in the digest of the open view — `sample-check` still accepts it —, and the check must refuse it as another task.
- **Checked:** `just ci-canvas-check` from scratch — 16 samples: 10 sheets and 3 pages read, 3 child samples refused, the empty sheet 110 and the empty page 116 do not go to the model; the reports of the set compared; `canvas-check-test` — "ok". The check of `spec`, `checkReport` and `sampleBreaches`, accepts each of the 5 reports of the set against its 16 samples whole, and `report-check` passes all 15 reports.

— *Why:* `spec` tells the CI of `tutor` to take the synthetic sheets of `canvas` by an exact tag and sum and to check that the runner reads them, without calls of AI and without a key. The bank at the sample's tag keeps the check of `canvas` independent of `tutor`'s edits of its banks, and one check that runs each sample to a report says that the runner brings it there.

— *Rejected:*
- **only the current bank** — an edit of any bank on which the synthetic sheets stand would make the job red until `canvas` releases new ones, and `tutor` would wait for `canvas`;
- **a check of reading alone**, the recipe separately from the run on the stub: a check without a run would not say that the runner brought the sample to a report;
- **the real files of `canvas` in testdata** — their digests are named on the banks of v0.1.10, and the tests would fail with the first edit of a bank; the tests write such samples from their own, and the recipe checks the real ones;
- **two reports per set** — the set is one, and the eval protocol measures a run of a set;
- **an empty sheet to the model**, with a crop of the whole sheet: the protocol's crop rules do not know such a crop, and a call about an empty sheet is a payment for nothing.

— *Consequence:* `internal/evalsample/`; `internal/reading/`; `internal/report/`; `internal/workbook/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `justfile`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `docs/architecture.md`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `.bestpractices.json`.

## Move to `spec` `v0.9.9`

**R77. The move of `spec` to the tag `v0.9.9`, the latest on 2026-11-08, with the Go modules of `spec` and of the contracts at `v0.9.9` and `contracts/v0.9.9`. On the tag `spec` accepts `tutor`'s rule that a sheet without strokes is measured without answer slots, and names its own recipe `report-check` for the runner's report. The runner's code does not change.**
- **What the tag brought** (`git log` and `git diff v0.9.5 v0.9.9` of a clone of `spec`, read 2026-11-08):
  - the eval protocol: the `first_error` of a synthetic sample and the marks of an essay are chosen by code by the seed, and the admission rule counts no measures on them; a recipe `report-check` for the report; a sheet without strokes is measured without answer slots, and `sampleBreaches` expects not a single answer slot for a sheet without crops;
  - the protocol's rule of snapping: snapping takes the strokes as they came — in eval, those that the writing history shows —, and the final geometry of a sample does not serve as its input;
  - `spec`'s rules of the production images and who deploys them, in RULES.md too; `REASON_CODE_ROTATION`, an edit of `cause.proto` of the contract `app` ↔ `api`; examples of the format; and what `tutor`, `canvas` and others take.
- **`tutor`'s questions:** the tag answers both of `tutor`'s questions to `spec` then open, on the report's recipe and on the sheet without strokes; they went from `run/QUESTIONS.md` to `docs/plan/questions.md` word for word, as `spec` read them, with their answers.
- **What `tutor` takes:**
  - the check of `spec` `v0.9.9`, `checkReport` and `sampleBreaches`, accepted all 5 reports of the set of `canvas` v0.1.6 that `tutor-eval canvas` writes, whole: 0 remarks;
  - the admission rule counts no measures on the first error and the marks of synthetic samples, and `tutor`'s admission rule in `internal/report` keeps to it; `report-check` in CI comes with the move to the tag that releases it;
  - `protocol.Snap` in eval only on samples with a writing history: the runner does not call it in eval, a test holds only the vectors, and the comment of `Snap` says so.
- **Does not concern `tutor`:** the production images — `tutor` has no image, it is a library in the process of `api`; `REASON_CODE_ROTATION` and `cause.proto` — the contract `app` ↔ `api`, which `tutor` does not import.
- **What was done:** the submodule — `v0.9.9`, commit fa5da0b, `gh release verify v0.9.9 -R altnovatrail/spec` confirmed the release by its attestation; `go.mod` and `go.sum` — `spec` and `spec/contracts` `v0.9.9`, and `TestSpecVersion` compares them with the submodule and CLAUDE.md; the tag — in CLAUDE.md, README, the start of RUN.md and the links of `.bestpractices.json` to RULES.md. The tests, `sample-check` of the `testdata` samples and `canvas-check` on the new tag are green.

— *Why:* the tag is the latest and answers both of `tutor`'s open questions to `spec`; the submodule and the Go modules of `spec` move together, in one move.

— *Rejected:* **waiting for a tag with the recipe `report-check`** — the two questions answered on this tag would stay open, while `spec` tells to close them by the move to it; the recipe comes with a move of its own. **A new epic for the sake of the move** — the move is one task.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `RUN.md`; `.bestpractices.json`; `internal/protocol/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## Epic E5 — the library's code

**R78. Epic E5, the code of the library `tutor`, is the path of the first app, and it is done: its description, task table and task bodies are in the archive [docs/plan/E5.md](plan/E5.md). The path is the admission function, the rule of admitting models and the list of the admitted, the skeleton of a call, the reading of a sheet by two readers, the checks of words, the call of words and its eval with the clauses on gender and safety, the run that admits the first pair of readers and the move to the latest tag of `spec`. The rest of the library's code — usage and prices, the provider's refusals, the own key check, the workbook, the reading of an essay and the assessment by the rubric — and the repeated admission of words went to epic E6.**
- **No move to a tag opens the epic:** the tag was already the latest, and the Go module `spec` already imported.
- **The inputs of `spec`** for the epic stand in its description.
- **The rule of admitting models is a task of the epic**, once for reading and once for words. Its bounds, targets and repeats are `tutor`'s, by the eval protocol, and without admitted models the admission function closes everything, so the first app waits for it too. The synthetic sheets admit no reading of a sheet, so reading for the first app is admitted on the author's sheets. The rung "full" of the assessment by the rubric waits for a measurement on the essays of adult volunteers (П-16), which the epic does not have.
- **The clauses on gender and safety are on the path of the first app:** without them the words model is not admitted.
- **Eval measures the same code:** each task of a call moves its kind on the runner to the library's code, and does not write a second copy of the prompt and the parsing.
- **`spec`'s cross-repository plan** has the path of the first app as a row of its own, which the first app of `api` waits for, and the rest of the library's code as another row, which the first app does not wait for.

— *Why:* the outcome of detailing proposed the epic, and `spec`'s cross-repository plan waits for it. The path of the first app goes first, because `api` waits for it, and the rest is not part of the first app: the quota, the own key and a task with a rubric are not in `api`'s first app. The division is by one check per task: what the calls share is a task of its own, so as not to build it in each; words in three, because a new version of a prompt goes with its own eval, and the set with the clauses on gender and safety separately from the paid run.

— *Rejected:*
- **a move to a tag as the first task** — the tag was already the latest;
- **a task per part of how `tutor` is built** — the tasks would come out larger than one check, and the first app would wait for the essays;
- **the rubric and the essays before usage:** neither is part of the first app, and the usage of `api` is needed by the first deployment, earlier than the essays module;
- **the admission of words without the clauses on gender and safety**, with them later: `spec` requires them before admission.

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `docs/plan/E5.md`; `docs/plan/E6.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `README.md`.

## The admission function

**R79. The admission function is the package `admission` at the root of the module, `github.com/altnovatrail/tutor/admission`. The input is the types `admissionv0` of the Go module `spec`, the codes of the table; the file of deployment facts is YAML in the form of `facts` of the vectors; the table is `spec.Admission`, and the tests run `spec`'s vectors of answers and rules, not a copy.**
- **A package at the root, not the root of `tutor`:** `api` calls it before an attempt, and `tutor` before a call, and the package is pure: without the providers' SDKs, input-output and clocks. In the root it would be mixed with the calls of models.
- **The types `admissionv0`:** `spec`'s vectors carry tables of their own with the providers `alpha` and `beta`, and a closed enumeration of `tutor` would not accept them. An unknown value of the input is a refusal by the table: the function itself holds "closed by default", not the type.
- **YAML:** people edit the file by a pull request, and beside a fact one can write why it is declared. JSON is YAML too, so it reads as well. The function reads the table by the same path, and YAML rejects a key named twice, which JSON would let through silently; an anchor with an alias and the merge key `<<`, which hide it, the function rejects itself.
- **Schemas:** the function checks the table and the facts file by the schemas of `spec.Admission` — `admission-table.schema.json` and `$defs/declared_facts` of the vectors' schema —, as the package `admissionv0` tells: code that reads a value from outside first checks it against the schema. `github.com/santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema/v6` v6.0.3, Apache-2.0, checks them — the same library and version that already went into the build of `tutor` through `module-kit`, and now a direct dependency. `module-kit` does not give its own schema check outward: it is in `internal/`.

— *Why:* `api` and `tutor` both call admission, before an attempt and before a call, so it is pure and apart from the calls of models; `spec`'s own vectors hold the function to `spec`'s table, which a copy would not; and people edit the facts file, with a reason beside each fact.

— *Rejected:*
- **the function in the root of `tutor`** — one entry point, but the root would mix admission with the calls;
- **the facts file in JSON**, like the table, — without comments, though people edit the file;
- **`tutor`'s own enumerations** with a translation into the codes of the table — the vectors with others' providers would have to be translated, and an unknown code the function would close anyway.

— *Consequence:* `admission/`; `go.mod`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The rule of admitting models

**R80. A model is admitted to a capability by the admission rule of `tutor`, by the runner's report `Report` with the verdicts of `module-kit`, or, while `spec` suspends eval before admission, until 2027-01-17, by the author's word for the rung "author" alone. There are two rungs: "full" — the upper bounds of the shares of false alarms and of false "correct" are no more than 1 %, `spec`'s target —, and "author" — not one such event on 30 answer slots or more where a false alarm is possible and on 20 or more where a false "correct" is. The deployment names the least rung: on `novatrail` it is "author" for adults, and "full" is the target of quality measured on the samples of adult volunteers. The shares are counted by answer slots, not by readings. The list of the admitted is `admission.Admitted`, ordered, each entry with its report or the author's word, its rung and its prompt fingerprint, and `Table.Select` chooses the model. The first entry is the pair Opus 5.5 · `high` and Haiku 5.5 · `medium`, at the rung "author" on 60 of the author's sheets.** How it is built is in `docs/architecture.md`.
- **What the rule admits.** Reading a sheet, by the pair. The workbook by box, by this rule on the boxes an adult writes, with mirrored and crossed-out boxes as rows of the report without a target. The essay's reading by a rule of its own, with one reader. The assessment by the rubric at the rung "author" by the upper bound of its false alarms on met criteria of no more than 1 %; on the volunteers' essays its target stays the rung "full". The sets of these three kinds are synthetic. Reading has no measure of the first error and no marks of an essay, so `spec`'s measures of them do not touch the rule.
- **A refusal without a rung** — verdicts that are not `module-kit`'s; a refused sample — a run that refuses a sample does not pass, by the refusals of the eval protocol —; and a report of a sheet or of the workbook that is not of a pair: a sheet is read by a pair, and the reading of one reader is not the platform's reading.
- **The rung "author" also needs answer slots for false "correct"** — no fewer than 20 whose truth is not "correct": otherwise this share would pass on nothing.
- **The unit is the answer slot.** Three repeats of one sheet are not three independent trials, and at zero events `report.Upper` cannot widen the bound for clusters. So an answer slot is an event if at least one of its readings gave it, and it is counted once: the bound has a margin. The protocol's measures by readings go beside, without a target.
- **The rung "author".** Its bound at zero events is about 8 % on 35 answer slots and about 11 % on 25, and it says nothing about the handwriting of other people. `Table.Select` takes the least rung that the deployment names, and the condition concerns any AI route, an own key too. The admission table opens an adult's platform AI with `data_processing_agreement` alone, and a test of `admission` holds it.
- **False "correct" has a target too.** `spec` names false alarms the main measure, but a false "correct" is a missed error of the learner, and admission holds it to the same target.
- **The order of admission.** A model of the list of a tag of `tutor` is admitted only once the pull request of `modules` that moves to this tag and runs the eval sets of all the modules is merged, and `api` takes such a tag after this merge. While the runner is not in the CI of `modules` and there are no eval sets of modules, the list has only the rung "author"; an entry of the rung "full" waits for this order.
- **An entry without a run**: its evidence is the author's word, the day it was given and its last day, 2027-01-17, not a report, and it serves the least rung "author" alone; the list's test fails once the word's last day has passed. The workbook, the essay's reading and the rubric are so admitted, and the rules above stand for the runs on Haiku of Д17.
- **The admission run** — `-reads plain`: the final sheet without the key at 32 px, as the platform reads it, each reader in each repeat, 3 repeats. The key, other heights and states are a measurement, not admission. Opus stays marked "reads once" in the run's record: by the mark the report knows the strong reader of the pair.
- **The prompt fingerprint** in the entry: admission measures the code that `api` calls, and a new prompt fails the list's test until it is measured anew.
- **Retention of 30 days:** Fable 5.1, Fable 5 and both Mythos require it at Anthropic and are unavailable under zero data retention without its permission (sources 1.13). The list's test does not let such models in.
- **The author's sheets** — the plan `tutor-eval gen sheets -tasks 9 -seed 113 -prefix author`, `eval/author-sheets.json`: 9 tasks and 54 sheets of lines, 27 with a correct answer, with the truth by construction; the recipe `just admit-record` writes them into `samples/author-v1`. The author recorded 7 of them, so with the earlier 53 the set has 60 sheets: 35 answer slots with a correct answer and 25 with a wrong one. The run takes the recorded sheets of the plan, not the whole plan, — `tutor-eval run -part recorded`, with a default ceiling of $8.
- **The first entry.** The run of the pair on the 60 sheets, 3 repeats each, gave 0 false alarms on 35 answer slots and 0 false "correct" on 25 — the rung "author" —, for $1.68 (measurements 3.23).

— *Why:* the admission rule is `tutor`'s, and without it and without the list the admission function closes reading, and the first app does not read the work. Rungs — because `spec`'s target of 1 % needs ~300 answer slots without events, and while only the author learns, their handwriting is enough: only theirs is read. The minimum of the rung "author" is 30 and 20 because the author records no more sheets than the 60. The author's word stands while no one but the author learns and the paid runs wait.

— *Rejected:*
- **waiting for the samples of volunteers**, or postponing admission until them — the first app would wait for the samples page of `canvas` and for volunteers, and would not read the work;
- **only the bound no more than 1 %** — the author would have to write ~250 more sheets;
- **the share no more than 1 % without a bound** — it says nothing about accuracy;
- **the bound by readings** — at zero events three repeats would give a bound three times too narrow;
- **the pair Opus · `high` and Haiku · `low`**, which `spec` named first — on the author's ten illegible sheets Haiku · `low` found three unreadable, and Haiku · `medium` read them all;
- **the rule in the package `admission`** — the rule reads the reports and the measures of eval, and `api` calls the package `admission`: they would pull the runner's code into its build. `admission` has only the list and the choice;
- **a choice without a rung** — an entry of the rung "author" would also reach a deployment that names "full";
- **the pair in the order of `-readers`** — `runner.Chosen` takes the configurations in its own order, and chance would name the strong reader.

— *Consequence:* `admission/`; `internal/report/`; `internal/reading/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `eval/author-bank.json`; `eval/author-sheets.json`; `justfile`; `docs/architecture.md`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`.

## The skeleton of a call

**R81. The library's calls are at the root of the module, the package `tutor`: `api` creates `tutor.New` at start — the admission table, the deployment facts, the least rung and the platform's keys at the providers —, and each call carries `tutor.Route`: the AI route, the age category and, on an own key, `tutor.OwnKey`. `tutor` builds the provider's client itself, from the platform's key or an own one; `api` gives the call's deadline as a context. The call's outcome is `tutor.Outcome`: an answer, an AI failure, "limit reached" with a cause, "not admitted" with the admission answer and an own key refusal, — and with it a usage record for each call of a model.** How it is built is in `docs/architecture.md`.
- **The layout** — the calls at the root, and the admission function and the checks of words as subpackages: `api` calls them without a call of a model too, and the root pulls in the providers' SDKs.
- **`tutor` builds the provider's client** — so the providers' SDKs reach the build of `api` only through `tutor`: `api` passes the keys as values, not ready clients.
- **The deadline — as a context of `api`**: `api` knows the request's time, and a deadline of a call kind's own would argue with it.
- **An own key** lives only in the memory of the request: the fields of `OwnKey` are closed, the key itself is behind a pointer, in any format `OwnKey` prints only the provider, and in JSON nothing. So the key does not get into a log even if `api` puts it in a closed field of its own structure and prints it.
- **The choice is only among the providers that can be called.** `admission.Table.Select` takes a list of providers: with an own key, its provider; on platform AI, those whose platform key exists. Otherwise with an own key of Anthropic the choice could take a model of OpenAI — the table opens an adult's own key at both —, and the call would go with someone else's key, and on platform AI a model of a provider without a key. If there are no entries of such providers, the answer is the table's refusal for the first of them, and "model not admitted" only after its reasons.
- **"Not admitted"** — before the client and before the call, without usage: neither the key nor the work goes to the provider.
- **The pair — in parallel**, each with its effort and thinking from the list's entry, not from the request. Usage is written for each call that returned with tokens — for an answer without JSON and for a cut-off one too: it is paid for. A panic of the adapter is a failure of the call, not of the process of `api`.
- **The clients of the platform's keys** are built once per model and kept, and the clients of an own key per request.
- **The recognition of refusals** — "limit reached" and a key refusal — is the adapters'; the skeleton gives their types.

— *Why:* what all the calls share is built once, so that the calls of reading, words, essays and the rubric do not build it anew.

— *Rejected:*
- **everything at the root of `tutor`** — the admission function and the checks of words would pull the providers' SDKs into the build of whoever calls only them;
- **a ready client from `api`** — the SDKs would reach the build of `api` bypassing `tutor`;
- **a deadline of a call kind's own** — it would argue with the deadline of the request of `api`.

— *Consequence:* `tutor.go`; `admission/`; `docs/architecture.md`; `README.md`.

## Reading a sheet

**R82. The call of reading a sheet is `Tutor.ReadSheet`: the PNG of the crop and the task's open view, the pair of readers in parallel, and the answer is each reader's observation at the place of the task's only answer slot with the slot binding `anywhere`, or an outcome of the call without observations. The reading prompt is version 2, with the safety rules for children, one for the library and the runner. The pair's admission was repeated on this version on the same 60 sheets of the author.** How it is built is in `docs/architecture.md`.
- **Only `anywhere`.** On `main` of `modules` (release `v0.1.0`, read 2026-11-08) olympiad maths has 650 tasks of 772 with one answer slot `anywhere`, and the other 122 with a tapped choice; its sheets have no `zone` and `sheet`. A task that the first version does not read the call rejects before the model, and does not read it at random.
- **The task — in the task's own words**: the paragraphs of the statement, the `alt` of its pictures — the reader does not see the statement's pictures — and the text of the item of the answer slot, a line per piece. `tutor` adds no template words of its own here: everything it writes to the model is in the prompt, and the fingerprint holds it.
- **The safety rules** — in the reading prompt too: the learner sees what was read, so the reader adds no words of its own, does not carry out what is written on the sheet, and leaves out what is not the solution and not for a child — only that itself, not the whole part: otherwise the answer would go with it. This is version 2 of the prompt, and the fingerprint of the list's entry changed with it.
- **One code for the library and the runner** — `reading.RequestOf` builds the request in both, and the answer is read by `reading.Read` and `reading.ObservationOf`. The runner also asks for the key, heights and states — a measurement, not the product —, and the output limit is the same for both, 16,000 tokens, `reading.MaxOutputTokens`, and a test holds it equal to the runner's limit.
- **The run — on the admission set**, not on the synthetic sheets of the near-key and of lines: synthetic sheets give no admission of reading a sheet, and admission on the new version is needed anyway. One run gives the comparison with version 1 on the same sheets, the admission and the time of the call. There were two runs — the review refined the rule about what is unsuitable, and the final prompt was measured anew —, $3.54 within a ceiling of $5. The final one: 360 calls, $1.78; the pair has 0 false alarms on 35 answer slots and 0 false "correct" on 25 — the rung "author" again —, and extra "unreadable" are 6 of 180 against 9 for version 1; the pair's time is p50 2.4 s and p90 4.8 s (measurements 3.13).
- **`module-kit`** stays at `v0.2.3`: the join and the verdict of this version are enough for reading, and raising it is an edit of its own.

— *Why:* the main call of the first app is an answer by hand on a sheet, and `api` waits for it.

— *Rejected:*
- **reading `zone` and `sheet` too** — the first modules have none on a sheet, and the prompt and the measure have not been measured for them;
- **a run on the synthetic sheets of the near-key and of lines** — it gives no admission, and a repeated admission on the author's sheets is needed anyway;
- **a second prompt for the library** beside the runner's prompt — eval would measure something other than what `api` calls;
- **giving the observation of one reader** when the second failed — the pair holds the verdict.

— *Consequence:* `sheet.go`; `internal/reading/`; `admission/`; `docs/architecture.md`; `README.md`.

## The checks of words

**R83. The checks of the tutor's words that `api` calls beside the leak check are the package `words` at the root of the module: `words.Hint` — the form of the hint, `words.Feedback` — the feedback rule, with the limits `spec` sets for the tutor's words. The count of words and sentences, the script and the matching of quotes are only in it: the measurement of the capabilities calls it too, with its own limits, `words.HintForm` and `words.FeedbackForm`.** How it is built is in `docs/architecture.md`.
- **`spec`'s limits:** a hint — exactly one question, no more than two sentences, the letters of the explanation language, no more than 30 words in Russian and 35 in English, and in a language that the measurement did not measure, without a limit of words; feedback — no more than three sentences and 80 words in English, and in another language without a limit, a quoted stretch of four words or more — the words of the read text in a row, without case and the marks around them, and the letters of the language. The language is a tag, `en` or `ru-RU`, and its first part counts. The script is checked for Russian and English; a language of another script has no check of the script — the platform does not know its script yet.
- **A quote — against the read text**, not against the essay as it is written: the platform has no essay, only the text that the assessment got.
- **Quotes are read with a margin:** the quotation marks are straight ones, «guillemets», “curly quotes”, ‘single ones’, „German ones“ and ‹single guillemets›, full-width ones after NFKC; ’ between two letters is an apostrophe, not the end of a quote; an unclosed quotation mark draws the quote to the end of the text. Otherwise a made-up quote would pass the check, and it holds that the tutor does not write for the learner.
- **A language that cannot be read is a violation `language`**, not a silent skip of the limits: an empty tag or a tag whose first part is not two or three letters. A language that is read but not measured is without a limit of words and a check of the script.
- **The script of feedback — as written**, without NFKC, as the measurement of the capabilities read it; the script of a hint — after NFKC.
