## Move to `spec` `v0.14.0`

**R90. The move of `spec` to the tag `v0.14.0`, the latest on 2026-11-10, with the Go modules `spec` and of the contracts at `v0.14.0` and `contracts/v0.14.0`, and `module-kit` at `v0.3.0`. The tag brings format 0.4 and the admission table 0.2, and with them everything it tells `tutor` to take, in the same move: the kind `time` in the leak check and in the boxes of the workbook, the file of open views in 0.4, the providers' adapters that the service connects, the eval job with the key from Secret Manager and the rules of measurement samples. The prompts are checked against the tag and do not change, and the move spends nothing on paid runs.**
- **What the tag brought** (`git log` and `git diff v0.9.16 v0.14.0` of the submodule, read 2026-11-10; 59 commits, the tags `v0.10.0`–`v0.14.0`): format 0.4 — the `notation` of a choice by hand, the kind `time`, a worked example on the workbook, `numbered_list`; the legal analysis before the first child — the parts `deployment/`, `legal/`, `consent-age/` and `export/`; the admission table 0.2, with the paths under zero data retention; the keys of eval in Secret Manager; the rule that each part of the Go module `spec` has an `embed.FS` of its own.
- **`tutor`'s question:** the tag recorded where the repeated admission of words stands in `spec`'s cross-repository plan. `spec` set it apart as a step of its own, which not the first app of `api` waits for but the next one, the app with the tutor's words — not inside the first app's step, as the question recommended. These are records of the plan, not a rule. The question went from `run/QUESTIONS.md` into [docs/plan/questions.md](plan/questions.md) word for word, with its answer.
- **What `tutor` takes** — by the rows of `spec`'s technical debt that name `tutor`:
  - **Format 0.4.** `module-kit` `v0.3.0` is its only release on 0.4: it requires `spec` `v0.12.0`, and the format's schemas of `v0.12.0` and `v0.14.0` are the same. `openview.ModuleFormatVersion` is `"0.4"`. The kind `time` is in the leak check `internal/leak`, by `timesIn` and `timeOf` of `spec`: a time is hours of one or two digits and minutes of two or three, not starting from zero, by the hours and minutes as numbers, so `07:30` is the time `7:30`, and `17:30` is not; the vectors are 19 files, 96 texts. In the boxes of the workbook, `sample.KindTime` — the key and the values in the notation of time, without a form, the report's group "time"; `TestTimesVector` runs the observation vector `times.json` by the report's path, `workbook.ItemsOf` → `observation.Join` → `observation.Verdicts` of `module-kit`. Boxes of time are not drawn by code in this move;
  - **the open views** — `eval/open-tasks.json` in format 0.4;
  - **the providers' adapters** — `provider.Dial`, `claude.Dial` and `gpt.Dial`, the field `tutor.Config.Adapters`; `Choose` takes models only of providers with an adapter, and `New` rejects the key of a provider without an adapter — otherwise an error of the service's build would look like an admission refusal. The descriptions of Claude models, the prices and the effort levels are the package `provider/claude/catalog`, without the SDK, one list for all: `claude.Model` is its type alias, and the board, the runner and the tests read `catalog`. Through `internal/reading` and `internal/hint` the root pulled in the eval runner, and that pulled in `provider/claude`; now `runner.Models` takes `provider.Dial` and a key, and the adapter of Claude, `claude.Dial`, is given to it by `cmd/tutor-eval`. `TestRootImportsNoProvider` keeps the root without `provider/claude`, `provider/gpt` and the SDKs, so `api` gets an SDK only with the adapter it connects;
  - **the paths under zero data retention**, by the admission table 0.2 — at OpenAI, `/v1/responses`, `store: false` and a picture `data:` in the request's body, at Anthropic, `/v1/messages`, without the Files API and Batch; the adapters' tests hold all three. Covered Models, which require 30-day retention (sources 1.13), are outside the list of the admitted: `TestAdmitted` does not let Fable 5.1 in there. Not one call judges the learner's feelings. Anthropic publishes no system safety prompt for minors (sources 1.11);
  - **the eval job** [eval.yml](../.github/workflows/eval.yml), with its key from Secret Manager — the environment `eval`, `id-token: write`, as the first step a check of the fork and of the author of the pull request, `google-github-actions/auth` `v3.0.0` pinned by SHA 7c6bc77, without `service_account`, the pool provider `github-eval`, and the secret `eval-key-anthropic` — `get-secretmanager-secrets` `v3.0.0` pinned by SHA bc9c54b, Apache-2.0. The key counts the tokens of one word, which is free (sources 1.16). The run is only manual: Secret Manager has no values of the keys yet, and a run on a pull request is the debt Д6. The environment `eval` has "Selected branches and tags", rules of the type `branch` `main` and `refs/pull/*/merge`, without reviewers (the GitHub API, `environments/eval`); it was set up before the first run, since otherwise GitHub would create it itself at the first run, without a branch rule. The immutable `sub` of `tutor` is on (`use_immutable_subject: true`, the GitHub API);
  - **the rules of measurement samples** — the runner does not compare writers by handwriting, refuses a sample `WRITER_CHILD`, the report has no grade bands, and a sample of a task of another source — a package of `modules` or another tag of `tutor` — gets `REFUSAL_OTHER_TASK`; the new test cases are a task of a package of `modules`. The term of keeping the samples of people and their bucket are in `docs/architecture.md`;
  - **the order of admission** — in the rule of admitting models;
  - **the tools of `spec`'s container** in CLAUDE.md: "Node.js, Go, buf and just".
- **Does not concern `tutor` or is not done by it:** an `embed.FS` of its own for the parts of the module `spec` — `spec.Format` and `spec.Admission` are still `fs.FS`, and `tutor` does nothing; the group `shared-workflows` of Dependabot, which `spec`'s session adds; the parts `deployment/`, `legal/`, `consent-age/` and `export/`, `AttemptService.ReportProblem` and the edits of `api/v1` — `api`, `app` and `infra`; `.devcontainer/` and the template of the kind `go` are the same, but for a comment, and `RULES.md` did not change.
- **What is done:** the submodule is `v0.14.0`, commit c318cb4; `gh release verify v0.14.0 -R altnovatrail/spec` — "Release v0.14.0 verified!"; `go.mod` and `go.sum` — `spec`, `spec/contracts` and `module-kit`, by the Go checksum database; the tag — in CLAUDE.md, README, at the start of RUN.md and in the links of `.bestpractices.json` to RULES.md; the reference log of the reading stub names `module-kit` `v0.3.0`.

— *Why:* the tag is the latest. The submodule and the Go modules of `spec` move in one move, and `module-kit`, whose release on format 0.4 requires the new `spec`, with them. What the tag tells to take is done in the same move: without it the leak check, the open views and the workbook's boxes would lag the format on the tag.

— *Rejected:* **splitting the move** — the move apart, the adapters and the eval job as tasks of their own, and the safety prompt and the runner's rules as inputs of the next epic: one move takes what the tag tells to take. **A new version of the prompts now** — an edit of the reading prompt would take away its admission, since an entry admits only the prompt it measured; a new version waits for a paid run, and there was nothing yet to compare the prompts against, while Anthropic publishes no safety prompt for minors. **A paid eval on a pull request** — every such pull request would cost money, and `tutor`'s eval sets have no plan in CI yet. **Adapters by a factory from `admission.Model`** — `provider/claude` would import `admission`, and the tests of `admission` import `provider/claude`: an import cycle.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `tutor.go`; `provider/`; `provider/claude/`; `provider/claude/catalog/`; `provider/gpt/`; `internal/runner/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `internal/leak/`; `internal/sample/`; `internal/report/`; `internal/workbook/`; `internal/openview/`; `eval/open-tasks.json`; `internal/evalsample/`; `.github/workflows/eval.yml`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `RUN.md`; `.bestpractices.json`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R91. The move of `spec` to the tag `v0.15.0`, the latest on 2026-11-11, with the Go modules of `spec` and of the contracts at `v0.15.0` and `contracts/v0.15.0`; `module-kit` stays at `v0.3.0`. The tag answers `tutor`'s question on Anthropic's safety prompt for minors: the measure "filters" is met by the safety rules in `tutor`'s prompts and the item on safety in its eval sets, and Anthropic's prompt enters it only if Anthropic gives it. A new call — the words after a correct answer — the tag gives `tutor` as a task of its own, not as an edit for the move.**
- **What the tag brought** (`git log` and `git diff v0.14.0 v0.15.0` of the submodule, read 2026-11-11; 16 commits, one tag): the legal texts in force, the laws of the US states, the part `legal/` of version 2 and the types `legalv2` in place of `legalv1`, edits of `AccountService` and `LegalService` in `api/v1`. `RULES.md`, `.devcontainer/`, `repo-template/`, `format/`, `admission/`, the types of the format and of the admission table, and the contracts `observation` and `eval` are the same; the `go.mod` of the module `spec` is the same. There is no release of `module-kit` newer than `v0.3.0` (the Go module proxy, 2026-11-11).
- **`tutor`'s question:** the tag recorded it with the option that `tutor`'s own safety rules meet the measure, not with the question's recommendation to ask Anthropic for the prompt together with the request for zero data retention. The question went from `run/QUESTIONS.md` into [docs/plan/questions.md](plan/questions.md) word for word, with its answer.
- **What `tutor` takes** — by the rows of `spec`'s technical debt that name `tutor`:
  - **the safety prompt for minors** — changes neither the prompts nor the eval sets: the safety rules for children are already in them. `docs/architecture.md` says that Anthropic's prompt enters the words and the reading only if Anthropic gives it;
  - **the words after a correct answer**, `api`'s request — praise of the step and a question about which technique helped, by a call of its own, with a form rule of its own beside the rules of the tutor's words, and not a rung "0" of `Words`. This is a new capability — a prompt, a form check, an eval set and a paid admission —, not an edit for the tag, and a task of its own.
- **Does not concern `tutor`, or is not done by it:** the laws of the US states — `spec` assigns the Texas law on AI (sources 3.8 spec) to `tutor`'s safety rules and to no call judging the learner's feelings, which `tutor` already has, without new work; the texts in force, `legalv2` and `api/v1` — `api`, `app`, `canvas` and `modules`; the container's name on the network comes with `spec`'s template on a later tag; an `embed.FS` per part of the module `spec` and the Dependabot group, as on the move to `v0.14.0`.
- **What was done:** the submodule is at `v0.15.0`, commit 58d3f46; `gh release verify v0.15.0 -R altnovatrail/spec` — "Release v0.15.0 verified!"; `go.mod` and `go.sum` — `spec` and `spec/contracts`, by Go's checksum database; the tag — in CLAUDE.md, README, the start of RUN.md and the links of `.bestpractices.json` to RULES.md.

— *Why:* the tag is the latest. The submodule and the Go modules of `spec` move in one move. What the tag says to take for the move itself is done in the same move, and the new capability is a task of its own, as `spec` asks.

— *Rejected:* **the call of the words after a correct answer in the move** — it needs a prompt of its own, a form rule and an eval set with a paid admission, a task for several checks and a paid run. **Putting the words after a correct answer into a postponed epic at once** — the make-up of an epic is changed by the author, not by a move to a tag. **Waiting for the tag with the container's name on the network** — the move takes the latest tag, and the name comes with the next move.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `CLAUDE.md`; `README.md`; `RUN.md`; `.bestpractices.json`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R92. The longest tests of `cmd/tutor-eval` that substitute nothing shared run in parallel; `ci-test` gives `go test` a timeout of its own — 15 minutes instead of the default 10 — and runs the race detector only on the packages that start goroutines, `RACE` of the justfile, so `cmd/tutor-eval` runs without it.**
- **The parallel tests.** In CI on `main` on 2026-11-10 the tests of `cmd/tutor-eval` under `-race` ran for 458.6 s of the default `go test` timeout of 600. A profile of 2026-11-11, locally: 77 tests, 253 s, and almost as much processor time — the tests ran one at a time. The fourteen longest, 174 s together, substitute nothing shared — neither `checkSamples`, nor the environment, nor the working folder — and write only into their own `t.TempDir()`, and they read the shared folder `drawnOf`. They call `t.Parallel()`. Go runs the parallel top-level tests only after all the sequential ones, so they do not overlap with the substitution of `checkSamples` in the sequential ones. On four cores, as on GitHub's runner, the package takes 209 s instead of 253, and `-race` found no races: under the detector the tests run slower beside one another, and the gain is 17 %.
- **The timeout.** `-timeout 15m` stands on both runs of `go test` in `ci-test`: the timeout of the job of the shared `go.yml` of `.github` is 20 minutes, and it will still stop a hung test.
- **The race detector** runs only on the packages of `RACE`, those that start goroutines; the rest, `cmd/tutor-eval` among them, run without it (measurements 5.1).

— *Why:* the tests of `cmd/tutor-eval` under `-race` ran close to the default timeout. Parallel tests give a margin without edits of the runner's code, and a timeout of its own gives a margin that does not depend on the runner's speed; the gain of 17 % alone might not have been enough on a slow runner. The race detector finds nothing in a package that starts no goroutines, and only slows it.

— *Rejected:* **all the package's tests in parallel** — the tests with `checking`, `t.Setenv` and `t.Chdir` substitute what is shared, and the rest together take less than a third of the time; **speeding up drawing in `internal/gen`** — an edit of the code that draws the eval sets would change their bytes, and that means new fingerprints of the sets and a new run; **a timeout without parallel tests** — the package would still run for almost eight minutes.

— *Consequence:* `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `justfile`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R93. Epic E6 — the admission of words and the library's code outside the first app — returns as the current one once the account is topped up. Its seven tasks go into table 1 of RUN.md with the status `[ ]`, and the description and the bodies into RUN.md. The repeated admission of words goes first, then usage and prices, the provider's refusals and the own key check without spending, and then reading the workbook, reading an essay and assessment by the rubric with runs.**
- **Money.** The account holds about $22. The epic's paid runs by the tasks' ceilings — the workbook $3, the essay $3, the rubric $5, the words $7 — are $18 together, about $10 expected. The executor starts each run within the ceiling of its task, having checked the prices on Anthropic's Pricing page on the day of the run.
- **The repeated admission of words first.** The app with the tutor's words waits for it in `spec`'s cross-repository plan, and only the account held it.
- **How it was moved** — by the plan's rules, as the return of a postponed epic: the table of tasks is table 1, with its result, basis and order, and the description and the bodies are in RUN.md, word for word; only the addresses of relative links were corrected. [docs/plan/E6.md](plan/E6.md) is the header of the archive: the done tasks come there one by one. Table 1 holds no tasks outside the epic, and table 2 is empty: the next epic is written out when E6 nears its end.
- **The tasks' bodies** were written before the moves to `v0.14.0` and `v0.15.0`; what those tags changed is the inputs in the epic's description. The planning of each task checks it against `spec` `v0.15.0`.
- **`spec`'s cross-repository plan** called E6 postponed: a question «В реестр spec» told `spec` that it is current.

— *Why:* there is money for the runs. The next app waits for the repeated admission of words, and not for the rest.

— *Rejected:* **returning only the repeated admission of words**, and leaving the rest postponed — the plan's rules know no partial return; **usage and prices first** — it is without spending, but no app waits for it; **the words after a correct answer into the epic** — their call needed a capability of `spec`'s admission table, which it did not have yet.

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `docs/plan/E6.md`; `README.md`.

**R94. The usage of a call comes with the cache, its model's time and an estimate in dollars, the price table grows from the adapters' prices, and `Tutor.Price(Call, Route)` gives the expected and the worst price of a call by call kind and route. The expected tokens are constants in the code from the measurements, and the output limit is 16,000 for reading and for the words, the same as the eval runner's.**
- **The provider's answer.** `provider.Response` carries the input without the cache, the writes to the cache by retention period and the reads from the cache. At Anthropic `input_tokens` does not include the cache, at OpenAI `input_tokens` includes both the reads and the writes, and the adapter subtracts both (sources 1.14). A write that the answer does not divide by period is counted at the provider's shortest period: at Anthropic 5 minutes, its default period, at OpenAI 30 minutes.
- **Prices** — `provider.Prices` for each model of an adapter, in place of the former two numbers of `Price()`: the input, the output, the reads from the cache, the writes by period, the prices of a long prompt and the date read. Claude's prices are in `provider/claude/catalog`, by Anthropic's Pricing page: at Haiku 5.5 a prompt longer than 100,000 tokens, the cache counted in, goes at prices of its own (sources 1.14). The prices of `gpt-6-astra` are the standard ones, and the prices of a long context, above 272,000 tokens, OpenAI's page gives only by a script; they are absent, and `Prices.Cost` gives no estimate above the threshold.
- **The usage record** `Usage` — the cache, `USD` and `Priced`, and its model's time: `Latency`, which the library times around the adapter's `Ask`, the SDK's `Retries`, and `FirstToken`, the adapter's own, zero at GPT. A call whose tokens are not the whole bill (`provider.Billed`) is estimated at the worst: the largest input of the kind and the request's `MaxOutputTokens`.
- **The price table** `Tutor.Prices` — each model of the list of admitted ones that has an adapter from the config, once, with the adapter's prices. It needs no key: a client without a key calls nothing.
- **The price of a call** `Tutor.Price`, by call kind: reading a sheet and reading the workbook have one capability, but their prompts, limits and entries of the list are their own, so a capability's price would not say which call it estimates. The expected price is by the tokens measured for each model of the chosen entry, constants in `price.go`; the worst — by the largest input of the kind and its output limit, without the cache. The largest input: the limit of the request's text — 24,000 bytes of instructions and text together, and the library refuses a longer request before the call — and the library's largest crop, 21,609 tokens. A token takes at least a byte of text, so there are no more tokens in a text than bytes — our conclusion; a request of the words is about 5,400 bytes. Each token of the worst input goes at the higher of the prices of the input and of a write to the cache: a call may write its instructions to the cache, and OpenAI caches by itself. The price of a call of the words is that of the entry's first model: only it is asked for the words. A model without a measurement has no expected price, and prices that do not hold the worst give no worst one: `api` then takes the value of its own configuration.
- **The kinds of the words.** `Price(CallWords)` refuses the words with the work whose hint is not admitted on the analysis's model, as the call does, so `api` reserves no quota for a call that would answer "not admitted". The hint without the work, `CallHint`, has no expected tokens — the hint was measured with the work —, so its price is the worst alone: the bound of the text, no picture, and the words' output limit. The words after a correct answer, `CallCorrect`, have both: the expected — the averages of the calls that admitted them —, and the worst — the bound of the text and one crop, as with the work.
- **The eval runner and the adapter.** The eval runner counts its calls by the catalog's prices, the cache counted in; its calls are shorter than 100,000 tokens. The Claude adapter takes the division of a write to the cache by period from `message_start`, and the rest of the total as five-minute writes, the period the library asks for.
- **The output limit** — 16,000 for both kinds, as the runner's (`runner.MaxOutputTokens`). The measurements saw no more than 1,772 output tokens, but a narrower limit is no longer the call that eval measured: a model for which it was not enough would give on the platform a refusal without an answer, and in eval it would not.

— *Why:* by the usage `api` counts the platform's AI quota and reserves it before the call, so it needs the tokens with the cache, their price and the worst price of a call before it is made. Constants in the code are checked by a test and a review, while a file would also have to be read and kept in the release.

— *Rejected:* **the expected tokens in a file** — it would have to be read and kept in the release, while constants are checked by a test and a review; **the price by capability** — two calls of one capability differ in prompt, limits and entry; **narrowing the output limit to, for example, 4,000** — eval measured 16,000, and narrowing needs a run of its own; **estimating the long context of `gpt-6-astra` at the prices of the short one** — prices that were not read are not stated; **the worst price by the runner's `inputBudget`, 8,000** — the library takes a crop up to 4,096 pixels a side, and that is up to 21,609 tokens of the picture; **the worst text of the words by the measurement, 4,000 tokens, without a check** — the text carries the statement, the solution, the traps and the past questions without a limit, and such a bound would not hold; **the price through the client of an own key** — prices need no key, and the key would get where it is not needed.

— *Consequence:* `provider/`; `provider/claude/`; `provider/claude/catalog/`; `provider/gpt/`; `tutor.go`; `price.go`; `sheet.go`; `tutorwords.go`; `internal/board/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R95. The adapters recognize the provider's refusals by limit and the key's refusal and give them as errors of their own, and the request layer does not let the SDK repeat them — by the header `x-should-retry: false`. The outcome of a pair is the heaviest refusal: the limit, then the key, then an AI failure.**
- **What is recognized** — the rows of the table of refusals in `docs/architecture.md`, by the providers' documentation (sources 1.15). Anthropic: 400 `invalid_request_error` with the words of the owner's limit — the owner's limit; 429 with `error.details.error_code` `enforced_spend_limit_reached` — the tier limit; the words "Your credit balance is too low to access the Anthropic API" with any code — the balance; 402 — the payment; 401 — the key, except a 401 with the words "CORS requests are not allowed for this Organization", which is not about the key. OpenAI: 429 with the codes `credit_balance_exhausted`, `project_spend_limit_exceeded` and `organization_spend_limit_exceeded`, `organization_usage_limit_exceeded` — the balance, the owner's limit, the tier limit; 401 — the key.
- **The key's refusal** — only 401: an invalid, revoked or expired key, as `spec`'s contract of the own key names it. 403 "the key has no rights" is an outcome of the own key check, and in a call it is an AI failure.
- **Repeats.** Both SDKs decide whether to repeat an answer first by the header `x-should-retry`, and then by the code (sources 1.15). The layer `provider.NoRetry` reads the start of the body of each answer with an error — up to 64 KB, at any code from 400, since Anthropic's words about the balance name no code —, gives the SDK the body as it was, with the error of a broken read too, and sets `x-should-retry: false` on a refusal: ordinary 429, 408, 409 and 5xx the SDK repeats as before. At OpenAI a 429 of the type `insufficient_quota` with an unknown code is the refusal "payment": the page names this type common to the refusals by bill, and only the owner can lift it.
- **The pair.** The limit of even one reader is "limit reached" as a whole, with the cause; the usage of the reader that answered stays, and the refused call has no tokens. The key's refusal comes next, then an AI failure.
- **The words are not a contract.** If the provider changes the text or the code, the refusal becomes an AI failure until the table and its tests catch up: the cause will not reach the key's owner, and an answer with a code that the SDK repeats — 429 or 5xx — it will repeat twice. The library's type `LimitReason` is the same type as `provider.Limit`, so the adapter's cause is always the cause of the outcome. The sources are checked before each release of `tutor`, as the prices are.

— *Why:* repeating such a call is useless, and the key's owner must be told the cause, as `spec`'s contract of the own key asks. Without the layer the SDK itself would repeat a 429 of the tier limit twice, and the adapter would see a failure.

— *Rejected:* **turning off the SDK's repeats entirely** (`MaxRetries` 0) — the repeats of overload and 5xx, which are needed, would be lost; **recognizing the refusal after the SDK has used up its repeats** — needless calls and delay; **403 as the key's refusal** — `spec` names as the key's refusal an invalid or revoked key, and "no rights" is a separate outcome of the check.

— *Consequence:* `provider/refusal.go`; `provider/claude/`; `provider/gpt/`; `tutor.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R96. An own key is checked by `Tutor.CheckKey` on the own key route — by a free request to the provider without the learner's content, for each model that the library will call with this key, of every call kind. The outcomes are the same as those of `spec`'s contract `OwnKeyCheck`: "accepted", "invalid", "the key has no rights", "provider closed" and "the provider did not answer".**
- **The request.** Anthropic — counting the tokens of the word "ok" for the model: the Token counting page calls it free, and it has a limit of its own, separate from the limit of calls. OpenAI — `GET /v1/models/gpt-6-astra`: it writes no tokens, and OpenAI charges for tokens; the pages name no price for it, so "free" is our conclusion (sources 1.16). Both checks go to the model, not to the provider in general: a key to which the model is closed is "no rights".
- **Outcomes.** 401 — "invalid": the key is invalid, revoked or expired, as for the refusals of a call. A 403 of the model — "no rights". 404 is not the key's fault: the provider may not have a model that `tutor`'s catalog knows — and that is "the provider did not answer". A refusal by limit on the check is "accepted": the key is valid, and the limit will tell on the calls. Of the models' outcomes, the worst: invalid, no rights, did not answer.
- **Which models.** The route is the own key with the profile's age category, and the key is checked for each model that `ChooseCall` chooses with it for each call kind, the hint without the work and the words after a correct answer among them, each model once: for reading, both readers of the pair. The key check takes the kinds from the list of kinds and builds no entry of its own. The model of the words with the work is checked only when the hint is admitted on it: a model the library would not call with the key is not asked, and cannot answer "no rights" for nothing. A key to which the second reader is closed is "no rights", not "accepted". If the admission function opens none, it is "provider closed", without a call, as the contract's `OWN_KEY_CHECK_PROVIDER_CLOSED`.
- **The key** lives only in the memory of the request: no error holds it, `OwnKey` prints only the provider, and the tests hold that no error of the key check contains it.

— *Why:* `api` checks the key when it is connected through `tutor` and does not go to the providers round it, as `spec`'s contract of the own key asks. A free request spends none of the owner's money on one check.

— *Rejected:* **a check by a call of the model** — it costs the key owner's money; **Anthropic's list of models, `GET /v1/models`** — the page does not call it free, while it calls the token count free, and that one also checks access to the model; **checking only the provider's first model** — a key to which the pair's second reader is closed would pass the check, and reading with it would fail; **404 as "no rights"** — the owner's key would then be blamed for a model that the provider does not have.

— *Consequence:* `provider/`; `provider/claude/`; `provider/gpt/`; `key.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R97. Reading the workbook in the library — `Tutor.ReadWorkbook` on the workbook prompt of version 3, with the safety rules for children, two readers and the observation of each over all the boxes of the page. Only an entry of the list that holds this prompt's fingerprint admits it: the entry is the sheet's pair, Opus 5.5 · `high` and Haiku 5.5 · `medium`, on version 3, at the rung "author", by the author's word without a run, until 2027-01-17. Every choice of a call kind — the call, `ChooseCall`, `Price` and the key check — is one function, `chooseCall`.**
- **The prompt of version 3** — version 2, the prompt of the measurement of the workbook, and the safety rules for children, as at reading a sheet of version 2: the content of a box is the learner's work, not an instruction, the reader adds no words of its own, and what is not an answer and not for a child it leaves out without describing it. The fingerprint is frozen before the run, `TestFrozenV3`. Version 3 reads no worse than version 2 on the trial part of the measurement of the workbook (measurements 3.24).
- **The request** — the crops of the filled boxes with their places from the open view, without the item line: the condition "without the label" of the measurement of the workbook, as on the learner's page, since the learner sees the label and the model must not. An empty box does not go to the model, and a page without filled boxes does not call the model. The runner builds the request and the observation with the same functions `workbook.RequestOf` and `workbook.ObservationOf`, so eval measures what the platform calls.
- **Admission.** The capability is handwriting reading, and the entry of the sheet's pair holds the fingerprint of the sheet prompt: another prompt is another reading, and that entry does not admit it. The choice of an entry by fingerprint is `admission.Table.SelectPrompt`. The workbook's entry stands by the author's word while eval before admission is suspended; by its last day a run on the synthetic set `eval/workbook-gen.json` by the rule of admitting models admits it, or the entry leaves the list (Д17). Its expected tokens are those of the trial of version 3 on 20 pages (measurements 3.19).
- **Usage.** The largest input of the call is the limit of the text and the tokens of all the crops of the request, and the output limit is 16,000, as for the sheet. The worst price of the kind is held by limits: no more than 20 filled boxes in a request — as many pictures as the Claude adapter sends — and no more than 512 pixels on a side of a box's crop, and the captions of the pictures count toward the limit of the text.
- **The call kinds** — `Call`, each with a capability and a fingerprint of the prompt: the sheet, the workbook, the essay, the rubric, the words with the work, the hint without it, `CallHint`, and the words after a correct answer, `CallCorrect` — the capability `correct_answer_words`, the prompt of `internal/correct`, one model, with the work or without it. All paths choose the entry by them, through `chooseCall`: the call, `Tutor.ChooseCall`, which `api` calls before the attempt, the price `Tutor.Price` and the key check; for the words with the work it also holds the hint on the analysis's model. Otherwise the sheet could take the workbook's entry, and `api` could admit what the call will not admit. `Choose` by capability stays and chooses the capability's first entry.

— *Why:* the code of reading the workbook was only in eval, and the platform calls it. The safety rules are in all the platform's prompts.

— *Rejected:* **admitting the workbook by the entry of the sheet's pair** — the entry measured another prompt; **the prompt of version 2 without the safety rules** — the safety rules are in all prompts; **a label by the item line** — the learner sees it on the workbook page, but the model must not: so `spec` decided; **a filter by prompt only for the workbook** — the review showed that the sheet, the price, the key check and `Choose` would not know it, and an entry of the workbook inserted first would read sheets.

— *Consequence:* `internal/workbook/`; `admission/`; `tutor.go`; `workbook.go`; `price.go`; `key.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R98. Reading an essay in the library — `Tutor.ReadEssay`: one reader, the text as written, "unreadable" only for the essay as a whole, on the reading prompt in two calls of version 2 — with the flag `legible` and the safety rules for children. The call kind is `CallEssay`, with its own fingerprint of the prompt; its entry is Haiku 5.5 · `medium` at the rung "author", by the author's word without a run, until 2027-01-17.**
- **The prompt.** Version 2 of the prompts in two calls: the reading has `legible`, false when the essay as a whole cannot be read, while a word that cannot be made out does not make the essay unreadable, and an empty sheet is readable, with an empty text; and the safety rules for children, as at reading a sheet. The assessment of the text is the same as in version 1, and version 3 edits it. The fingerprint of the two calls is frozen before the run, `TestFrozenTwoV2`; the library has the fingerprint of the reading alone, `essay.ReadFingerprint`. The reading is no worse than version 1 on the test part of the essays in two calls (measurements 3.24).
- **The observation** — the task's answer slot bound to the whole sheet: the text as written, "unreadable" for an illegible one, "blank" for an empty one. A task without such an answer slot or with two is not an essay, and the call refuses before the model.
- **The reader** — one, the entry's first model: one reader reads an essay, without the rubric. Admission is by the fingerprint of the essay reading prompt, as for the workbook. The entry stands by the author's word while eval before admission is suspended, and by its last day a run on the synthetic essays admits it, or it leaves the list (Д17).
- **The runner** `essay -two` builds the reading request and reads the answer with the same functions. An essay read as illegible is a reading error on a legible essay drawn by code, not an answer outside the schema, and the runner does not ask for the assessment of the text. The log of a run in two calls also names the fingerprint of the reading alone, `read_prompt`: the entry of the list of essay reading holds it. The report on the logs of version 1 reads their readings without `legible`, `essay.ReadReadingOf`.
- **The models of a call.** `ChooseCall`, the price and the key check take from the entry only the models that the call asks: for the essay and the words, the first.

— *Why:* the first of the two calls of a task with a rubric: an essay that cannot be read does not reach the assessment, and the learner writes it again.

— *Rejected:* **a field for a word that cannot be read** — Т-37: its form will come from the measurement on the essays of volunteers (П-16); **`legible` only in the library** — eval would measure another prompt than the one the platform calls; **a pair of readers** — one reader reads an essay, by `spec`'s rule.

— *Consequence:* `internal/essay/`; `essay.go`; `price.go`; `key.go`; `admission/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R99. Assessment by the rubric in the library — `Tutor.AssessEssay`: the criteria by numbers, the assessment of each with what decided it, and one piece of feedback, on the text assessment prompt of version 3 — with the feedback language from the request, the clause on gender and the safety rules. The gender and safety of the feedback are judged by the words judge. The rubric is admitted at the rung "author" by the author's word without a run, until 2027-01-17: Haiku 5.5 · `medium` on the reading of Haiku 5.5 · `medium`, feedback in English only.**
- **The prompt.** Version 3 of the prompts in two calls: the text assessment has the feedback language from the request in place of English, the clause on gender, as the hint has, and the safety rules for children. The reading is the same as in version 2, and version 2 never ran anywhere. The fingerprint is frozen before the runs of the reading and of the assessment, `TestFrozenTwoV3`; the library has the fingerprint of the assessment alone, `essay.TextFingerprint`. The assessment and the feedback are no worse than version 1 on the test part of the essays (measurements 3.24).
- **The criteria** of the module format are only text, without an ID (`formatv0.Criterion`), and they go to the model by numbers, from 1. A criterion's assessment is its one judgement; two or none — without an assessment, and a failure — all without an assessment.
- **The feedback** is checked by `api` with the feedback rule, beside the leak check: the library does not have its rules, and it gives the feedback as it is.
- **The gender and safety of the feedback** — the same words judge, its prompt and control phrases, as for the hint: feedback is also the tutor's words to the learner. Its prompt knows feedback on an essay as rung 0, and five of its control phrases are about feedback, one of them feedback not about the task; the judge is checked by the control phrases before it judges, as the rule of words requires. `tutor-eval judge-words -log` on the log of essays in two calls judges each piece of feedback as a hint without a rung, in English, with the essay's topic as the task. The judge's command reads the file of essays that the log named from where the run went, and checks each essay of the log against it — by part and by unmet criteria.
- **Admission.** The capability is assessment by a rubric. The rule of its run at the rung "author" is on the synthetic set `eval/essays-gen.json`: the upper 95 % bound of false alarms on met criteria no more than 1 %, feedback in English only — the target `spec` records for the rung "author"; the target on the essays of volunteers stays that of the rung "full" (П-16). The entry stands by the author's word while eval before admission is suspended, and by its last day a run on Haiku by that rule admits it, or the entry leaves the list (Д17). The log of a run in two calls names the fingerprint of the text assessment alone, `text_prompt`, which the entry holds. The library sends any language that `words.Feedback` checks; feedback in another language than English waits for its own measurement.
- **The request.** An empty text, topic or style, an empty criterion and a language that `words.Feedback` will not read the call refuses before the model: otherwise paid feedback would always go to the fallback text.

— *Why:* the second call of a task with a rubric; feedback is the tutor's words to the learner too, so it takes the clause on gender and the safety rules of the words.

— *Rejected:* **IDs of criteria in the library** — the format does not have them, and the number in the rubric's order is unambiguous; **the check of feedback in the library** — `api` calls the feedback rule, beside the leak check; **a judge of feedback of its own** — feedback is the tutor's words too, and one judge with its control phrases judges them all; **no admission until the measurement on the essays of volunteers** — that measurement sets the target of the rung "full", and the rung "author" has its own on the synthetic essays; **the reading of Opus 5.5 · `high` under the rubric's entry** — the entry is Haiku's on both calls, and the run that is to confirm it is on Haiku.

— *Consequence:* `internal/essay/`; `rubric.go`; `price.go`; `internal/wordsjudge/`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `admission/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R100. The tutor's words are admitted: Opus 5.5 · `high` on the hint prompt of version 3 passed the admission rule of words at the rung "author", with the judge Opus 5.5 · `high` on the judge prompt of version 3. The list of admitted models has two entries of the words, the analysis of the course of solution and the hint. The words are admitted by new versions of the prompts with their own eval, not by weakening the targets after the first run.**
- **The hint prompt of version 3** — by the outcome of the first run of the rule of words: the simpler case of rung 3 is made of its own numbers, without a single number of the key, and the hint counts its words and cuts words, not the question. The trial run is on separate synthetic sheets `lines-gen-2`, not in the admission sets, so that the prompt is not fitted to the test. After it the prompt is frozen, `TestFrozenV3`.
- **The judge prompt of version 3** — the simpler case of rung 3 is about the task, praise of correct lines does not do what a note asks, and rung 0 is feedback on an essay. To the control phrases are added 6 of the same kind and 5 about feedback — among them feedback not about the task —, 50 in all.
- **The judge.** Sonnet 5.5 · `low` passed the first 49 phrases, but on the hints it flagged two correct ones "not about the task", and the rule would not have admitted Opus. The hints were judged again with the judge Opus 5.5 · `high`, on the same prompt and with the same targets, on the condition that it passes all the control phrases: it passed all 50, and the rule gave "author". The judge was changed when Sonnet's flags were already visible, and this too is a choice after the data. Its bound is that the hints and the targets are the same, and the new judge was checked by control phrases before judging, as the rule requires; the logs of both judges are kept, and the rule's output with Sonnet is recorded here as well.
- **The outcome** (measurements 3.16): leaks 0 of 300, form violations 1, gender, leaving the task, unsuitable for the age and picked-up notes — 0. The entries of the list come with the rule's output, `samples/runs/t127-words.admit.md`, sha256 1e49fa51…d68a, its sha256 and the fingerprint of the hint prompt; the test of the list holds that the entries of words have one model and the fingerprint of the prompt of this build.
- **Money.** $11.08 was spent. The ceiling of $7 was raised to $8, then lifted. $1.68 went on two attempts that the spending limit cut short — of the workspace `api-dev`, whose key was used, then of the organization.

— *Why:* the app with the tutor's words needs an admitted call of the words, and the outcome of the first run showed that the errors come from the prompt's words and from the judge, which new versions with their own eval fix.

— *Rejected:* **weakening the targets after the run** — that would move the target after the data; **accepting "not admitted" with the judge Sonnet** — its two flags are false alarms, and the rule measures the hints, not the judge; **a person reading the judge's flags beyond the rule** — a person has no check by control phrases, and the rule recognizes only a checked judge; **the trial run on the admission sets** — the prompt would be fitted to the test.

— *Consequence:* `internal/hint/`; `internal/wordsjudge/`; `eval/words-judge.json`; `admission/`; `tutor_test.go`; `internal/runner/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R101. Epic E6 is done and goes whole into the archive [docs/plan/E6.md](plan/E6.md); the author chooses the next epic. The debt Д6 stays in the table of debt, `run/DEBT.md`: it waits for the eval keys in the Secret Manager of `infra`.**
- **What the epic did** — all seven tasks: the tutor's words are admitted — Opus 5.5 · `high` on the hint prompt of version 3, the rung "author"; the library has the usage of a call and the price table, the provider's refusals, the own key check, reading the workbook and essays and assessment by the rubric. The workbook, the essay and the rubric wait for entries of the list of their own.
- **The archive** — as for a done epic by the plan's rules, and as for E5: the tasks' bodies word for word, in the order of the table, and table 1 with the description of E6 and the start of its section at the end of the file. The tasks did not go into the archive one by one: all seven were done in one day. Only the addresses of relative links were corrected.
- **Д6** — [eval.yml](../.github/workflows/eval.yml) has had no runs (`gh run list`, read 2026-11-11), and E6 did not bring the first run of an eval set in CI: its condition "when to return" is the keys and a task of its own.
- **The words after a correct answer** are not part of E6: `spec` gave them a capability of their own, `correct_answer_words`, with rules as for `hint`, in its admission table 0.3, and without that table in the submodule the admission function would not let the call through. Once the submodule carries the table, they wait only for a task of their own.
- **`spec`'s cross-repository plan** — a question «В реестр spec» told `spec` that all of E6 is done.

— *Why:* the rules of RUN.md keep only what is live there.

— *Rejected:* **leaving E6 current without open tasks** — table 1 would hold an epic with nothing to start; **writing out the next epic at once** — the author chooses it, and table 2 is empty; **taking the words after a correct answer into work in E6** — without the admission table 0.3 the call cannot be admitted, the task is one for several checks, and the author chooses its place.

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `run/EPICS.md`; `run/DEBT.md`; `docs/plan/E6.md`; `README.md`.

## Move to `spec` `v0.16.3` and the token savings

**R102. `spec` is at the tag `v0.16.3`, the latest on 2026-11-12, the Go modules of `spec` and of the contracts are at `v0.16.3` and `contracts/v0.16.3`, and `module-kit` is at `v0.3.1`. With the tag `tutor` takes the way of working of the 0.16 line, `spec`'s token savings: one task is one session and the hook, the rules of the parts `.claude/rules/`, the body of a done task and its gist sentence straight into the archive, the technical debt in `run/DEBT.md`, the external facts as records of `docs/sources.md`, the measurements in `docs/measurements.md`, `docs/` in English and the size budgets of `docs-check`. The platform's plan has every repository take the line in one change — the move, the restructuring of the documents after `spec`'s pattern and the translation of `docs/` —, and `tutor` took it whole, in parts.**
- **What the tag brought** (`git log` and `git diff v0.15.0 v0.16.3` of the submodule; 44 commits, the tags `v0.15.1`–`v0.16.3`):
  - `spec`'s token savings: one task is one session and the hook `spec/tools/session/`, the rules of the parts by `paths`, the debt table in a file of its own, the bodies of done tasks straight into the archive, external facts as records of `docs/sources.md`, measurements into `docs/measurements.md`, `docs/` in English by `spec/tools/translation/`; the size budgets in `docs-check` and the measurement `tokens-report`; the plugin `gopls-lsp` of `spec/claude-plugins/`;
  - RULES.md: the documents of `docs/` are written for a session of Claude Code, a fact is a record of `docs/sources.md`, the language of `docs/` is English, and a document in Russian stays so until a task of its own translates it; `spec`'s inbox triage is on the author's word or at the end of an epic;
  - the admission table 0.3 with its seventh capability, `correct_answer_words`; Go 1.27.4 in the stage `toolchain` of the template; the contract `app` ↔ `api` — `own_key_provider`, the names in progress and the quota share —, which `tutor` does not call.
- **The parts.** `tutor`'s `docs/` in Russian were about 1.8 MB — architecture 823 KB, the log 828 KB, the descriptions of the sheets 124 KB —, more than one review, and the other repositories were to wait for the pilot of `module-kit`. `tutor` took the change without waiting, in six parts: the move with the template's rules; the sources; the measurements; the translation of architecture and of the descriptions of the sheets; the translation of the log; the budgets with the measurement "after".
- **What the move did:**
  - the submodule at `v0.16.3`, commit 664398e, its release verified by `gh release verify`; `go.mod` and `go.sum` — `spec` and `spec/contracts` `v0.16.3` and `module-kit` `v0.3.1`, whose release moves `module-kit` to `spec` `v0.15.0` without edits of code; the golden log of the reading stub names `v0.3.1`;
  - `.claude/settings.json` — as `just new-repo-draft tutor go 3.6 v0.16.3` builds it from a copy of `spec` in a scratch folder: the lines of `claudeMdExcludes` that keep `spec`'s own CLAUDE.md and rules out, the size at which a session compacts, the claude.ai connectors off and the hook `UserPromptSubmit`. The plugin `gopls-lsp` loads from `spec/claude-plugins/gopls-lsp` by `CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_DIRS` in `containerEnv` of `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`, not from a marketplace of `.claude/settings.json`, as the template has it;
  - the rules of the parts — [go](../.claude/rules/go.md), [eval](../.claude/rules/eval.md) and [ci](../.claude/rules/ci.md), and CLAUDE.md names them. The details of `synthetic/` by command are in the rule `eval`, while the prohibitions of `samples/` and `synthetic/` stay in CLAUDE.md: `docs-check` checks `paths` only against the files that git sees, and both folders are outside git, so a rule by them would not load. AGENTS.md follows `spec`: an item about the rules by paths, so that a session other than Claude Code knows of them too;
  - RUN.md — the template's rules, in English and with their numbers: one task is one session, the debt table in [run/DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md), the body of a done task and its gist sentence straight into the archive, the token measurement at an epic's archiving;
  - the devcontainer follows the template: the comment on the build of gopls and the line `go` of `.devcontainer/gopls/go.mod` at the template's Go.
- **The translation.** The log and every document of `docs/` but for the epic archives `docs/plan/` are in English, by `spec/tools/translation/README.md`, in batches of 70–80 KB, each piece clean by `pair`; `translation-check` of the whole log against its Russian original was clean, and a blind reading of six stretches found one difference of meaning — «запись сбоку» is a note at the side, not working —, fixed. The names of the blocks of architecture that went to `docs/sources.md` and `docs/measurements.md` stay Russian in «…».
- **The budgets.** [docs-budgets.json](../docs-budgets.json) names, over the block `repository` of `spec/tools/docs-check/budgets.json`, the sections and entries that need more, each at its size plus 12–15 %, rounded (measurements 1.9 spec); the rest keeps within the block. The first `docs-budgets.json` of a repository is its limits, not a raise: `spec`'s threshold of an analysis of the sessions' tokens — three raises of a budget since the last analysis, by `git log` of the file — counts the edits after it.
- **"After"** — the six sessions of the change, measured by `tokens-report` (measurements 4.1): the reference the sessions of `tutor`'s later epics are compared with.
- **What `tutor` takes** by the rows of `spec`'s debt table that name it: admission table 0.3 — the tests of `admission` run its answer vector, 23 cases instead of 16 —, and four debts of its own: the words after a correct answer, the adapters without the environment's defaults, the hint without the work as a call kind, and the time of each model in its usage record. The rows of `api` and `app`, and the container's name on the network, are not `tutor`'s.

— *Why:* the tag is the latest. The submodule and the Go modules move in one move, by the platform's rule of the submodule, and `module-kit` with them, as `tutor` always moves it. The template's rules are taken as they are, so that `tutor` does not diverge from the other repositories, and its own differences — the rules of the parts and the prohibitions of the folders outside git — are named here. Raising a limit is the edit `spec`'s budgets provide, seen in review: splitting the sections over it by headings on top of the translation would have taken a part past one review.

— *Rejected:* **the restructuring and the translation after the pilot of `module-kit`** — the change would wait, while `spec` was already on the 0.16 line; **all in one task** — `docs/` were about 1.8 MB, and `spec` translated its 2.7 MB in four parts; **a rule of a part for `samples/` with `paths` by the folder** — `docs-check` would count a pattern without a file as an error, and Claude Code would not load the rule when a command touches the folder without reading a file; **the budgets before the sources and the measurements left architecture** — it would legitimize what was to leave; **headings under the sections over their budget** — the block "Packages" alone was 27 KB, and the rest would be a restructuring of architecture, not a translation; **one limit for every section** — it would let every other section grow to the largest.

— *Consequence:* the submodule, `go.mod` and `go.sum`; `internal/report/testdata/reading-stub.jsonl`; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`, `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json` and `.devcontainer/gopls/go.mod`; `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/rules/`; `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `.bestpractices.json`; `RUN.md` and `run/DEBT.md`; `docs-budgets.json`; `docs/`.

## RUN.md without tasks outside an epic

**R103. RUN.md keeps only what a session acts on and what is not said elsewhere, and has no tasks outside an epic. A task that does not come from an epic — a move to a tag of `spec`, an analysis of the sessions' tokens, an instruction of the author — goes into table 1 of the current epic, and when there is none, it is the first task of the next; `run/TASKS.md`, which `spec`'s template brings for such tasks, is not taken. The rules keep their numbers, so that the plan and the archives that name them hold.** The pattern is `module-kit`'s RUN.md, and `spec`'s rule of epics lets a repository whose own rule makes a task outside an epic a task of an epic keep that rule.
- **What was cut:** the section of tasks outside an epic — its gists and table went word for word to [docs/plan/E6.md](plan/E6.md), where their bodies are; the case of rule 9 without a current epic; the history of the epics, of tables 1 and 2 and of the postponed epics, which the archives and this log hold; the list of links, and the text of the rules that RULES.md and CLAUDE.md already say — the devcontainer, external facts and measurements, what `docs-check-repo` checks. The statuses, «Кто», «План» and the body of a task are each one paragraph, and the rules of the plan's layout say the same in fewer words.
- **What RUN.md does not keep:** the gist sentences of done tasks and the list of done epics — they are in the archives `docs/plan/`.

— *Why:* RUN.md is read by every task's session, so each byte of it is paid for in every session. What CLAUDE.md and RULES.md say is loaded into each session anyway, and the history of the epics is in their archives and in this log.

— *Rejected:* **renumbering the rules after cutting the text of 3 and 5** — the plan and the archives name rules 4, 9, 11 and 12 by number; **keeping the section of tasks outside an epic empty, as in the template** — a section and a rule for a case `tutor` does not need; **`run/TASKS.md`** — one more table for that same case.

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `CLAUDE.md`; `docs/plan/E6.md`.

## Epic E7: the release for `api` and the debts without spending

**R104. Epic E7 — the release for `api` and the debts without spending — takes from the debt table what needs neither money nor the author's decisions: the adapters without the environment's defaults, the hint without the work as a call kind, the time of each model in its usage record with the time of the words measured on the logs of the admission of the words, and the sources of the outside facts of the cost and time of a check. The adapters go first. The epic is done, all four tasks, and its archive is [docs/plan/E7.md](plan/E7.md).**
- **What `api` waits for.** `spec`'s cross-repository plan has a release of `api` take the adapters without the environment's defaults, and the platform's AI opens to anyone but the author only after it; the hint without the work and the time of each model are `api`'s too. Each task with code gives a release of its own: the release follows CI on `main`.
- **Found while planning** (the modules' sources in the module cache; sources 1.17): Anthropic's SDK without the environment's defaults still writes its OpenTelemetry spans by default, and OpenAI's has no public option without the environment. The adapters' choices answer both.
- **The time of the words without a run.** The logs of the hints of the admission of the words, `synthetic/t127/`, carry `latency_ms` and `first_token_ms` for each call, so the measure of eval that `api` asks for is taken from them, and the epic has no paid run.

— *Why:* the debts that the next release of `api` waits for need no money and no decision of the author, and the sources were the one debt left of the token savings. A task that does not come from an epic is the first of the next one, so they make an epic.

— *Rejected:* **the words after a correct answer in the epic** — they need the form of the words and a paid admission run; **the eval keys in CI** — they wait for the keys `eval-key-…` in `infra`'s Secret Manager, the author's step (Д6); **the effort `medium` for the words** — it is measured at the next admission of the words, a paid run; **the sources outside the epic** — `tutor` has no tasks outside an epic; **one task for the hint without the work and the time of each model** — two kinds of change, one check each, and `api` takes them by different rows.

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `run/DEBT.md`; `README.md`; `docs/plan/E7.md`.

## The adapters without the environment's defaults

**R105. The adapters of Claude and GPT take nothing from the process's environment: a call goes to the provider's production address, with the key that `tutor` was given, with the adapter's client and with no header, profile, federation or tracing of the environment. The Claude adapter builds its client with `option.WithoutEnvironmentDefaults()` and `option.WithoutOpenTelemetry()`; the GPT adapter builds the services it calls, `responses.NewResponseService` and `openai.NewModelService`, without `openai.NewClient`; both send by `provider.HTTPClient`. What Go's transport takes from the process — the proxy and the certificates — stays the deployment's.**
- **What the SDKs read** is sources 1.17, the code of anthropic-sdk-go v1.84.0 and openai-go v3.94.0. `WithoutEnvironmentDefaults` skips the whole of Anthropic's `DefaultClientOptions` — the address, the key, the token, the profile with its folder, the federation, the webhook key and `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` — but not the tracing, which by default writes a span of each call into the process's `TracerProvider`, sends its trace context to Anthropic, and with `ANTHROPIC_OPEN_TELEMETRY_TRACES_CONTENT_MODE=content` puts the request and the answer, the learner's work among them, in the span. `openai.NewClient` always puts the variables `OPENAI_…` first, and its switch without them is internal; its services, built by themselves, read none.
- **The client.** Without the defaults, Anthropic's SDK would send by `http.DefaultClient`, without the timeout of 10 minutes for the headers of an answer that its own client has. `provider.HTTPClient` is the adapters' client: one transport, shared by every client so that they share its connections, with the settings of Go's default transport and that timeout. It is not `http.DefaultTransport`, as the SDKs' own client is when the process has wrapped it: an instrumentation there, such as `otelhttp`, would write a span of the call and send its trace context to the provider, past `WithoutOpenTelemetry`.
- **The proxy and the certificates** — `HTTPS_PROXY`, `NO_PROXY`, `SSL_CERT_FILE`, `SSL_CERT_DIR` — are read by Go's transport for every client of the process, not by the SDKs: they are the network of `api`'s deployment, and through a proxy of `CONNECT` the TLS goes on to the provider.
- **The tests** — `TestNoEnvironment` of each adapter — set each variable the SDK reads, and all of them together, and hold that `Ask` and `Check` go to the production address with the configured key and with no header of the environment, and for Claude, with the caller's context in a trace and the process tracing, that the SDK asks for no tracer and sends no `traceparent`. Without the options, the address, the token, the profile, the headers, the organization and the project, and the tracing fail them; the key of the environment, the federation and the folder of the configuration yield to the key the adapter gives, and their subtests hold that they go on yielding.

— *Why:* where a variable is set, the platform's key and the learner's work would go past the processor that the platform's privacy policy names, and `api`'s log would not see it; the release of `api` that opens the platform's AI to others waits for this. A span of the SDK would decide for `api` what goes into its traces, and the time of a call comes with its usage record.

— *Rejected:* **for GPT, `openai.NewClient` with options over it** — the production address and `WithHeaderDel` of the organization, the project and the headers of `OPENAI_CUSTOM_HEADERS`: it holds only as long as the SDK adds no variable; **an adapter that is not built when a variable is set** — a variable of someone else's would bring down `api`'s process; **spans without content** — `metadata_only` and the propagation off: the spans would still go to `api`'s traces, as the SDK decides; **the process's `http.DefaultTransport`, as the SDKs take it** — what the process wraps it with would reach the call; **an adapter that ignores the proxy** — `api` behind an egress proxy would not reach the providers, and the TLS goes to the provider through it anyway.

— *Consequence:* [provider/http.go](../provider/http.go), [provider/claude/claude.go](../provider/claude/claude.go), [provider/gpt/gpt.go](../provider/gpt/gpt.go) and their `environment_test.go`, with what those share, [internal/providertest](../internal/providertest/providertest.go); `go.mod` — `go.opentelemetry.io/otel` and `otel/trace` direct, for the test; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The hint without the work as a call kind

**R106. The hint without the work — "explain it another way", which sends no work — is a call kind of its own, `CallHint`: the capability `hint`, the fingerprint of the words' prompt, one model and no picture. `Tutor.ChooseCall(CallHint)` chooses its entry of the list, and `api`'s gateway asks it instead of `Choose(hint)`. It has no expected tokens, so `Price` gives its worst alone.**
- **What changed.** `Choose` takes the first entry of a capability, whatever prompt it measured, while the call of words takes only an entry of its own prompt. An entry of `hint` on another prompt put before the present one would make the gateway admit "explain it another way" and the call refuse it. The words without the work and the key check take the hint's entry from the list of kinds, `kinds[CallHint]`, and the words with the work hold the hint by the same kind, in the one choice of a call kind.
- **The price.** The input is the bound of the text, `maxTextBytes`, with no picture; the output is the words' bound, 16,000. The admission of the words measured the hint with the work (measurements 3.16), so the kind has no expected tokens: `CallPrice.Expected` is false, and `api` takes the value of its own config, as for the kinds without a measurement.
- **The tests.** `TestChooseCallHint`: with an entry of `hint` on another prompt put first, `Choose(hint)` takes it, `ChooseCall(CallHint)` takes the entry of the words' prompt, and the words without the work ask its model; with that entry alone, neither admits. `TestPrice` — the worst and no expected price of `CallHint`; `TestCheckKeyHint` — the key check asks the hint's model by its prompt alone, and `TestCheckKey` asks the same models as before.

— *Why:* `api`'s gateway decides the attempt, and reserves its quota, by what `tutor` will call: the choice of the gateway and the choice of the call must be one function of the same kind.

— *Rejected:* **`Choose` by capability filtered by the words' prompt** — `Choose` would answer for one call of a capability, while the words' prompt is not the only one a capability can be admitted on; **expected tokens of the hint from the words with the work** — the request without a picture is shorter by the picture's tokens, and a price not measured would pass for a measured one; **`CallWords` serving both, by the crop** — `ChooseCall` has no crop, and the gateway asks before the call.

— *Consequence:* [price.go](../price.go) — `CallHint`; [tutorwords.go](../tutorwords.go); [key.go](../key.go); `tutor_test.go`, `price_test.go`, `key_test.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

**R107. Each record of usage carries its model's time: `Usage.Latency`, which the library times around the adapter's `Ask` — the span the eval runner times as `latency_ms` —, with the SDK's `Usage.Retries`, and `Usage.FirstToken`, the adapter's own measure, zero at GPT. The time of the words on the hint prompt of version 3 is measured on the logs of the admission of the words, without a new run: p50 5.5 s and p90 7.6 s over 425 calls (measurements 3.16). The effort `medium` of the hint is measured in part, and Д11 keeps the rest.**
- **Where the time is taken.** The runner times a call itself, around the adapter's `Ask` (`internal/runner`), not in the adapter. So `ask` in [tutor.go](../tutor.go) times each model the same way, in the goroutine that asks it, the SDK's retries counted in; the time is set in the deferred function that also catches a panic, and `usageOf` builds the whole record with it. The one difference: the runner's first call of a model also builds the model's client, which sends nothing, while the library builds it before `ask`. The record carries the retries too, `provider.Response.Retries`, as the runner's log does: without them a call the SDK sent again would pass for a slow model. `provider.Response` and the adapters do not change: `FirstToken` was already there, counted from the attempt that answered; the Claude adapter gives zero when no text came, and the GPT adapter, which does not stream, always.
- **The measure.** Every call of prompt version 3 that came back with a hint: the author's sheets, 150, from `samples/runs/` — only numbers leave it —, the set of the words, 150, the answered calls of the two attempts the limit cut short, 102, and the trial run, 23. The 145 refusals for the limit do not count. The percentile is the nearest rank, as in `internal/report`. The time to read a sheet is measurements 3.13, and eval of reading is not repeated.
- **The effort `medium`** — 136 hints of the synthetic part of the set, by code, without the judge: p50 4.4 s against 5.2 s at `high` on the same cases, p90 6.2 s against 7.2 s (measurements 3.18). The credit ran out before the rest, which waits (Д11).
- **The tests.** `TestAskTimes`: the readers of a pair on fakes with their delays — each record carries its own time, the one with the delay the longer, the first token and the retries of one fake reach its record and the other's are zero, and a billed answer without the JSON is timed too. The bounds leave a fake that answers at once 200 ms of slack, so that a loaded runner under `-race` does not fail the test. A fake clock would not do: the two readers ask it from two goroutines in no fixed order.

— *Why:* the log of `api` sees the tokens of each model and not its time, so it does not tell which reader holds the pair's p50 of 6.7 s, nor whose are the ≈ 10 s of the words that `api` measured. One span for eval and the library keeps what eval measures the code's own.

— *Rejected:* **the time in the adapter, a field `provider.Response.Latency`** — made before it was seen that the runner times the call itself: to keep one measure the runner would then read the adapter's time and its stub and fixed-clock tests would change, for the same number; **the measure on `synthetic/t127/` alone** — half of the admission run, without the author's sheets, which are what the first app reads; **the admission run alone, 300 calls** — the cut-short attempts and the trial ran the same prompt on the same model, and leaving them out would only shrink the sample.

— *Consequence:* [tutor.go](../tutor.go) — `Usage.Latency`, `Usage.Retries`, `Usage.FirstToken`, the timing in `ask`; [price.go](../price.go) — `usageOf` takes the time, the retries and the first token; [tutor_test.go](../tutor_test.go) — the fake's delay and `TestAskTimes`; `docs/architecture.md`.
