**R108. Epic E7 is done and goes whole into the archive [docs/plan/E7.md](plan/E7.md); the author chooses the next epic. The text on the cost and time of a check keeps its marks: its conclusions rest on none of the facts no source holds now, and the marks point to the records.**
- **What the epic did** — all four tasks: the adapters take nothing from the environment, the hint without the work is a call kind of its own, each usage record carries its model's time, and the outside facts of the cost and time of a check have their records in `docs/sources.md`; only the effort `medium` of the words stays open (Д11). The three tasks with code each gave `api` a release.
- **The archive** — as for every done epic: the bodies of the tasks went in one by one as each was done; table 1 with the description of E7 and the opening of its section go to the end of the file, and the summary phrases of the tasks are dropped. Only the address of the link to `release.yml` was corrected.
- **The measurement** — measurements 4.1, the row of E7: its five sessions from its planning to this archive. The context of a main call is below `tutor`'s reference, the sessions after the move to the 0.16 line, so `spec`'s threshold of an analysis of the sessions' tokens is not reached; the quarterly check of Claude Code's changelog is `spec`'s.

— *Why:* the rules of RUN.md keep only what is live there.

— *Rejected:* **sources of other dates in one table, or the rows without a source dropped** — for numbers the conclusions do not use; **writing out the next epic at once** — the author chooses it; **an analysis of the sessions' tokens** — `spec`'s threshold is not reached.

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `docs/plan/E7.md`; `docs/plan/questions.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/measurements.md`; `README.md`.

## Epic E8: the words after a correct answer

**R109. Epic E8 — the words after a correct answer and `spec` `v0.16.7` — has four tasks: the move to the latest tag of `spec` first; one function of the choice of the words with the work, which holds the hint on the analysis's model; the call of the words after a correct answer — its kind, its form rule, its prompt and judge and its eval set; and the paid admission of that call with the measure of the effort `medium` for the hint. The form of these words is option (2) of the three below. The epic is done, and its archive is [docs/plan/E8.md](plan/E8.md).**
- **Why now.** `spec` decided the capability of these words in admission table 0.3, `correct_answer_words`, and the submodule carries it since `v0.16.3`. The last admission of the words cost $11.08. A paid run spends the account's prepaid credit balance, not the month's spending against the organization's limit that the console shows as «$214.83 of $300»: the credit ran out during the admission, at −$0.17, and the rest of `medium` waits (Д11).
- **The form** — option (2): exactly one question, the last sentence, and before it no more than one sentence of praise of the step; the word limit of the language as the hint's, `words.HintWords`; the letters those of the answer's language. It holds by code what `spec`'s glossary names for these words — the praise of a step and the question which technique helped; option (1) expects a question about the place of an error that does not exist, and (3) does not hold the question.
- **What `api` waits for.** `spec` puts version 3 of its privacy policy — the key, the solution and the techniques that go to the AI for these words — in force before `api` takes the call in `GetCorrectAnswerWords`. `tutor` builds and admits the call on synthetic sets meanwhile: no learner's data goes anywhere.
- **The order.** The one choice of the words with the work comes before the paid admission: the gap of the hint on another model was to close at the next entry of the words in the list of admitted models, no later.

— *Why:* the debts needed no decision of the author but the form, and a paid run had its credit.

— *Rejected:* **the admission inside the task of the call** — a paid run on code not yet reviewed, and two kinds of check in one task; **the effort `medium` as a task of its own** — one paid session of runs for both; **the eval keys in CI** — they wait for the keys in `infra`, the author's step (Д6).

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `run/DEBT.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `README.md`; `docs/plan/E8.md`.

## Move to `spec` `v0.16.7`

**R110. `spec` is at the tag `v0.16.7`, the latest on 2026-11-13; the Go modules of `spec` and of the contracts are at `v0.16.7` and `contracts/v0.16.7`, and `module-kit` is at `v0.3.2`. Rule 4 of RUN.md takes `spec`'s rule that the executor runs a task's checks without the author: the calls of AI on the stub, and a real call only in a run of eval — the words, the reading of a sheet or an assessment by a rubric —, on the eval key under the provider's spending limit the author set and within the ceiling the task names, with «Сделано» naming what was spent.**
- **What the tag brought** (`git log` and `git diff v0.16.3 v0.16.7` of the submodule; 8 commits, the tags `v0.16.4`–`v0.16.7`):
  - `spec`'s rule that the executor runs a task's checks without the author, in `spec`'s RUN.md and its template, and that a repository made before the rule takes it into its own RUN.md with its move to a tag;
  - WebKit and Firefox beside Chrome Headless Shell in the stage of the kind `typescript` — the kind `go` and the devcontainer of `spec` do not change;
  - the language of a module's `LICENSE` and `NOTICE` is the license's — an edit of the format's text, `spec/format/README.md`, without a new version of the format: `tutor` reads neither file;
  - version 3 of the privacy policy, in force before `api` takes the call of the words after a correct answer; `tutor` waits for nothing from it;
  - the contracts, admission table 0.3, the vectors and `.devcontainer/` are the same as at `v0.16.3`, and the debt table brings `tutor` no new row.
- **Rule 4 for `tutor`.** The template's text names a path in the browser with Playwright and a sign-in by a custom token, and the stub of `tutor` in the dev mode of `api`; `tutor` has no sign-in, no Playwright in a container of the kind `go`, and its own stubs — the fake adapters of the tests and `-stub` of the runner. Its one page in a browser is the board, the author's tool, which the executor checks without a browser: by its tests and by `tutor-eval board` with `-dir` outside the work tree and a dummy key, by requests to its server; `just board` takes a real key and writes to `samples/`, so the rule leaves it to the author. `spec`'s rule allows a real call "only where the task checks the model's words", and `spec` reads that as every paid run of eval: in `tutor` every paid run is eval of what a model answers, and `tutor`'s rule of paid runs already lets the executor start one within the task's ceiling. The rule of the part `eval` names the limit and what «Сделано» names too, since a session of a paid run reads it, not RUN.md, after a compaction. Devices: `tutor` has no screen of the learner's.
- **What was done:** the submodule at `v0.16.7`, commit 117be50, its release verified by `gh release verify`; `go.mod` and `go.sum` — `spec` and `spec/contracts` `v0.16.7`, and `module-kit` `v0.3.2`, verified the same way, whose release only moves it to `spec` `v0.16.3`, with Go 1.27.4; the golden log `internal/report/testdata/reading-stub.jsonl` names `v0.3.2`; CLAUDE.md, README and the two links of `.bestpractices.json` name the tag; RUN.md — the start and rule 4.

— *Why:* the tag is the latest; the submodule, the Go modules and `module-kit` move together, by the platform's rule of the submodule and as `tutor` always moves `module-kit`. `spec` asks a repository made before its rule of checks without the author to take it with its move to a tag.

— *Rejected:* **rule 4 word for word from the template** — it would name a sign-in, Playwright and a stub of `api` that `tutor` does not have, and a reader of the rule would look for them; **a real call only for the words** — the admissions of reading and of the rubric are paid runs of eval too, which the rule of paid runs allows within a ceiling; **`module-kit` at `v0.3.1`** — the release before its move to the 0.16 line, while `module-kit` moves with `spec`.

— *Consequence:* the submodule, `go.mod` and `go.sum`; `internal/report/testdata/reading-stub.jsonl`; `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `.bestpractices.json`; `RUN.md`; [the rule of the part `eval`](../.claude/rules/eval.md).

## One choice of a call kind

**R111. Every place that chooses the models of a call kind — the call itself, `Tutor.ChooseCall`, `Tutor.Price` and `Tutor.CheckKey` — chooses by one function, `chooseCall` in [tutor.go](../tutor.go). For the words with the work it holds that the hint is admitted on the analysis's model: the model `ChooseCall(CallHint)` chooses, by the words' prompt, is the analysis's first, its ID and its effort. Otherwise the words with the work are "not admitted": with the hint's refusal when the hint has no entry, and with `model_not_admitted` when its entry is another model's.**
- **What changed.** Only `Words` checked the hint, after `prepare` had chosen the analysis's entry; `ChooseCall(CallWords)`, `Price(CallWords)` and the key check took that entry as it was. With `solution_analysis` on one model and `hint` on another or on another prompt, `api`'s gateway would admit the words and reserve the quota, the call would refuse them, and the key check would ask a model the library does not call with the key. Now `prepare` takes the call kind, `prepare(Call, Route)`, and chooses by `chooseCall`, and the empty page of the workbook does too, its observations as many as the models the kind asks: no call of the library chooses by capability and prompt round it. `Choose` by capability stays for what is not a call of the library.
- **The answer of a refusal.** The hint's own, when it is not admitted — its cell, its fact —, since that is what `api` would ask `ChooseCall(CallHint)` and get; when the hint's entry is another model's, a fresh `model_not_admitted` with the table's version, as the list answers when no entry is left, not the hint's admitted answer with its flag turned.
- **What did not change.** Both entries of the words in the list are Opus 5.5 · `high` on the words' prompt, so the library's answers stayed the same.
- **The tests.** `TestWordsHintSameModel`: the analysis on Opus 5.5 · `high` and the hint on the same model, on Haiku, on Opus at `medium` and on another prompt — `ChooseCall(CallWords)`, `Price(CallWords)` and `Words` admit only the first, and `CheckKey` asks Opus only where a call would ask it: for the words, or for the hint at `medium`. With the condition taken out the test fails.

— *Why:* the gateway of `api` decides an attempt, and reserves its quota, by what the call will do: two choices that can differ make it admit what the call refuses. The gap was to close before the next entry of words in the list, no later.

— *Rejected:* **the condition in each of the four places** — the gap it fell through: a fifth place would have to remember it; **an entry of the hint of the analysis's model anywhere in the list** — `ChooseCall(CallHint)` would then serve "explain it another way" by another model than the one that words the hint with the work, and `api` would see two hints of one capability; **the condition inside the `admission` package** — it knows capabilities and prompts, not that one call serves two of them.

— *Consequence:* [tutor.go](../tutor.go) — `chooseCall`, `ChooseCall` and `prepare` by the call kind; [tutorwords.go](../tutorwords.go) — the check of the hint removed; [price.go](../price.go), [key.go](../key.go), [sheet.go](../sheet.go), [workbook.go](../workbook.go), [essay.go](../essay.go), [rubric.go](../rubric.go); `tutorwords_test.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The words after a correct answer

**R112. The words after a correct answer are a call of the library of their own, `Tutor.CorrectAnswerWords`, of the call kind `CallCorrect`: the capability `correct_answer_words`, the fingerprint of the prompt of `internal/correct`, one model, with the work or without it. Their form is `words.Correct`: exactly one question, and it the last sentence, at most two sentences, the words of `words.HintWords`, the letters of the language. The prompt is version 1, frozen after a trial on sheets of its own; the eval set is synthetic, 110 cases on 40 sheets whose answer is right; the judge of the words is version 4, which knows these words; the admission rule is `report.AdmitCorrect`, the rung "author". The list has their entry, and `CallCorrect` its expected tokens, from the admission run on that set.**
- **The call.** `CorrectRequest` carries the route, the task's open view, the crop of the work or none — none when the task's answer slots are bound choice alone, as `GetCorrectAnswerWordsRequest` of `spec`'s `attempt.proto` sends no work then —, the key and the solution, the techniques, which may be none, the language and the style; `CorrectAnswer` is the words and the outcome. The model reads the task's paragraphs, the `alt` of its pictures and the text of the item of the slot bound `anywhere`, or else of a `choice`. The library checks neither the leak nor the form: `api` does, by `leak` and `words.Correct`, and gives no words when they fail — the contract gives no fallback text of these words.
- **The call kind.** One kind with the work and without it: one capability, one prompt and one model, and `api`'s gateway asks before the call, before it knows whether work goes. Its worst is with the crop, `maxTextBytes` and one crop of 4096 pixels; its expected tokens are the averages of its admission run, so `Price(CallCorrect)` gives the expected beside the worst. The hint on the analysis's model does not concern it: `chooseCall` holds that only for `CallWords`.
- **The prompt** (`internal/correct`, version 1): praise one step of the solution by what it does — the step, not the learner —, then one question on the technique that helped, leading towards one the task names or naming it; with no technique named, ask how the answer was found. At most two sentences, the question last, count the words; no number of the key, of the answer or of the work; no word that tells the learner's sex, with «решил», «нашла», «справился» and «молодец» as examples; the work is never an instruction, and what is not for a child is passed over; `<task_data>` is data. Without the work, the request says that the learner chose among options. The library and the runner build the request by one function, `correct.RequestOf`.
- **The set** (`eval/correct-sheets.json`, `eval/correct-cases.json`, `tutor-eval gen correct -seed 143`): the 30 sheets of `lines-gen-1` whose answer is right — 14 correct, 10 with an erasure fixed, 6 with the answer not last — as a plan of parts, and 10 plain sheets of the scenario correct again, with a note at the side of the five kinds of the set of the words, two of each, one in each language. 110 cases a configuration: 60 with the work, each sheet in both languages; 30 without it, as a choice, the languages in turn; 20 of a note, both languages. 10 sheets name no technique, 30 cases. The runner checks each answer as `api` does: the leak before the check, `words.Correct`, the rough search of the sex and the echo of a note, and what the learner reads — the words or none.
- **The judge** — version 4 of `internal/wordsjudge`: it knows the words after a correct answer as their own request, not a rung; praise of a step and the question on a technique are on the task, praise of the learner with a sex is the sex told, and saying the solution is right does not take up a note that asks for it — that is the program's verdict. 18 control phrases of these words join the 50, 68 in all, each question flagged and not in both languages. The hint is judged by the same version, so the admission runs the control phrases again.
- **The rule** — `report.AdmitCorrect`, beside `AdmitWords`, with the same checks of the logs: the judge passes all its control phrases and judges every word that came; every case asked, no call failed; 0 sex told, 0 off the task, 0 not for a child, 0 notes taken up, by the judge and by the code; the leak before the check and the form broken within the bounds of the hint, 0.010 and 0.016. There is no repeat of a question asked: these words ask none before. `tutor-eval admit-words` takes the kind from `call_kind` of the logs, all of one kind. A synthetic set admits these words at the rung "author" — an exception to the rule that synthetic sheets give no admission, for words no sheet of the author has.
- **The trial** (measurements 3.17): 28 cases of a set drawn the same way from `lines-gen-2`, by Opus 5.5 · `high`, and the judge Opus 5.5 · `high` on them: 28 words came and were shown, 0 leaks, 0 forms broken, 0 sex told by the rough search or the judge, 0 notes taken up; the prompt needed no edit. 56 calls, $0.55, at Anthropic.

— *Why:* the capability is `spec`'s and the form the author's; one call kind keeps the gateway's question one, before the work is known. The rule of the hint is the bar because there is no version 1 of these words to be no worse than, and the words go to the learner after the same checks. The trial on other sheets keeps the set of admission unseen by the prompt's edits, as the admission of the words did for the hint.

— *Rejected:* **two call kinds, with the work and without** — two entries of one capability and one prompt, and a gateway that cannot tell which before the call; **the words in `Words` as a rung 0** — `spec` makes them a call of their own; **no call without techniques** — a task that names none would get no words, and the general question serves it; **a fallback text** — the contract gives none; **the author's sheets in the set** — the set stays code's, drawn again by its seed; **a rule of its own for leaks and the form** — no measurement to set it by.

— *Consequence:* [correct.go](../correct.go), [price.go](../price.go) — `CallCorrect`; [words/words.go](../words/words.go) — `Correct`, `CorrectForm`, `Last`; `internal/correct`, `internal/gen/correct.go`, `internal/report/correct.go` and `AdmitCorrect`, `internal/wordsjudge` version 4; `cmd/tutor-eval` — `gen correct`, `run -kind correct`, `judge-words` and `admit-words` on these logs; `eval/correct-sheets.json`, `eval/correct-cases.json`, `eval/words-judge.json`; `correct_test.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The admission of the words after a correct answer

**R113. The words after a correct answer are admitted: Opus 5.5 · `high` on version 1 of the prompt of `internal/correct` passed `report.AdmitCorrect` at the rung "author", on the 110 synthetic cases of their set, with the judge Opus 5.5 · `high` on version 4 of the judge's prompt, which passed all 68 control phrases first. The list has the entry `correct_answer_words`, and `CallCorrect` has expected tokens. The effort `medium` of the hint is measured in part — 136 of 300 hints, by code, without the judge —, since the account's credit ran out; the rest waits, and Д11 keeps it.** The run was only a measurement of `medium`, with no change of the entries of the words, under a ceiling of $12.
- **The runs** (measurements 3.18). First the judge's control phrases of version 4, one run for both admissions: 68 of 68 right. Then `run -kind correct -part all -cases eval/correct-cases.json` on `synthetic/correct-v1`, Opus 5.5 · `high`, one repeat: 110 words came and were shown, 0 leaks before the check, 0 forms broken by `words.Correct`, 0 sex told by the rough search; the judge on each: 0 sex told, 0 off the task, 0 not for a child, 0 notes taken up of 20; the code: 0 echo of a note. The rule gave "author".
- **The entry** — in [admission/models.go](../admission/models.go): `correct_answer_words`, one model, Opus 5.5 · `high`, the rung "author", the evidence `synthetic/t144/correct.admit.md` with its sha256 and the date 2026-11-14, and the fingerprint of `internal/correct`, `ee01e82a…`. Unlike the entries of the words, its evidence lies in `synthetic/`: the set is code's, and `samples/` holds only the author's handwriting. A seed draws the sheets again, but not the model's answers: the logs and the output of `synthetic/t144/` are kept, like those of the runs before them in `synthetic/`. `TestAdmitted` holds one model and the fingerprint of this build, and `TestPrice` the expected price of the list's own entry.
- **The expected tokens** of `CallCorrect` in [price.go](../price.go) — Opus 5.5 · `high` 1,371 of input and 402 of output, the averages of the 110 calls, so `Price(CallCorrect)` gives the expected beside the worst, as `CallWords` does. The average is of the set's mix, 20 of the 110 with a note, which take the most: a call without a note takes 1,362 and 386 with the work and 1,206 and 407 without it, so the expected is a little above most calls, as a reservation of a quota should be.
- **The hint at `medium`.** 150 cases of the set of the words at `synthetic/words-v1` and the 150 of the author's sheets were to run. The account's prepaid credit ran out after 136: Anthropic refused the other 164, «Your credit balance is too low», at no cost. The console's «$214.83 of $300» is the month's spending against a spending limit, and the money is the credit balance. On the same 136 cases `medium` answers 0.8 s sooner at p50 than `high` at the admission of the words, 4.4 s against 5.2 s, and 1.0 s at p90; by code 0 leaks and 1 form broken. The judge read none of them, and of the author's sheets none ran, so the rule of words says nothing of `medium`, and the entries of the words stay at `high`.
- **Money** — 424 calls at Anthropic, $4.81 of the ceiling of $12: the control phrases $0.56, the words $1.49, their judge $0.72, the hints at `medium` $2.04.

— *Why:* `api` answers `GetCorrectAnswerWords` with "not admitted" until the list has an entry, and the rule, its set and its judge were frozen before this run, so the admission is by targets set before the data. The time of the hint is what Д11 asks, and the 136 hints give its direction at no extra cost.

— *Rejected:* **finishing `medium` after a top-up** — the words at `high` are admitted, and the first app does not wait for the effort; **catching up the 14 refused cases into the same rule** — a failed call is a failure for the rule, and without the judge and the author's sheets the rule cannot pass anyway; **switching the words to `medium` on these numbers** — no judge and no author's sheets, and the hint on the analysis's model would need the analysis's entry to switch with the hint's.

— *Consequence:* [admission/models.go](../admission/models.go) — the entry; [price.go](../price.go) and [correct_test.go](../correct_test.go) — the expected tokens of `CallCorrect`; [cmd/tutor-eval/main.go](../cmd/tutor-eval/main.go) — `correct` in the usage of `run`; `docs/architecture.md`; `README.md`.

## The archive of epic E8

**R114. Epic E8 is done and goes whole into the archive [docs/plan/E8.md](plan/E8.md); the author chooses the next epic.**
- **What the epic did** — all four tasks: `tutor` is at `spec` `v0.16.7` and `module-kit` `v0.3.2`; one function, `chooseCall`, holds the hint on the analysis's model for every choice of the words with the work; the words after a correct answer are a call of their own, `CorrectAnswerWords`, with their form rule, prompt, set and judge, and are admitted — Opus 5.5 · `high`, the rung "author". The effort `medium` of the hint is measured in part: the account's credit ran out, and the rest waits (Д11).
- **The archive** — as for every done epic: the bodies of the tasks went in one by one as each was done; table 1 with the description of E8 and the opening of its section go to the end of the file, and the summary phrases of the tasks are dropped. The moved text has no relative links.
- **The measurement** — measurements 4.1, the row of E8: five sessions from its planning to this archive. The context of a main call is below `tutor`'s reference, the sessions after the move to the 0.16 line, so `spec`'s threshold of an analysis of the sessions' tokens is not reached.

— *Why:* the rules of RUN.md keep only what is live there.

— *Rejected:* **writing out the next epic at once** — the author chooses it; **an analysis of the sessions' tokens** — `spec`'s threshold is not reached; **keeping E8 open for the rest of `medium`** — it is not needed now, and Д11 keeps it.

— *Consequence:* `RUN.md`; `docs/plan/E8.md`; `docs/measurements.md`; `README.md`.

## Epic E9: the move to `spec` `v0.18.3`

**R115. The move of `spec` to the tag `v0.18.3`, the latest on 2026-11-16, with the Go modules of `spec` and of the contracts at `v0.18.3` and `contracts/v0.18.3`, and `module-kit` at `v0.3.3`, its own move to that tag. The move is epic E9 of its own, in three tasks: the tag with the devcontainer, `.claude/settings.json` and table 3; the layout and the language of the plan; CI on the release `v0.1.24` of `.github`. The plugin `gopls-lsp` loads by `CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_DIRS` of the devcontainer, not by a marketplace in the shared `~/.claude`, and the container has the name `tutor` in its network. `spec` recorded every row of table 3, and they left it.** The move came from `spec`'s orchestrator, whose first wave moves each repository of an epic to the latest tag of `spec` and asks for a split when the move is more than one review; with no current epic, a task that comes from no epic is the first task of the next, so it began E9.
- **What the tag brought** (`git -C spec log --oneline v0.16.7..v0.18.3`, 69 commits; `grep -n '`tutor`' spec/run/DEBT.md`; `spec/RULES.md`; `spec/run/INBOX.md`):
  - `spec/RULES.md`: «Сделай эпик E…» runs an epic across repositories — its executor commits, pushes and opens the pull request by `spec/tools/orchestrator/executor.md`, and the orchestrator merges it on green checks; the plan's rules and tables are in English, terse, for the session, in `run/`, with the bodies of tasks in Russian, translated with the move to a tag that brings this layout;
  - the template, by `just new-repo-draft tutor go 3.6 v0.18.3` in a clone of `spec` at the tag in a scratch folder: the base image `devcontainers/base` 3.0.13-noble by its digest instead of 3.0.12 — the registry gives the same digest, `sha256:48be4ade…` —, the Dockerfile's opening comment on `spec`'s stage `orchestrator`, which a copy leaves out; `--network-alias=tutor` after the repository's own network; `CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_DIRS` in `containerEnv` and `.claude/settings.json` without `extraKnownMarketplaces` and `enabledPlugins`; RUN.md, CLAUDE.md, README.md and the files of `run/` in the plan's new layout; Go, gopls and its lock file, AGENTS.md and `host-network.sh` are as before;
  - `spec`'s inbox recorded every row of table 3, each with the fingerprint of its row on `main` of `tutor`, 680bd43; the answers are in [plan/questions.md](plan/questions.md);
  - the admission table 0.4 and `tutor`'s card in `spec`: the cells of platform AI of `adult` at Anthropic and at OpenAI are open with `data_processing_agreement` alone, under the provider's usual retention, and on `novatrail` the rung is "author" for adults, "full" the target of quality, not a condition; the code takes both from the deployment and does not change;
  - three rows of `spec`'s cross-repository plan ask new work of `tutor`: the admission entries of `CallWorkbook`, `CallEssay` and `CallRubric`, as an epic once the author opens it; the date access returns to an own key in `Outcome.Limit` at Anthropic; `-race` on fewer packages and the Go cache of the job "Canvas" (measurements 3.1 spec). They are not the move, and went to table 4 as debts;
  - `module-kit` `v0.3.3`, released while the move ran: its own move to `spec` `v0.18.3` and the plan's new layout, its `go.mod` requiring `spec` and `spec/contracts` `v0.18.3` (the module proxy's `.mod`) — `v0.3.2` required `v0.16.3`;
  - the rest does not concern `tutor` or is held already: the format is still 0.4; `tutor` has no npm package of `spec`; buf in CLAUDE.md is held already; the effort `medium` of the hint's words waits for Д11; the image from the cache and one group of Dependabot are the move of CI; the card's other changes are in lines of other components.
- **What the move changed:**
  - `spec/` — at `v0.18.3`, commit 922886a, its release verified by `gh release verify`; `go.mod` and `go.sum` — `spec` and `spec/contracts` `v0.18.3`, the hashes of their `go.mod` the same, and `module-kit` `v0.3.3`, its release verified the same way; the golden log `internal/report/testdata/reading-stub.jsonl` names it; the tag in CLAUDE.md, the start of RUN.md and the two links to `spec/RULES.md` in `.bestpractices.json` — `contribution_requirements_justification` and `test_policy_justification`; [architecture.md](architecture.md) names no tag;
  - `.devcontainer/` — the template's output, with `tutor`'s own differences added again: golangci-lint and the comment on `buf` and the contracts' image; `.claude/settings.json` — the template's, byte for byte;
  - the comments of `admission.Stage`, `Table.Select` and `tutor.Config` — the rung by the deployment, not "only where one author learns"; a test of `admission` — an adult's platform AI open with the agreement alone; [architecture.md](architecture.md), where it describes admission to AI;
  - CLAUDE.md — the plugin by `CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_DIRS`; RUN.md's rule on commits — the orchestrator's run as the author's word, as `spec/RULES.md` has it;
  - RUN.md — epic E9 in table 1, the bodies of its tasks going to [plan/E9.md](plan/E9.md); table 3 without its rows, which went word for word to [plan/questions.md](plan/questions.md); [DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md) — the debts of the three new rows.
- **The epic and the three rows:** the plan's layout and CI were done the same day, and E9 is in its archive, [plan/E9.md](plan/E9.md); `-race` and the Go cache of "Canvas" became epic E10, in [plan/E10.md](plan/E10.md); the date access returns is `Outcome.AccessReturns`; the admission entries of the workbook, the essay and the rubric became epic E12. The three debts are closed.
- **After the merge** the author rebuilds the container — the base image, the network alias and the plugin by its path —, and once all six repositories have moved, runs `claude plugin marketplace remove novatrail`. The merge releases the next patch of `tutor` with the same behaviour.

— *Why:* the repository stands on the latest tag of `spec`, and the submodule and the Go modules move together, so that code and the verdicts eval counts come from the tag `tutor` stands on. The move is an epic of its own because a task that comes from no epic is the first task of the next one and table 2 held none, as `sim` did for the same reason. The plugin moves now, since a marketplace in the shared `~/.claude` holds one container's path and loses the others' gopls. The plan's layout and its translation rewrite RUN.md and `DEBT.md`, add four files and weigh `run/TASKS.md` against `tutor`'s rule of no tasks outside an epic, and a review of a translation is longer than the move: a task of its own, as in `learner-model` and `sim`. Until then the old layout passes: `docs-check` of `v0.18.3` still reads table 3 in RUN.md and table 4 in `DEBT.md`, and `spec/RULES.md` lets table 3 stay in RUN.md until the move. CI is the next task, as in `sim`.

— *Rejected:* **`module-kit` at `v0.3.2`** — it requires `spec` `v0.16.3`, and the verdicts that eval counts would come from a library of an older tag than the one `tutor` stands on; **the whole move in one task** — more than one review; **the tag alone, without an epic** — `tutor` has no task outside an epic, and the platform lets a repository keep that rule of its own; **the new rows of `spec`'s cross-repository plan in this epic** — new work of `tutor`, not the move, and the composition of an epic is the author's; **the keys of the marketplace kept in `.claude/settings.json`** — `new-repo-check` refuses them, and a session that has them may overwrite the entry `novatrail` of the shared `~/.claude`; **the group `shared-workflows` of Dependabot in the first task** — the move of CI raises the same references and takes it with them.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `admission/models.go`; `admission/admission_test.go`; `tutor.go`; `internal/report/testdata/reading-stub.jsonl`; `docs/architecture.md`; `.devcontainer/`; `.claude/settings.json`; `CLAUDE.md`; `.bestpractices.json`; `RUN.md`; `run/DEBT.md`; `docs/plan/E9.md`; `docs/plan/questions.md`.

## The work plan in `run/` and in English

**R116. The work plan takes the layout and the language of `spec`'s plan: tables 2, 3 and 4 are `run/EPICS.md`, `run/QUESTIONS.md` and `run/DEBT.md`, which replaces `DEBT.md` at the root, each opening with its rules and its next free number, in English. RUN.md keeps its rules, in English and with their numbers, its section Epics and table 1; the epic's description, table 1 and the bodies of tasks stay in Russian. A done task's gist sentence goes to the archive with its body. There is no `run/TASKS.md`: a task that comes from no epic stands in table 1, in the current epic or as the first task of the next.** The template, `just new-repo-draft tutor go 3.6 v0.18.3` on a clone of `spec` at the tag in a scratch folder, gives the layout, and `module-kit` took the same.
- **From the template:** the openings and headings of the three files — «Table 2. Epics for later», «Table 3. Questions to the author», «Table 4. Technical debt» —, with the rows translated and holding only what is open. RUN.md's opening, Rules and Epics: the rules keep their numbers, so "rule 4", "rule 9" and "rule 12" in older texts still name them, and «Как пользоваться» in older texts is Rules, «Как устроен план» and «Как устроен план: эпики» are Epics. The rule of a done task: the gist sentence goes to the archive with the body, an unheaded item at the start of the receiving section. CLAUDE.md's list of documents names RUN.md and `run/`.
- **What differs from the template:**
  - the rule of what to read first reads the task as RUN.md holds it — its row of table 1 and its body in the epic's section —, with no `run/TASKS.md` to look in;
  - the rule of what waits for the author's confirmation is `tutor`'s: the board checked without a browser, the calls of AI on the stub, a real call only in an eval run on the key of `~/.config/tutor/anthropic.key` within the task's ceiling, nothing on devices;
  - the rules of the devcontainer and of external facts stay pointers to `spec/RULES.md`; the rule of the checks names the rules of the parts for code; the rule of closing the findings of SonarCloud, CodeQL and Scorecard holds, as they run here;
  - table 1 may hold two epics, and Epics keeps only the search by heading;
  - `run/QUESTIONS.md` keeps [plan/questions.md](plan/questions.md): an answered question's row leaves, and a row «В реестр spec» that `spec` recorded leaves verbatim to «Переданы в spec» there, its answer to «Закрыты», where the template only deletes the row; `run/EPICS.md` keeps the rule of an epic put off with its tasks in its archive.
- **The history** the openings held — the list «Сделанные эпики» of RUN.md, the opening of table 3, the opening of `DEBT.md` with its paragraphs of the closed debts, and the open rows with their dated notes — went verbatim to [plan/E9.md](plan/E9.md), with the gist sentence of the move's first task. An archive is found by its file, `docs/plan/<epic>.md`.
- **Links:** the links to `DEBT.md` in this log, in [architecture.md](architecture.md) and in the archives [plan/E6.md](plan/E6.md), [plan/E7.md](plan/E7.md), [plan/E8.md](plan/E8.md) and [plan/questions.md](plan/questions.md) lead to `run/DEBT.md`, their text otherwise untouched, as `spec` did with its own archives; the rule `ci` names `run/DEBT.md`. A link to a row closed before the move leads to `run/DEBT.md`, which holds it no more: its closing paragraph is in [plan/E9.md](plan/E9.md), which the opening of `run/DEBT.md` names. "Table 3 of RUN.md", "table 4" and `DEBT.md` in older archives read through RUN.md's opening, which names each table's file.

— *Why:* the tag brought the layout, and `docs-check` and `just inbox` read the old layout and the Russian headings only until the last repository has moved. Every task reads RUN.md, and a task that cites a row reads its file of `run/`: RUN.md went from 23,402 bytes to 13,322, and table 4 from 12,661 bytes in `DEBT.md` to 4,312 in `run/DEBT.md`. English is cheaper in tokens than Russian for the same text, and the plan's reader is the session.

— *Rejected:*
- **`run/TASKS.md`, empty, by the template** — a file no task here fills, and a rule that changes `tutor`'s rule of no tasks outside an epic without a reason of `tutor`'s own. `spec` moves the bodies out of RUN.md because there they pile up between epics; here such a task is the first task of the next epic, and the platform lets a repository keep a rule that makes it a task of an epic;
- **translating the bodies of tasks or the archives** — the platform keeps them in Russian, for the author;
- **deleting the history of the openings** — older archives cite the closed rows;
- **dropping [plan/questions.md](plan/questions.md) from the rules of table 3** — it is where the answer to a question is found.

— *Consequence:* `run/EPICS.md`; `run/QUESTIONS.md`; `run/DEBT.md`; `RUN.md`; `CLAUDE.md`; `.bestpractices.json`; `.claude/rules/ci.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/plan/E6.md`; `docs/plan/E7.md`; `docs/plan/E8.md`; `docs/plan/E9.md`; `docs/plan/questions.md`.

## CI on the release `v0.1.24` of `.github`

**R117. The move of CI to the release `v0.1.24` of `.github`, its latest on 2026-11-16: every `uses:` of `.github/workflows/` calls one release of the shared workflows, by the SHA of its tag, fab102d — `ci.yml`, `codeql.yml` and `scorecard.yml` were at `v0.1.15`, `release.yml` at `v0.1.11`. The jobs of `tutor`'s own, "Samples" and "Canvas", take the image of the stage `toolchain` from the cache of GitHub Actions, the entry the jobs of `go.yml` use, and build it only on a miss. Dependabot raises every reference to `.github` in one pull request, the group `shared-workflows`. The input `same-bits` of `go.yml` stays off. Epic E9 is done and goes into its archive.**
- **What `v0.1.24` changes for `tutor`** (`gh release view v0.1.24 -R altnovatrail/.github` — immutable, published 2026-11-13; `git diff v0.1.15 v0.1.24` and `git diff v0.1.11 v0.1.24` of `.github/workflows/` in a clone of `.github` in a scratch folder):
  - `go.yml` — every job in the stage takes its image from the cache: `actions/cache/restore` and `actions/cache/save` v6.1.0, the key `toolchain-<OS>-<architecture>-<sha256 of .devcontainer/Dockerfile>`; the cache of `main` is filled by CI on a push to `main`, and a pull request reads it. The new input `same-bits`, false by default, adds "Go / targets", "Go / fma-diff", "Go / arm64" and "Go / rules". Without it the jobs, their names, the permissions and `ALLOWED_LICENSES` are those of `v0.1.15`;
  - `documents.yml` — the stage by `spec/tools/toolchain/toolchain.sh` of the submodule, its image from the cache by `TOOLCHAIN_IMAGE_FILE`, which the script reads from `spec` `v0.13.0`; `codeql.yml` and `scorecard.yml` — `github/codeql-action` v4.39.3 instead of v4.39.0; `secrets.yml` and `dco.yml` — the same bytes;
  - `release.yml` — the input `previous-file`, which `tutor` does not set; before publishing, a check that the caller's workflow is `.github/workflows/release.yml` on `main` and pins the shared one by a commit's SHA, which `tutor`'s does, on `workflow_run` of CI on `main` and on the button; `timeout-minutes` 45 instead of 30; the recipes of `spec` by `toolchain.sh`. `release-verify` accepts the signature of the shared `release.yml` at the SHA of a release of `.github` `v0.1.7` or newer, and `v0.1.24` is one;
  - the names of the checks are the same, among them the eleven that the ruleset of `main` requires — "Secrets / gitleaks", "DCO / sign-off", "Documents / docs-check-repo", "Go / lint", "Go / test", "Go / vuln", "Go / licenses", the two of CodeQL, SonarCloud and `codecov/patch` (`gh api repos/altnovatrail/tutor/rules/branches/main`).
- **"Samples" and "Canvas"** need the submodule, which `go.yml` does not check out, so they stay jobs of `tutor`'s own. An entry of the cache is its path's as well as its key's, so the file one job saves the others load, and a save that fails costs the run its speed, not its verdict. The rule `ci` names how each takes the image: a move to a newer release of `.github` compares a copy with that release's `go.yml`. Dependabot's pull request of the group compares nothing, but a copy that drifts from `go.yml` builds and saves an entry of its own, so it costs time, not a verdict.
- **What holds now:** the release in force is the one every `uses:` of `.github/workflows/` names; "Samples" and "Canvas" take the one image `novatrail-toolchain` from the file `novatrail-toolchain.tar` that "Go / toolchain" and "Documents" save — "Samples" by a copy of the image steps of that release's `go.yml`, byte for byte, saving nothing, "Canvas" with the caches of Go by `.github`'s composite action `toolchain`; "CodeQL / analyze (go)" takes Go from the archive the Dockerfile names, without the image; the release builds the stage `toolchain-core`.
- **Checked:** `actionlint` 1.7.12 with `shellcheck` 0.11.0, by the versions and sums of `self-ci.yml` of `.github` `v0.1.24`, over every workflow — no findings; `ci-lint`, `ci-test`, `ci-vuln` and `ci-licenses` in a clean clone with `env -i` and `ALLOWED_LICENSES` of `go.yml` `v0.1.24`; the checks of pull request #173, green, their names those of before.
- **Epic E9** — the tag of `spec` `v0.18.3`, the plan in `run/` and in English, CI on `.github` `v0.1.24`. Its description and table 1 go to [plan/E9.md](plan/E9.md), and table 1 of RUN.md reads «Current epic — none»; the row of its sessions is measurements 4.1.

— *Why:* every job built the image from the network, so an outage of nodejs.org, of GitHub or of the snapshot of Ubuntu's archive failed the checks while the Dockerfile stayed the same. One release in every file is `tutor`'s rule for its CI, and the files held two: `release.yml` at `v0.1.11`, the rest at `v0.1.15`. With the group, Dependabot opens one pull request for a new release of `.github` instead of one for each reference, each of which would need its own round of checks.

— *Rejected:* **`same-bits: true`** — the platform holds the same bits only for the libraries whose numbers must be the same on every build, `learner-model` and `engine`, and the bench of `sim`; `tutor`'s numbers are the models' answers and the verdicts of `module-kit`, and four more jobs would add only time; **"Samples" and "Canvas" by `toolchain.sh build`** — they would share the entry of "Documents", not that of `go.yml`'s four jobs, and the steps of the cache would be copied all the same; **one job of a matrix, or a YAML anchor, for the two** — the steps would no longer be `go.yml`'s byte for byte, which the comparison on a move reads by a diff; **leaving them to build the image** — an outage of the network would still fail them; **a row «В реестр spec» for a shared step of the image** — `spec`'s cross-repository plan asked it of `.github` already.

— *Consequence:* `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `.github/workflows/codeql.yml`; `.github/workflows/scorecard.yml`; `.github/workflows/release.yml`; `.github/dependabot.yml`; `.claude/rules/ci.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `RUN.md`; `docs/plan/E9.md`; `docs/measurements.md`.

**R118. `ci-test` runs the race detector only on the packages whose code or tests start goroutines — the root, `internal/board`, `provider/claude` and `provider/gpt`, the list `RACE` of the justfile —, and the other packages without it, `cmd/tutor-eval` and `internal/evalsample` among them, in a second `go test`, into the same profile `coverage/go.out`. `TestRaceStartsGoroutines` holds `RACE` to the code both ways. With the caches of Go of "Canvas" and the stage `toolchain-core`, which came with later releases of `.github` and a later tag of `spec`, it is epic E10, done and in its archive [plan/E10.md](plan/E10.md).** It answers the row of `spec`'s cross-repository plan on the speed of CI, which asks for the detector only where goroutines are; with no current epic, the task was the first of the next.
- **Who starts goroutines** (`go list ./...`, every file of each package, tests among them): the library's calls to the models, `ask` of `tutor.go` by `sync.WaitGroup.Go`; the board's checks, `internal/board/server.go`, the same; the tests of the adapters on servers of `httptest`, whose handlers run on goroutines of their own. No other package has a `go` statement, a method `Go` or a server of `httptest`. `cmd/tutor-eval` serves the board by `http.Server`, but its tests give `runBoard` a `serve` of their own and start no server.
- **What the detector still watches:** every goroutine the module starts, with the tests that drive it — the readers of a pair on fakes in the root, the board's tries, the adapters' requests and streams. What it no longer watches: the parallel tests of `cmd/tutor-eval` beside one another. They share only `drawnOf`, behind `sync.Once`, and files they read; `checkSamples`, the package's one variable that tests substitute, is substituted only by sequential tests, which Go runs before the parallel ones, and the detector found no race there in any run.
- **The guard.** `TestRaceStartsGoroutines`, in `tutor_test.go`, parses every Go file of every package of the module, those under build constraints too, and fails on a package that starts a goroutine and is not in `RACE`, on one in `RACE` that starts none, and on a name in `RACE` that is no package. A start is a `go` statement; a method `Go` or `TryGo`; `AfterFunc`; a server of `httptest`, by the name its file imports it under; `Serve` or `ListenAndServe`; and `t.Parallel`. `cmd/tutor-eval` may start two kinds outside `RACE`, which the test names: its parallel tests and `ListenAndServe` of `serveHTTP`, which no test runs. What the guard does not see: a goroutine the standard library starts for the code, as `os/exec` does, and a test of a package outside `RACE` that drives the goroutines of one in it — none does now: `cmd/tutor-eval` takes `internal/board` for the command `board`, and its tests ask the board for its models and plan, not for a check, which runs the models side by side. Such a test goes to the package of the goroutines, under the detector.
- **The time** — measurements 5.1: locally on four cores with an empty `GOCACHE`, 716 s before and 359 s after, the same coverage, 95.5 %, the same in each package.
- **The rest of the epic** waited for a release of `.github` and a tag of `spec`, since it changes `.devcontainer/`, which nobody rebuilds during an unattended run of the orchestrator: "Canvas" takes the caches of Go under a key of its own and the jobs of `go.yml` take theirs, and the release and the checks without browsers and Go tests run in the stage `toolchain-core`.

— *Why:* under the detector `cmd/tutor-eval` and `internal/evalsample` drew their samples seven to thirteen times slower (measurements 3.1 spec), and `internal/workbook`, `internal/report`, `internal/synth`, `internal/render` and `internal/essay` took 33–63 s each for the same reason, with nothing concurrent to watch. A list held to the code both ways keeps the check what it was as the code changes, where a list of exceptions would silently leave a new package that starts goroutines to the detector or not.

— *Rejected:* **only `cmd/tutor-eval` and `internal/evalsample` without the detector** — tried first: 427 s instead of 359, with five more packages under the detector that start no goroutine; **smaller sets of pictures under the build tag `race`** (the other option `spec`'s plan named) — the tests under the detector would check other samples than those without it, and the golden samples of `internal/evalsample/testdata` would need a second set; **the two runs side by side** — four cores already run the packages of one `go test` in parallel, and the logs would interleave; the dependencies are compiled twice, with the detector and without, and the cache of Go is what saves that; **two profiles for Codecov** — `go.yml` sends the one file `coverage/go.out`; **dropping `-timeout 15m`** — it costs nothing and still stops a hung test before the job's 20 minutes.

— *Consequence:* `justfile`; `tutor_test.go`; `.claude/rules/go.md`; `.claude/rules/ci.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `.bestpractices.json`; `docs/measurements.md`.

## When access returns after a limit

**R119. An outcome "limit reached" says when access returns, where the provider's terms say it: `Outcome.AccessReturns`, a `time.Time`, zero where they do not. Today only the cap of Anthropic's tier says it — 429 `enforced_spend_limit_reached` lets the key go at 00:00 UTC on the first day of the next month —, and the month is the one the refusal fell on by the provider's clock, the response's header `Date`, or the service's where the header is missing or not a date. The owner's own spend limit names the time only in the words of its message, which are not parsed, and has none.** It answers `spec`'s cross-repository plan, which asks `tutor` for the time an own key's access returns, on the provider's terms (sources 2.8 spec).
- **What it builds:** `provider.Refusal` and `provider.LimitError` carry `Returns`; `provider.NoRetry` gives the function that reads a refusal the time the provider answered, from `Date` by `http.ParseTime`, or `time.Now`; `claude.Refusal` gives the cap of the tier `Returns` — the first of the next month in UTC, December's to January —, and `gpt.Refusal` takes the time and gives none; `ask` carries the time of the refusal the outcome takes into `Outcome.AccessReturns`, and of two limits of a pair takes the one that holds the call longer — one with no time, then the later —, so that the date `api` tells the owner is not one at which the other reader is still refused; `LimitError`'s text names the time, for the logs. [architecture.md](architecture.md) says it in detail, where it describes the spending limit of an own key.
- **What `api` does with it:** `access_returns` of `OwnKeyLimit` (`api/v1` of `spec`), once it takes a release of `tutor` that has it. Such a release comes with the merge, since the change is in `*.go`, which `paths` of `release.yml` names.

— *Why:* `api` cannot tell the key's owner when to come back without it: the cap of the tier is no limit the owner can lift — only Anthropic can raise it, or the month can turn —, and a refusal that says "the key works again on 1 December" saves the owner raising a limit that is not theirs. The provider's clock decides the month, not the service's: a service whose clock is a few seconds off at the end of a month would otherwise send the owner a month ahead or tell them it had already returned.

— *Rejected:* **reading the time from the words of the owner's limit**, "You will regain access on …" — `spec`'s plan says not to, and the words of a message are no contract: a change of them would give the owner a wrong date rather than none; **the date the message of the tier's cap names** — the same words, and the terms name the time already; **the service's clock alone** — wrong by a month at the turn of one when the clocks differ; **a pointer or a separate flag for "no time"** — the zero `time.Time` says it, as the proto's comment of `access_returns` reads, «only when the provider says so»; **a time on `Tutor.CheckKey`** — the check answers "accepted" for a key refused for a limit, and `spec`'s `OwnKeyCheck` is an enum of its ends, with no time.

— *Consequence:* `provider/refusal.go`; `provider/claude/claude.go`; `provider/gpt/gpt.go`; `tutor.go`; their tests; `docs/architecture.md`.

## CI on the release `v0.1.30` of `.github`

**R120. The move of CI to the release `v0.1.30` of `.github`, by the SHA of its tag, 8311cbf, in every `uses:` of `.github/workflows/`: the jobs that run Go take the caches of Go from the cache of GitHub Actions; "Samples" and "Canvas" take the image `novatrail-toolchain` from the file `novatrail-toolchain.tar`, the one entry that the job "Go / toolchain" and "Documents" save on a push to `main`; "CodeQL / analyze (go)" builds no image. "Samples" builds the image on a miss by a copy of the image steps of `go.yml` and saves nothing. "Canvas" takes the image and the caches of Go, under the check `canvas` in the key, by `.github`'s composite action `toolchain`: on a miss of the image it builds it, and a push to `main` saves the image and the caches. The stage `toolchain-core` came with the next release, `v0.1.31`, and a tag of `spec`.**
- **What `v0.1.30` changes for `tutor`** (`gh release view v0.1.30 -R altnovatrail/.github` — immutable, published 2026-11-16, `gh release verify` passes; `git diff v0.1.24 v0.1.30 -- .github/workflows/` in a clone of `.github` in a scratch folder):
  - `go.yml` — the jobs that run Go take `GOMODCACHE`, `GOCACHE` and `GOLANGCI_LINT_CACHE` from the cache, one folder mounted at `/go-cache`, by a key of the OS, the architecture, the check, the hash of the Dockerfile and that of the `go.sum` files, checked by `go mod verify` in every module, their builds touched; only a push to `main` saves. The image is `novatrail-toolchain`, the file `novatrail-toolchain.tar`, and a new job "Go / toolchain" looks the entry up and on a miss alone builds the image for the four jobs after it, by the artifact `go-toolchain-image`; only a push to `main` saves. "Go / toolchain" holds no merge. With `same-bits`, off here, a matrix of targets;
  - `documents.yml` — the same file and image, saved only on a push to `main`; `typescript.yml` — the input `engines`, which `tutor` does not call;
  - `codeql.yml` — the analysis of `go` takes go.dev's archive by `ARG GO_VERSION` and `GO_SHA256_AMD64` of the stage `toolchain`, checks its sha256 and `go version`, and stops on another form of the Dockerfile or on a `GO*` or `CGO_*` in the stage's `ENV` but `GOTOOLCHAIN`. `tutor`'s Dockerfile has both `ARG`s once each, and its `ENV` sets `PATH` and `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` alone, so it passes;
  - `release.yml` — signs and verifies eight files at once and checks the attestation of a release by one `gh release verify`; no new input, permission or condition on the caller;
  - `secrets.yml`, `dco.yml` and `scorecard.yml` — the same bytes; the names of the checks are those of `v0.1.24`, and so are the eleven the ruleset of `main` requires.
- **"Samples"** stays a job of `tutor`'s own and cannot take the artifact of "Go / toolchain": a job of the caller sees the jobs of a called workflow only by `needs` on the whole call, which would hold it until the four checks of `go.yml` end. So on a miss it builds the image, as "Documents" does; on `main` the entry is saved by "Go / toolchain" and "Documents" already, and a third save would race for the same key, so it saves nothing. Its step "Toolchain image" is the one script of `go.yml` byte for byte, with `SAVE` `false` and `STAGES` `toolchain`, as in the jobs after "Go / toolchain"; the step of the artifact is left out.
- **"Canvas"** takes the image and the caches of Go by `.github`'s composite action `toolchain`, `.github`'s own steps, not a copy: on a miss of the image it builds it, and the save runs only on the first push to `main` after a change of the Dockerfile. The caches of Go are an entry of the check `canvas`, since the checkout of "Canvas" has the submodule and that of `go.yml` none, and `go run ./cmd/tutor-eval` builds the runner without the race detector and the coverage of "Go / test", so the entry of `test` would give it little, and an entry is read-only once saved. The key hashes every `go.sum` of the checkout, `spec/`'s too, and the restore by the prefix keeps the builds on a move of the tag. The run of `ci-canvas-check` mounts the folder and points the three variables into it, as `go.yml`'s work does. "Samples" runs no Go and takes no cache. The folder of "Canvas" holds 170 MiB of modules and 718 MiB of builds before compression, measured locally — within the limit of 10 GB.
- **Checked:** `actionlint` 1.7.12 with `shellcheck` 0.11.0 by the sums of `self-ci.yml` of `.github` `v0.1.30`; the copied steps against `go.yml` of `v0.1.30` by `diff`; the `ci-` recipes in a clean clone with `env -i`, `ci-canvas-check` twice on one folder of caches, cold and warm; the checks of the pull request, their names those of before; the times of the jobs before and after. The record is in [plan/E10.md](plan/E10.md).

— *Why:* the time of the Go checks goes into compiling with an empty `GOCACHE` (measurements 3.1 spec); "Canvas" took 106–119 s on `main`, most of it building the runner twice, and "CodeQL / analyze (go)" 119 s, of which the build of the stage is a part. One entry of the image instead of two — `toolchain.tar` of `go.yml`, "Samples" and "Canvas" beside `novatrail-toolchain.tar` of "Documents" — halves what the image holds of the cache.

— *Rejected:* **"Samples" and "Canvas" waiting for the job "Go" by `needs`** — they would take no artifact either, only the entry, and wait for "Go / test", the longest check; **"Samples" saving the image on `main`** — "Go / toolchain" and "Documents" save the same key, and a third save only races for it; **"Canvas" on the entry of `go.yml`'s `test`** — built with the race detector and coverage, not what `go run` builds, and read-only; **a job "toolchain" of `tutor`'s own for the two** — a fourth build of the same image on a miss, to save at most one build in a run that already builds two; **the stage `toolchain-core` in the same move** — it was not released, and its stage needs `.devcontainer/`, which an unattended run of the orchestrator does not change; **the step "Toolchain image" without the branch of `docker save`**, which `SAVE` `false` never runs — the copy would no longer be `go.yml`'s byte for byte, which the comparison on a move reads by a diff, as an anchor was rejected for the same reason.

— *Consequence:* `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `.github/workflows/codeql.yml`; `.github/workflows/scorecard.yml`; `.github/workflows/release.yml`; `.claude/rules/ci.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `.bestpractices.json`.

## The move to `spec` `v0.18.4`: the stage `toolchain-core`

**R121. The move of `spec` to the tag `v0.18.4`, the latest on 2026-11-16 and the one that brought the stage `toolchain-core`, with the Go modules of `spec` and of the contracts at `v0.18.4` and `contracts/v0.18.4`; `module-kit` stays at `v0.3.3`, since none of its releases is on `v0.18.4`. CI calls the shared workflows of `.github` at `v0.1.31`, the release that builds the core, by the SHA of its tag, 93c848f, in every `uses:` of `.github/workflows/`. `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` has the template's three stages: `toolchain-core` — Node, just, Go, buf and `tutor`'s golangci-lint —, `toolchain`, built from it and adding nothing, and `devcontainer`. The release builds `toolchain-core`; every check runs in `toolchain`, which `go.yml` and `documents.yml` take for lint, vuln, licenses and the documents too, since it holds no more than the core, so the cache keeps one image. The comments of the stage `toolchain` and of gopls are the template's, one sentence true for every copy. Epic E10 is done and goes into its archive.**
- **What the tag brought** (`git -C spec log --oneline v0.18.3..v0.18.4`, four commits, and `git diff v0.18.3 v0.18.4` of the submodule): the stage `toolchain-core` in `spec`'s Dockerfile and the template, which the release builds by `TOOLCHAIN_STAGE` of `toolchain.sh`; `apt` from a source of the snapshot alone — `tutor` installs nothing by `apt`; the tests of `spec`'s `tools/` in three parts, `spec`'s own CI; the rows of its cross-repository plan on the speed of CI. `spec/RULES.md`, `tutor`'s card, the format, the contracts, the deployments, the admission table and `spec/run/DEBT.md` did not change, so the Go modules of `spec` move with nothing in `tutor`'s code; `gh release verify v0.18.4 -R altnovatrail/spec` — "Release v0.18.4 verified!". `module-kit` `v0.3.4`–`v0.3.7`, released the same day, all require `spec` `v0.18.3` (the module proxy's `.mod`), and the move takes `module-kit` only on the same tag.
- **What `v0.1.31` changes for `tutor`** (`gh release view v0.1.31 -R altnovatrail/.github` and `git diff v0.1.30 v0.1.31 -- .github/workflows/` in a clone of `.github` in a scratch folder): the job "Go / toolchain" of `go.yml` reads the Dockerfile, and where it has `toolchain-core` and its `toolchain` holds more — an instruction but `SHELL`, or another base —, "Go / lint", "Go / vuln" and "Go / licenses" run in the core, by a file and an entry of their own, `novatrail-toolchain-core.tar` under the key `toolchain-core-…`; elsewhere every check runs in `toolchain`, as before. `documents.yml` reads the same and hands the stage to `spec`'s `toolchain.sh` by `TOOLCHAIN_STAGE`; `release.yml` builds `toolchain-core` wherever the tree's Dockerfile has it; `codeql.yml` takes `GO_VERSION` and `GO_SHA256_AMD64` from `toolchain` and the stages it is built from. No new input, permission or name of a check.
- **The Dockerfile** — the template's, `just new-repo-draft tutor go 3.6 v0.18.4` in a clone of `spec` at the tag in a scratch folder, with `tutor`'s differences added again: golangci-lint in the core, since `go.yml` runs `ci-lint` there wherever `toolchain` holds more, and the comment of golangci-lint names `tutor`. `tutor`'s tests need nothing beyond the core — Go, and buf and Node for `sample-check` —, so `toolchain` adds nothing, and the reading of `go.yml` gives `toolchain` for every check: the cache keeps the one entry `novatrail-toolchain.tar`. `devcontainer.json`, `gopls/` and `host-network.sh` are the template's byte for byte.
- **"Samples"** — its step "Toolchain image" is the one of the jobs of `go.yml`, byte for byte, the loop over `STAGES` with `toolchain` and `SAVE` `false`; it keeps `toolchain`, the stage whose entry `go.yml` and "Documents" save.
- **After the merge** the author rebuilds the container. The Dockerfile's sha256 changes, so the pull request and the first push to `main` after it build the image from the network and run Go with empty caches; that push saves them again. The merge releases the next patch of `tutor`, the first built in `toolchain-core`, with the same code.

— *Why:* a release that builds the core alone does not build what only the tests need (measurements 3.1 spec), and a move to a tag of `spec` is a task of its own. For `tutor` the release gains nothing now: its `toolchain` adds nothing, so the release builds the same tools under the other name and the checks keep one image. The stage is there so that a tool for the tests alone goes into `toolchain`, out of the release and of the checks that run no test.

— *Rejected:* **golangci-lint in `toolchain`** — `toolchain` would then hold more than the core, `go.yml` would run `ci-lint` in the core without it, and "Go / lint" would fail; **"Samples" in the core** — it runs no browser and no test, but `tutor`'s cache holds no entry of the core, and a job of its own would build it on every run; **`module-kit` `v0.3.7`** — on `spec` `v0.18.3`, and the move takes it only on the tag it moves to.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; `.github/workflows/codeql.yml`; `.github/workflows/scorecard.yml`; `.github/workflows/release.yml`; `justfile`; `.claude/rules/ci.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `.bestpractices.json`; `CLAUDE.md`; `RUN.md`; `docs/measurements.md`; `docs/plan/E10.md`.

## The move to `spec` `v0.18.6`: the container outlives its window

**R122. The move of `spec` to the tag `v0.18.6`, the latest on 2026-11-16, with the Go modules of `spec` and of the contracts at `v0.18.6` and `contracts/v0.18.6`; `module-kit` stays at `v0.3.3`, since none of its releases is on `v0.18.6`. `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json` has the template's `"shutdownAction": "none"`, and `.devcontainer/Dockerfile` the template's comments of the stage `toolchain` and of gopls in place of `tutor`'s own. `spec` answered the row of table 3 on that comment, and the row left table 3. The move is epic E11 of its own, with one task, done and in its archive.** It came from `spec`'s orchestrator, whose first wave moves each repository to the latest tag; with no current epic, the task was the first of the next.
- **What the tags brought** (`git -C spec log --oneline v0.18.4..v0.18.6`, 14 commits; `git diff v0.18.4 v0.18.6` of the submodule; `grep -n '`tutor`' spec/run/DEBT.md`; `spec/RULES.md`; `spec/run/INBOX.md`):

The rest of the template — `.devcontainer/`, `.claude/`, CLAUDE.md, the rules of RUN.md and the files of `run/` — did not change from `v0.18.4` to `v0.18.6`, so `tutor`'s files stand as the moves to `v0.18.3` and `v0.18.4` and the plan's layout took them, with `tutor`'s differences;
  - the hook `UserPromptSubmit` of `spec/tools/session/`: the numbers that the orchestrator gives an executor's session in `NOVATRAIL_RUN_TASKS` count as one task, so a run that writes its task under another number than its prompt named goes on in the same session. It comes with the submodule, and `.claude/settings.json` does not change;
  - `docs-check` reads the tables of the plan only from `run/` and only under their English headings, and the block `repository` of `spec/tools/docs-check/budgets.json` has no `DEBT.md`: `tutor` has had both since its plan moved to `run/`, and the check is green on the new tag;
  - `spec/RULES.md`: table 3 is `run/QUESTIONS.md` alone, the sentence on translating a plan with the move to a tag is gone, and apt takes its packages from a source of its own whose URI names the snapshot's ID — `tutor` holds the first two already and installs nothing by apt;
  - `spec`'s inbox recorded the row of table 3 on the comment of the stage `toolchain` with the fingerprint `d66629cadc52`, the same as the row's on `main` of `tutor`, 5c2c0ad: the answer is one sentence true for all, neither a sentence that `new-repo` writes for each copy nor a comment rewritten on every move;
  - `spec`'s cross-repository plan closes its rows on the time access returns and on the speed of CI, done in `tutor`; the row on the admission entries of the workbook, the essay and the rubric stays;
  - the rest does not concern `tutor`: `tutor`'s card, the format, the contracts and the rows of `spec/run/DEBT.md` that name `tutor` did not change; a learner's start a band below the grade's and the adult's correction of a level are `learner-model`'s, `engine`'s and `app`'s; the orchestrator's code, the DPIA and `spec`'s measurements are `spec`'s own. `module-kit` `v0.3.8`, released the same day, requires `spec` `v0.18.4` (the module proxy's `.mod`).
- **What the move changed:** `spec/` — at `v0.18.6`, commit bf3dce9; `gh release verify v0.18.6 -R altnovatrail/spec` — "Release v0.18.6 verified!"; `go.mod` and `go.sum` — `spec` and `spec/contracts` `v0.18.6`, the hashes of their `go.mod` the same; `devcontainer.json`, `gopls/` and `host-network.sh` — the template's byte for byte; the Dockerfile — the template's, with `tutor`'s differences added again: golangci-lint in the core with its comment and sums, the opening's words on it, and the comment of buf on the contracts' image; the tag in CLAUDE.md, the start of RUN.md and the two links to `spec/RULES.md` in `.bestpractices.json`; the row of table 3 verbatim to [plan/questions.md](plan/questions.md).
- **After the merge** the author rebuilds the container, and the orchestrator waits for it before the next task; from then on closing its window does not stop it, and `docker stop` does. The Dockerfile's sha256 changes, so the pull request and the first push to `main` after it build the image from the network and run Go with empty caches. The merge releases the next patch of `tutor` with the same code.

— *Why:* the repository stands on the latest tag of `spec`, and the submodule and the Go modules move together. The template's comment of `toolchain` takes the place of `tutor`'s own because one sentence that holds in every copy is no longer rewritten by a move; what `tutor`'s comment said beyond it — that the shared `go.yml` runs every check in `toolchain`, one image in the cache — stays in this log and `.claude/rules/ci.md`. One task: two comments, one key of `devcontainer.json` and the tag are one review.

— *Rejected:* **`tutor`'s own comment of the stage `toolchain`** — a difference from the template that every move has to add again by hand, which the row of table 3 asked to end; **`module-kit` `v0.3.8`** — on `spec` `v0.18.4`, and the move takes `module-kit` only on its own tag; **a split into tasks** — the change is less than one review.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`; `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; `CLAUDE.md`; `RUN.md`; `.bestpractices.json`; `docs/plan/questions.md`; `docs/plan/E11.md`; `docs/measurements.md`.

## Epic E12: the admission entries on Anthropic's models

**R123. Epic E12 is the admission entries on Anthropic's models for reading a workbook, reading an essay and assessing by a rubric: first the author's decisions on the set, the rule, the target and the models of each of `CallWorkbook`, `CallEssay` and `CallRubric`, then the admission rules of the workbook and of the essay with the rubric, then the three entries of `admission.Admitted` with the expected tokens of `Price`, and last the release that carries them. The paid runs it planned for the entries are not done: while eval before admission is suspended, the three entries stand by the author's word. The epic is done and whole in its archive [docs/plan/E12.md](plan/E12.md); the rung "full" of the three kinds is Д19.**
- **Why decisions first.** Only the reading of a sheet by a pair was admitted, and the assessment by the rubric had no admission, its target waiting for the volunteers' essays (П-16); the reading of handwriting had no admission on synthetic sheets, and `samples/` holds no workbook page or essay of the author. Each of the three kinds needed a decision of the author before its rule could be built, and one return of questions keeps the code tasks from stopping halfway.
- **The paid runs are the author's step.** A paid run estimates its cost on the stub, returns the question of its ceiling and spends only after the answer. While the suspension holds they are not needed; the runs on Haiku by the rules the epic built are Д17.
- **The essay and the rubric together.** Their entries go in one task, because `api` begins an essay only where both are admitted.
- **Out of the epic:** the rung "full", which waits for the samples of adult volunteers (П-16) — the tests hold that a least rung "full" chooses none of the new entries; models of other providers; the calls in `api`; Д6 and Д11.

— *Why:* `spec`'s cross-repository plan asks the entries before a module with a workbook or an essay enters the catalog of a deployment, and until them `ReadWorkbook`, `ReadEssay` and `AssessEssay` answer "not admitted".

— *Rejected:* **the decisions inside each rule's task** — three rounds of questions, each stopping a task with code half done; **the paid run inside the rule's task** — a paid run on code not yet reviewed; **one paid task for all three kinds** — larger than one review; **waiting for the volunteers' samples** — the plan asks the entries now, at the rung "author", since only the author learns.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md; [docs/plan/E12.md](plan/E12.md); [DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md).

## The admission of the workbook, the essay and the rubric

**R124. `CallWorkbook`, `CallEssay` and `CallRubric` have their admission rules at the rung "author" on the synthetic sets drawn by code — `eval/workbook-gen.json`, 300 pages, and `eval/essays-gen.json`, 300 essays —, each a rule of its own: the workbook by the sheet's rule by box, on the boxes an adult writes; the essay's reading by its own rule; the assessment by the rubric by the upper bound of false alarms of no more than 1 %. The models: the workbook — the sheet's pair, Opus 5.5 · `high` and Haiku 5.5 · `medium`; the essay's reading — Haiku 5.5 · `medium`; the rubric — Haiku 5.5 · `medium`, on Haiku's reading. The entries read only the author's work, and need no pages of the author's handwriting. Until 2027-01-17, while eval before admission is suspended and the author alone learns, the three entries stand by the author's word, without a run: these rules are those of the runs on Haiku by which each entry is then admitted or leaves the list (Д17).**
- **The set.** The truth holds by construction (measurements 3.10). Only the author learns on `novatrail`, and the author's pages of a workbook and essays would cost a record mode of the board and hours of writing. So for these three kinds the rung stands on synthetic strokes, as the words are admitted on synthetic cases, and says nothing of live handwriting: each entry and its evidence name the set, and the author's first pages in `just dev-app` of `api` or on `novatrail` are the live check. The reading of a sheet keeps its set of the author's sheets. The admission run takes the test parts — pages 21–300 and essays 21–300 —, without the dev parts, the first 20 of each (measurements 3.10): the first 20 pages tuned the workbook's prompt of version 2 (measurements 3.15) and tried version 3.
- **The workbook's rule** — the sheet's, by box: the pair, the verdicts of `module-kit`, not one false alarm and not one false "correct", no fewer than 30 boxes where a false alarm is possible and 20 where a false "correct" is, an event counted once by box over all repeats — on the boxes of the kinds an adult writes: correct, an error, a near key, a similar digit, a fraction stacked or in a line. Mirrored and crossed-out boxes are rows of the report of their own, without a target: an adult does not write a digit mirrored; code draws a crossed-out digit with one short stroke, and a single digit under it is read together with the new one, so that the error comes partly from the drawing (measurements 3.7); and a child's workbook waits for children's samples. Their rows are kept, as the platform asks the workbook's eval set to measure mirrored digits as a row of their own. The cost: an adult does cross out and rewrite, and no target holds the pair on that — the author's first pages are its live check. On 300 pages of version 1 almost all false alarms were on such boxes — 31–41 % of mirrored ones and ≈ 16 % of crossed-out ones —, and 0–1 of 900 on boxes without peculiarities (measurements 3.12).
- **The unit of the essay's rules** is the essay and the criterion of an essay: an event is counted once if at least one repeat gave it, and the bound is by essay, as the sheet's rule counts an answer slot — not every judgement of every repeat, a bound too narrow. The essays carry no verdict of the program, so their refusal without a rung is a refused sample and, for the rubric, a judge that did not pass its control phrases.
- **The essay's reading rule** — one reader: not one legible essay read "illegible", not one of the learner's errors read corrected — a corrected misspelling, lowercase letter or missing full stop gives a false "met" —, and the upper 95 % bound of the errors in words, by essay, no more than 1 %.
- **The rubric's rule** — the platform's main measure of an assessment, the false alarms on met criteria: the upper 95 % bound by essay no more than 1 % on the synthetic set; the missed "not met" and κ by criterion go beside, without a target. The feedback is in English only — `english-essays` explains in `en`, and the set measures English —: the words judge of version 3 flags no feedback for gender, safety or leaving the task, and the breaches of the feedback rule go beside, since `api` replaces such feedback by the fallback text. A feedback language other than English is not admitted until it is measured. The rubric of the set is E4's six synthetic criteria: the published modules have no eval sets yet. `spec`'s decisions hold this target for the rung "author" before the volunteers' essays; on the volunteers' essays the target stays the platform's own, for the rung "full".
- **The models** — each kind by its best numbers of the 300 synthetic essays in two calls (measurements 3.11 and 3.12). Haiku · `medium` had the fewest false alarms of the rubric, 2 of 1350, at $0.0008 an essay. For the essay's reading Opus had the best numbers — it kept all 178 of the learner's errors and had 1 error in words of 17,683 —, but the runs go on Haiku, and Haiku · `medium` read the 15 essays of the two calls without an error in words (measurements 3.11). The rubric is measured on Haiku's reading, as the product calls it; the runner's `run -kind essay -two -assessor` names the assessor's configuration beside the reader's. Haiku · `medium` ran the reading of version 2 and the assessment of version 3 only on 15 essays: the admission runs measure them. None of the three needs 30-day retention.
- **Whose work:** only the author's — the workbook in `just dev-app` of `api` as a synthetic child, the essays on `novatrail`. A real child's workbook or essay stays closed by the admission table's facts for minors, which `novatrail` does not declare. `Table.Select` takes the rung the deployment names, not one by the age category: a deployment that declares facts for any cell under 18 names the rung "full", a condition of the deployment that `spec`'s decisions hold and no code of `tutor` holds.

— *Why:* `spec`'s cross-repository plan asks the three entries before a module with a workbook or an essay is in a deployment's catalog, and only the author learns on `novatrail`, so the rung "author" on synthetic sets is enough for now.

— *Rejected:* **the author's handwriting** — a record mode of the board and hours of writing, while the author recorded 7 of the 54 planned sheets of the sheet's admission; **the sheet's rule over all boxes** — on the synthetic set the child-like boxes would refuse an entry for an adult's work; **a bound for the workbook** — weaker per event than the sheet's zero; **the essay's reading only by the rubric's path** — it would leave `CallEssay` without a rule of its own; **not one error in words** — no configuration had zero on 300 essays; **zero events for the rubric** — only a set of ≈ 10 essays would pass, with a bound of ≈ 8–10 %; **no rubric entry until the volunteers** — `api` starts an essay only where both kinds are admitted, and the essay's entry would give `api` nothing; **both essay calls on Opus** and **the cheapest models** — more false alarms of the rubric, or learner's errors corrected; **Opus for the essay's reading** — the runs go on Haiku, and the essay's reading rule measures whichever reader runs; **"full" in code for every category but `adult`** — it would close the workbook with models in `just dev-app`, which `spec`'s cross-repository plan needs; **no workbook entry now** — the same plan needs it.

— *Consequence:* `internal/report/admit.go`; `internal/report/admitessay.go`; `cmd/tutor-eval/`; `eval/workbook-gen.json`; `eval/essays-gen.json`; `admission/models.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The mode of reading: the pair or one reader

**R125. The library reads a sheet and a workbook page in one of two modes, which `api` chooses by `Config.Reading`: `pair` — both readers of the entry chosen — and `single` — one reader, Haiku 5.5 · `medium`, the pair's second. An empty mode is `pair`; any other value fails `New`.
