The reading of an essay and every other call are the same in both. `spec`'s decisions hold both modes as the deployment's choice: `single` serves every age category at every rung, by the pair's entry at its rung with Haiku as its reader, and the reading is refused `model_not_admitted` where no entry of the pair serves the rung.**
- **What the mode changes.** In `single`, the calls `CallSheet` and `CallWorkbook` ask one model of the entry chosen: `anthropic`, `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `medium`, found as the list names it, not by its place in the entry. An entry without it is a failure of the call, `Failed`, before any model is asked, with an error that names the reader the mode wants: the mode asks no other model in its place. One choice, `chooseCall`, holds it for the call, `ChooseCall`, `Price` and `CheckKey` alike, so that what `api` admits, reserves a quota for and checks a key for is the one model the call asks. `SheetRead` and `WorkbookRead` give one observation then, and module-kit's verdict is by it; `Price` is Haiku's alone, by its expected tokens.
- **What it keeps.** The admission is the pair's entry, unchanged: `single` reads by an entry the pair passed, at its rung, and admits no new entry. The workbook reads by the pair's entry that the author's word admits, and in `single` by Haiku 5.5 · `medium` of that entry alone. `CallEssay` is one reader already; `CallRubric`, `CallWords`, `CallHint` and `CallCorrect` are one model each.

— *Why:* on the site the pair gave «Читается двояко» and cost $0.0087 a "Check" — Haiku 400 microdollars, Opus 8,276 —, while Haiku alone read the author's sheets well enough for the author. The pair's second reader insures against an error that the synthetic data did not show; the mode keeps that insurance for a deployment that wants it, and the default stays the pair, so that a config that names no mode reads by two readers, as the platform's rule of reading has it.

— *Rejected:* **replacing the pair by one reader** — a deployment that wants the pair's insurance would lose it; **the reader by its place in the entry**, the second model — an entry ordered otherwise would give Opus or another model silently; **falling back to the pair, or to the entry's first model, when the entry has no Haiku · `medium`** — the mode would then ask a model `api` did not choose it for; **a new entry of the list for Haiku alone** — no run measured it alone at the rung, and the mode is no admission.

— *Consequence:* `tutor.go` — `Config.Reading`, `Reading`, `Tutor.asked`; `sheet.go`, `workbook.go`, `price.go`, `key.go`; `reading_test.go`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The admission by the author's word

**R126. Until 2027-01-17, and only while the author alone learns on `novatrail`, the author's word admits, without eval, three entries of `admission.Admitted` at the rung "author": `CallWorkbook` — `handwriting_reading`, the sheet's pair Opus 5.5 · `high` and Haiku 5.5 · `medium` on the workbook's prompt of version 3, which Haiku reads alone in the mode `single`; `CallEssay` — `handwriting_reading`, one reader, Haiku 5.5 · `medium`, on `essay.ReadFingerprint`; `CallRubric` — `rubric_assessment`, Haiku 5.5 · `medium`, on `essay.TextFingerprint`. Their evidence is the word, its day and its last day, not a report; an entry by the word serves only the least rung "author", and the list's test fails once its last day has passed. The paid runs of these three kinds are not done.**
- **The evidence.** `admission.Evidence` has `Word` and `Until`: an entry by the word has `Word` "the author's word, while eval before admission is suspended", `Date` 2026-11-17 and `Until` 2027-01-17, and no set, file or SHA-256; `Evidence.ByWord` tells it. `Table.Select` and `SelectPrompt` skip an entry by the word unless the least rung is "author", whatever rung it names: no deployment that names "full" reads by it. The package has no clock, so the list's test holds the lapse: `TestAdmitted` faults an entry by the word whose `Until` has passed, and CI fails from 2027-01-18 until a run admits the entry by its rule or the entry leaves the list (Д17).
- **The models.** The workbook — the sheet's pair. The essay's reading — Haiku 5.5 · `medium`: the runs go on Haiku, and Haiku · `medium` read the 15 essays of the two calls without an error in words (measurements 3.11). The rubric — Haiku 5.5 · `medium`, on Haiku's reading. None of them needs 30-day retention.
- **The expected tokens** in `price.go` — the averages of the runs that tried the prompts `api` calls now, without new spending (measurements 3.19): the workbook — the 20 pages of the trial part on version 3, one call of each reader; the essay and the rubric — the 15 essays of E4's test part, Haiku · `medium`, the reading of version 2 and the assessment of version 3.
- **The bound of the suspension.** It holds until 2027-01-17 inclusive, on `novatrail` alone and only while the author alone learns there: the first code of `api invite` to another adult, from 2026-11-28, or the first child ends it sooner, and before either each entry by the word passes an eval on Haiku 5.5, leaves the list, or the author gives a new word. A self-hosted copy whose operator keeps the rung "author" while others learn on it names an `api` before `v0.1.60` or the rung "full"; after 2027-01-17 every deployment names only an `api` whose `tutor` admits each such entry by an eval, or admits none, and admission is by eval again, its runs on Haiku 5.5. At the rung "full" a model is admitted only by an eval, as the tests hold.
- **What stays.** The rules of the workbook and of the essay with the rubric and the sets `eval/workbook-gen.json` and `eval/essays-gen.json` are the way back: each entry by the word is admitted by its rule on a run of Haiku, or leaves the list (Д17). The sheet's pair, the words and the words after a correct answer keep their reports. The tests hold that the rung "full", an own key of OpenAI and a child's cells without the facts for minors refuse all three.

— *Why:* no learners but the author are expected for a long time, and `spec`'s cross-repository plan asks the entries for `api`'s `ReadWorkbook`, `ReadEssay` and `AssessEssay` now, while the paid runs, about $25 and $30, waited for a credit balance that was negative. The platform suspends for these entries its rule that a model is admitted only after an eval, within the bound above.

— *Rejected:* **the paid runs now** — not needed while the suspension holds; **an evidence that names a report it lacks** — a set, file or SHA-256 of nothing would pass the list's test as a measurement; **an empty evidence** — the list's test could not tell the word from a forgotten report; **the lapse in `Select`** — the package has no clock, and a library that stops admitting on a date would close `api`'s calls with no release and no warning; **Opus for the essay's reading** — the runs go on Haiku, and the essay's reading rule measures whichever reader runs.

— *Consequence:* `admission/models.go` — `Evidence.Word`, `Evidence.Until`, `Evidence.ByWord`, the three entries and `selectFrom`; `price.go` — the expected tokens; the tests — `admission/models_test.go`, `byword_test.go` and those of the workbook, the essay, the rubric and the key; `docs/architecture.md`; [DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md).

**R127. Epic E12 is done: the release `v0.1.46` of `tutor`, the tag on the merge of the entries, ad38bac, carries the three entries by the author's word and their expected tokens, and `api` opens `ReadWorkbook`, `ReadEssay` and `AssessEssay` from it. The rung "full" of the three kinds is Д19. The epic is whole in its archive [docs/plan/E12.md](plan/E12.md).**
- **The release.** `release.yml` released `v0.1.46` by itself after the merge of the entries: immutable, six files, and `gh release verify v0.1.46 -R altnovatrail/tutor` — «Release v0.1.46 verified!». A merge that changes documents alone releases nothing: its run of the release says «Nothing the release is built from has changed since v0.1.46: nothing to release». `api` moves to `v0.1.46`.
- **For `spec` and `api`** — `v0.1.46` gives the entries that `spec`'s cross-repository plan asked of `tutor`, and the plan keeps nothing of them.
- **The archive** — as for the epics before it: the bodies of the tasks went in one by one; then the task table with the description of E12 and the opening of its section go to the end of the file.
- **The measurement** — measurements 4.1, the row of E12: nine sessions from the archive of E11 to this archive. The context of a main call is 147.3 thousand, 27% below the reference of `tutor`, 202.4 thousand, over 647 main calls: below the platform's threshold of an analysis.
- **What stays open** — Д6, Д11, Д17 (the runs on Haiku by 2027-01-17), Д18 and Д19.

— *Why:* `spec`'s cross-repository plan asks a release with the entries, and the epic closes with the check of the release that carries them.

— *Rejected:* **a release of the close** — it changes nothing the release is built from, and `v0.1.46` already carries all three entries; **keeping the epic's debt open for the rung "full"** — the debt was the epic's, and "full" waits for П-16, not for the epic, so it is a row of its own; **writing out the next epic** — the author chooses it.

— *Consequence:* RUN.md; [docs/plan/E12.md](plan/E12.md); [DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md).

**R128. README.md has no sections "Status" and "License", no sentence on the languages of the documents and the plan, and names neither a tag of `spec` nor the submodule: it holds only what `tutor` is and its place in NovaTrail, and the license badge stays with the other badges. Epic E13 was this one task, and it is whole in its archive [docs/plan/E13.md](plan/E13.md).**
- **What "Status" held** — how to start the board and its record mode, the drawn sheets, the runs, admission, the words, `canvas-check`, `tutor.New`, and the history of the epics. What a session needs of it is in `docs/architecture.md` and the rules `eval` and `ci`; the history is in the archives of `docs/plan/` and in this log. Nothing is moved.
- **What called for them** — no rule of RUN.md, no open task, no check and no test. A move to a tag and the close of an epic do not touch README; the same holds for the README of `spec`'s template, by `spec`'s own rule.
- **The measurement** — measurements 4.1, the row of E13: two sessions from the archive of E12 to the measurement itself, 47 main calls, fewer than the 300 the platform's threshold of an analysis asks: no analysis.

— *Why:* a status and a tag in README go out of date with every task and every move to a tag, and the files LICENSE and NOTICE and the badge already name the license. Every NovaTrail repository's README keeps to the same.

— *Rejected:* **moving the board's instructions into `docs/architecture.md`** — it already holds them; **rewriting each entry that names README** — they are history, not rules; **a release** — README is not among the files the release is built from.

— *Consequence:* README.md; RUN.md; [docs/plan/E13.md](plan/E13.md).

## The move to `spec` `v0.18.14`

**R129. The move of `spec` to the tag `v0.18.14`, the latest on 2026-11-17, with the Go modules of `spec` and of the contracts at `v0.18.14` and `contracts/v0.18.14`; `module-kit` stays at `v0.3.3`, since none of its releases is on `v0.18.14`. RUN.md's rule on commits has the template's clause on commit messages and pull requests; the mode of reading, the admission by the author's word and Д17 rest on `spec`'s decisions in place of the author's word. The move is epic E14 of its own, with this one task, whole in its archive [docs/plan/E14.md](plan/E14.md): with no current epic, a task outside an epic is the first of the next.**
- **What the tags brought** (`git -C spec log --oneline v0.18.6..v0.18.14`, 25 commits; `git diff v0.18.6 v0.18.14` of the submodule; `grep -n '`tutor`' spec/run/DEBT.md`; `spec`'s card of `tutor`; RULES.md; `spec/run/INBOX.md`):
  - the reading mode: `spec`'s rule of the pair gains two modes, `pair` and `single`, the deployment's choice, and `single` serves every learner at every rung, admitted by the pair's entry with Haiku as its reader; `spec`'s card of `tutor` names the mode in its item of reading. `tutor` had built it, and the card and `tutor`'s entry of the mode agree;
  - the admission by the author's word: `spec`'s rule that a model is admitted only after an eval is suspended until 2027-01-17, with its bound by who learns; the essay is read by Haiku 5.5 · `medium`; `spec` keeps a debt of its own for the runs on Haiku. `tutor` had built the entries and released them in `v0.1.46`;
  - the template, by `just new-repo-draft tutor go 3.6 v0.18.14` in a clone of `spec` at the tag in a scratch folder: RUN.md's rule on commits gains "commit messages and a pull request's title and description in English and terse, for the session, never a copy of the report", which RULES.md now holds, and README names no version of `spec` and has no section of status or license, as `tutor`'s README already had it. `.devcontainer/`, `.claude/`, CLAUDE.md and the files of `run/` did not change: `devcontainer.json`, `gopls/`, `host-network.sh` and `.claude/settings.json` are the template's byte for byte, and the Dockerfile differs by `tutor`'s golangci-lint and buf comment alone;
  - the rest does not concern `tutor`: the tutor's words only while the tutor's panel is open are when `app` asks and `api` gives them, and `tutor`'s calls are the same; `SetModuleLevel` and the cause `CAUSE_BAND_NOT_NEXT` are `api`'s, `learner-model`'s and `app`'s; the answers to `canvas` and `app` are theirs. The rows of `spec/run/DEBT.md` that name `tutor` are as before but the new one for the runs on Haiku: of their parts that are `tutor`'s — buf in CLAUDE.md, the cached release of `.github`, `--network-alias=tutor` and gopls by `CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_DIRS` — all are already held by `tutor`'s files on `main`, for `spec` to read. `module-kit` `v0.3.9` and `v0.3.10` require `spec` `v0.18.6` (the module proxy's `.mod`).
- **What the move changed:** `spec/` — at `v0.18.14`, commit 20e6384; `gh release verify v0.18.14 -R altnovatrail/spec` — "Release v0.18.14 verified!"; `go.mod` and `go.sum` — `spec` and `spec/contracts` `v0.18.14`, the hashes of their `go.mod` the same; RUN.md's rule on commits; the tag in CLAUDE.md, the start of RUN.md and the two links to RULES.md in `.bestpractices.json`, whose two changed answers the author saves on bestpractices.dev; the comments of `Reading` in `tutor.go` and of `authorsWord` in `admission/models.go`, which now rest on `spec`'s decisions, and a comment of `admission/models_test.go` that still said a deployment for anyone but the author takes only the rung "full"; Д17 with `spec`'s bound and the options for the workbook's pair after a run on Haiku; the options of the then open question on the price of the words. No rebuild of the container: `.devcontainer/` is unchanged; the merge releases the next patch of `tutor` with the same code.

— *Why:* the repository stands on the latest tag of `spec`, as the shared rules ask, and the submodule and the Go modules move together. The mode of reading and the admission by the word waited for this tag to rest on `spec`'s decisions instead of the word; `spec`'s bound — before the first code of `api invite` — is earlier and plainer than "anyone but the author begins to learn", so Д17 returns by it. One task: no file of `.devcontainer/` changes, and the rest is one review.

— *Rejected:* **`module-kit` `v0.3.10`** — on `spec` `v0.18.6`, and the move takes `module-kit` only on its own tag; **a row «В реестр spec» for `tutor`'s parts of `spec`'s debts** — they ask `spec` nothing, and `spec` reads `tutor`'s files on `main`; **a split into several tasks** — the change is less than one review.

— *Consequence:* `spec/`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `tutor.go`; `admission/models.go`; `admission/models_test.go`; CLAUDE.md; RUN.md; `.bestpractices.json`; [DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md); [plan/E14.md](plan/E14.md).

## The reading at 24 px

**R130. Haiku 5.5 · `medium`, the site's single reader, reads the author's handwriting at 24 px no worse than at 32 px: on the 60 sheets of the admission, one extra "unreadable" against none, p 0.5 by the report's test of heights, and no false alarm or false "correct" — the rung "author" at 24 px as at 32. The format's default stands: a module without `canvas.digit_height` is read at 24 px, and the modules for adults set no `canvas.digit_height`. Д18 is narrowed to the stacked fractions and to Opus at 24 px. A run that broke off goes on in its own log by `tutor-eval run -rest LOG`.**
- **The run** — measurements 3.21, one paid run within a ceiling of $1 while the other paid runs wait: `run -kind reading -part recorded -readers haiku-medium -reads keys,heights` over the four recorded sets of the author's sheets, the prompt `c693bfa8…` of version 2; Anthropic, 645 calls, $0.2610, none failed. 16 px is no worse either (2 errors), and 48 px is worse (5, p 0.031), as on synthetic sheets for Haiku · `low`.
- **What was not measured** — the site's case that raised the question, a stacked fraction whose numerator runs into the bar: the sheets' synthesis draws fractions inline alone, and none of the 60 sheets has one; the author's «3x + 1 = 7» with 8/3 inline was read right at 24 px. Opus at 24 px: the site reads by Haiku alone in the mode `single`. A stacked fraction at 24 px stays Д18.
- **`-rest`** — the first run broke off after 279 calls with the session that ran it, and `internal/runner` had no way on but a new run of all 645. `Run.Done` reads what a log that stopped short holds — broken off, or stopped by its cap or an interruption: its record must be the run's byte for byte at the log's moment and cap, its sheets the run's, a note its step's, and a call's record the one its step gives again for the outcome the record holds, so that a step that takes an earlier one's answer, as an essay's assessment takes its reading, has it again; a log that ended whole, a line cut off and more steps than the run has are refused. `Run.Rest` asks the steps from there into the same log, opened for appending, with an end that tells the stop cut off, and the cap counts what the log spent; `tutor-eval` refuses a log that has grown since it was read, a run still writing it. The report reads the log as one run's; its record keeps the cap and the moment of the first start.

— *Why:* the question was whether the site's 24 px reads the author's handwriting as the admission at 32 px did; the run answers it on the same sheets and the same prompt, and the rule of the heights' test was set before the data. A rest asks only the calls not made, and the log stays the one a whole run writes.

— *Rejected:* **the modules for adults set 32 px** — 24 px is no worse, and it would reverse the format's default; **a new run of all 645 calls** — it pays again for 279 calls; **a second log for the rest, joined by the report** — the report reads one log, and a run's record and sheets would be written twice; **sheets with stacked fractions drawn now** — `internal/synth` writes them only in the workbook, and a sheet of them is code of its own, beyond this run.

— *Consequence:* `internal/runner/runner.go`; `cmd/tutor-eval/runner.go`; `docs/architecture.md`; [DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md); [plan/E15.md](plan/E15.md).

**R131. The Claude adapter caches the instructions of the tutor's words, never the work: a request whose instructions `provider.Request.CacheInstructions` marks — those of the hint, `CallWords` and `CallHint`, and those after a correct answer, `CallCorrect`, the same text in every call of their kind — sends its system prompt with a breakpoint of the cache, `cache_control` `ephemeral`, for the 5 minutes Anthropic keeps a write by default, and nothing after it is marked. The words' model stays Opus 5.5 · `high`; the prompts, their fingerprints and the admission do not change. Epic E16 was this one task, in its archive [plan/E16.md](plan/E16.md).**
- **The prefix** — measurements 3.22: the system prompt of the hint is 1,362 tokens and that after a correct answer 811, above the minimum of 512 on Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5 and Haiku 5.5, below which a breakpoint caches nothing and no error says so (sources 1.18). The prefix is the tools, none, and the system prompt; the message after it holds the pictures and the text of the work. A call of the same kind on the same entry asks the same schema at the same effort and thinking, so it hits; the hint with the work and without it share the prefix, since pictures change only the cache of the messages; a change of the schema or, on the models before Opus 5.5, of the effort would miss (sources 1.18).
- **The money** — on Opus 5.5 a hit saves $0.0052 a call of the hint and a miss costs $0.0014 more, $0.0031 and $0.0008 after a correct answer: the cache pays from ≈ 21 % of hits within 5 minutes (measurements 3.22). The cache is per workspace of the key: the calls on the platform's key share it, and an own key has its own.
- **The usage** — `Usage` already carries `CacheWrites` and `CacheReadTokens`, and its `USD` prices them; nothing of it changes. `Tutor.Price` keeps its expected tokens — the whole input, measured before the cache — all at the input's price: a miss costs $0.0014 more than it on the hint and a hit $0.0052 less, so it is the mean at ≈ 21 % of hits; a new measurement of them sums the input and the cache's tokens. Its worst already takes each input token at the higher of the input's and a write's price.
- **Eval** — the runner asks the same `hint.RequestOf` and `correct.RequestOf`, so a paid run of the words caches too; the answer does not change with the cache (sources 1.18), and the entries stand. A call's record in the log gets `cache_write_tokens` and `cache_read_tokens` beside `input_tokens`, and its `cost_usd` and the spending the cap counts take them at their prices; the worst case of a step, which the cap reserves, takes each input token at the higher of the input's price and a 5-minute write's, as `Tutor.Price`'s worst does. That raises the worst of every run's estimate, and the golden logs of the stub change in their run's record alone; a log of a run before the cache is not continued by `-rest`, whose record must match byte for byte.
- **In `api`** — `api` takes the cache with its move to the release that carries it; its `ai_usage` logs `cache_write_tokens` and `cache_read_tokens`, and the gain shows by the cost of the words' lines. `infra` reads the share of the words' cache hits in `ai_usage` from 2026-11-24 against the ≈ 21 % the cache pays from (measurements 3.22), and a share far from it comes back to `tutor` as a row «в реестр spec», to count the expected tokens again. `spec`'s card of `tutor` names the cache, and no decision of `spec` changed.
- **Other adapters** — the GPT adapter is unchanged and sends nothing for the mark; no entry of the list of admitted models names OpenAI. OpenAI caches by default, and its implicit mode puts the breakpoint at the end of the latest user message, the work (sources 1.18): what the adapter sends for it waits for an entry of OpenAI — Д20.

— *Why:* the words are ≈ 97 % of the money of a «Проверить» on `novatrail` (measurements 3.20), and their instructions are the platform's text, so the cache keeps nothing of the learner's — the work comes after the breakpoint and is never written —, and the model and its admission stay as eval measured them.

— *Rejected:* **Haiku 5.5 · `medium` for the words**, after a run of the words' prompt on it or by the author's word now — its leaks on the words' first prompt would show the learner the fallback text more often, and the prompt in use is unmeasured on it; **no change** — the words stay ≈ 97 % of the money of a check; **the automatic cache**, `cache_control` at the top of the request — it puts the breakpoint on the last block, the work, which changes with each call: a write every time, no hit, and the work in the cache (sources 1.18); **the adapter caching every system prompt** — it cannot tell the platform's text from a caller's, so the request names what may be cached; **the reading's instructions too** — Haiku 5.5's reading costs ≈ $0.0004 a check, and the words hold the money; **a write for an hour** — twice the input's price, so a miss costs $4 a million more against a hit's $3.80 saved, and it pays only from ≈ 51 % of hits, which no log shows yet.

— *Consequence:* `provider/doc.go`; `provider/claude/claude.go`; `internal/hint/hint.go`; `internal/correct/correct.go`; `internal/runner/runner.go`; `price.go`, the comment of the expected tokens; `docs/architecture.md`; [DEBT.md](../run/DEBT.md).

**R132. README.md holds only what `tutor` is and its place in NovaTrail: its title and badges, two paragraphs on what it does and does not do, a short list of the components it is linked with and how — api, module-kit with the CI of modules, engine and canvas —, and links to its card in `spec` and to `docs/`. It has no command, block of code, instruction, status, version, date, number or language, and no section but its opening. What it held that no other file did is in [docs/README.md](README.md), the index of `docs/` with the sections "Releases", "Contributing" and "Build and run". Epic E17 was this one task, whole in its archive [docs/plan/E17.md](plan/E17.md).**
- **What moved where:**
  - the paragraph "Releases" — to the section Releases of docs/README.md: the files of a release, their bundles, and how a release and a file are checked inside NovaTrail and outside it. What the release is built from, its line, the recipe of its files and the format of the open views stay in `docs/architecture.md`;
  - the list "Documents" — to the section Documents of docs/README.md, with the sheet documents and `docs/plan/` added;
  - the sentence on the working rules and the clone with `--recurse-submodules` — to the sections Contributing and Build and run, with the organization's CONTRIBUTING.md and the security policy, which `.bestpractices.json` already named; Build and run points to the justfile, the rules of the parts and `docs/architecture.md`, which hold the commands;
  - stage 2, the check board and the 56 tasks of the measurements — dropped: a status and numbers, which `docs/architecture.md` already holds.
- **What called for them:** no rule of RUN.md and no check. `.bestpractices.json` names docs/README.md and `docs/architecture.md`, not README, for how to obtain and use `tutor` and for its commands — `interact`, `documentation_basics` and `documentation_interface` —, and the author saves the three changed answers on bestpractices.dev.
- **The measurement** — measurements 4.1, the row of E17.

— *Why:* agents run the commands, not programmers, and a README with commands, numbers, languages or a status goes out of date. Contributing belongs with the other documents, and the details of building and running in a section of their own.

— *Rejected:* **dropping the paragraph "Releases"** — how someone outside NovaTrail checks a release is written nowhere else; **`docs/releases.md` and `docs/contributing.md`** — two small files where one index holds them; **"Build and run" with its commands** — the rules of the parts and `docs/architecture.md` hold them, and a copy would drift; **a release** — none of the changed files is among those the release is built from.

— *Consequence:* README.md; [docs/README.md](README.md); `.bestpractices.json`; RUN.md; [docs/plan/E17.md](plan/E17.md).

## The move to `spec` `v0.18.27`

**R133. The move of `spec` to the tag `v0.18.27`, the latest on 2026-11-18, with the Go modules of `spec` and of the contracts at `v0.18.27` and `contracts/v0.18.27`; `module-kit` does not move with it, since none of its releases is on `v0.18.27`. CI calls the shared workflows of `.github` at `v0.1.33`, by the SHA of its tag, f1c7e9e, and the job "Canvas" takes the image and the caches of Go by `.github`'s composite action `toolchain` of the same release in place of its copy of `go.yml`'s steps; "Samples", which runs no Go, keeps its copy. `spec` recorded the six rows of table 3 that `tutor` had put to it, and they left the table. The move is epic E18 of its own, «Переход на тег `spec` v0.18.27», with one task, which is done, and the epic is in its archive [docs/plan/E18.md](plan/E18.md).** With no current epic, a task outside an epic begins the next one.
- **What the tags brought** (`git -C spec log --oneline v0.18.14..v0.18.27`, 38 commits; `git diff v0.18.14 v0.18.27` of the submodule; `grep -n '`tutor`' spec/run/DEBT.md`; `tutor`'s card in `spec/docs/architecture.md`; `spec/RULES.md`; `spec/run/INBOX.md`; read 2026-11-18):
  - the answers to `tutor`'s rows «в реестр spec», from the inbox triage of `spec`'s orchestrator, each recorded with the fingerprint of its row on `tutor`'s `main`, e56bf1d, unchanged: the target of the rubric at the rung "author" on the synthetic set, at most 1 % false alarms on an essay, with `CallRubric` admitted by the author's word, its bound 2027-01-17; a deployment that declares the facts for a cell under 18 names the rung "full", which asks nothing of `tutor`'s code; the price of a Check and the tutor's words, with no decision changed and a row of the cross-repository plan for `infra`; the height of a digit, by one paid run of the reading at 24 px, which found it no worse than 32 px; nothing to record for the release of the author's entries; and the cache's tokens in `api`'s log, done by `api`. [docs/plan/questions.md](plan/questions.md) holds where each went;
  - `spec`'s rule of the admission by the author's word: it holds on `novatrail` alone, and after 2027-01-17 every deployment names only an `api` whose `tutor` admits the word's entries by an eval, or none; `spec`'s debt dates the first code of `api invite` from 2026-11-28. Д17 takes the date and, for `CallRubric`, `spec`'s target of the rubric;
  - `tutor`'s card in `spec/docs/architecture.md` names the cache of the words' instructions and no longer cites closed rows of the cross-repository plan; nothing else of it changed;
  - the shared rules: issues and pull requests in English, a person's too — [docs/README.md](README.md), Contributing, and the answer `english` of `.bestpractices.json` said "English or Russian" and now say English; a run longer than a few minutes goes to the background — no rule of `tutor` says otherwise;
  - the template: the README as a description alone and `docs/README.md` beside it, which `tutor` already had, and `new-repo-check`'s test of a README, which `tutor`'s passes; CLAUDE.md's list of documents gains `docs/README.md`, as the template's has it. `.devcontainer/`, `repo-template/kinds/`, `.claude/` and the rules of RUN.md in the template did not change, so `tutor`'s container is as it was;
  - `tokens-report` prints an eighth column, the context of a main call among the first 80 of its session; measurements 4.1 takes it, with "—" in the rows before;
  - the rest does not concern `tutor`: the orchestrator's rebuilds and containers are the orchestrator's; the DPIA's one signature before the first child is the operator's; `exportPicture()` of `CanvasElement` and the descriptions of export v3 are `canvas`'s, `app`'s and `api`'s. Of the rows of `spec/run/DEBT.md` that name `tutor`, one is closed, the one of `api invite` has its date, and the rest are as before. `module-kit` `v0.3.11`, its latest, requires `spec` `v0.18.14` (the module proxy's `.mod`, read 2026-11-18).
- **`.github` `v0.1.33`** (`gh api repos/altnovatrail/.github/git/ref/tags/v0.1.33`, commit f1c7e9e; its release notes; read 2026-11-18): `v0.1.32` adds the composite action `.github/actions/toolchain` for a caller's own job that runs Go, and `v0.1.33` restores the Go caches of a miss by the prefix without the hash of `go.sum`, their modules dropped and their builds kept. "Canvas" calls the action before its work and after it with `save: "true"`, with the check `canvas`; its key now hashes `spec/`'s `go.sum` too, which the copy left out so that a move of the tag kept the entry, and the restore by the prefix keeps the builds on such a move instead. The image steps of "Samples" are `v0.1.33`'s `go.yml` byte for byte. The rule [ci](../.claude/rules/ci.md) says so.
- **What the merge brings.** No file of `.devcontainer/` changes, so the container needs no rebuild. The merge changes `go.mod` and `release.yml`, what the release is built from, and so releases the next patch of `tutor` with the same code.

— *Why:* the repository stands on the latest tag of `spec`, and the submodule and the Go modules of `spec` move together. The action is the copy of `go.yml`'s steps that `.github` now keeps for its callers: a release that changes them reaches "Canvas" with the move of its reference, and the comparison step by step that a move of `.github` asked is left for "Samples" alone. One task: no file of `.devcontainer/` changes, and the rest is one review.

— *Rejected:* **`module-kit` `v0.3.11`** — on `spec` `v0.18.14`, and `module-kit` moves only by a release of its own on the tag; **the action for "Samples"** — it restores and saves Go caches, which a job that runs no Go would keep empty in an entry of its own; **keeping the copy in "Canvas"** — `v0.1.33` changed the steps of the Go caches, and the copy would have to be rewritten by hand on each release; the action's save of the image on a miss, which the copies do not do, and its key with `spec/`'s `go.sum`, which costs the modules, not the builds, on a move of the tag, are its price; **measurements 4.1 without the eighth column** — the row prints it, and the first calls tell a long task from heavier reading (measurements 1.9 spec); **a split of the move into several tasks** — the change is less than one review.

— *Consequence:* `spec`; `go.mod`; `go.sum`; `.github/workflows/` — `ci.yml`, `codeql.yml`, `release.yml`, `scorecard.yml`; `.claude/rules/ci.md`; `CLAUDE.md`; `RUN.md`; `.bestpractices.json`; `docs/README.md`; `docs/architecture.md`; `run/DEBT.md`; `run/QUESTIONS.md`; `docs/plan/questions.md`; `docs/plan/E18.md`; `docs/measurements.md`.

**R134. The finding of the tasks on the photo of a page from paper is a call of its own, `CallFinding`, of the capability `task_finding`: one model gets the photo of the whole page once and gives a box round each task in pixels of the photo, and nothing of the page's text; the program puts the boxes in the order of the page and gives them as task frames in thousandths, and above each the frame of its instruction, when the page prints one apart from the task. Without the learner's request, each attempt first asks its model which way up the page lies, and the program turns the photo upright by the answer before the tasks are found on it, and gives the turn with the frames. The photo is the one picture an adapter shrinks: `provider.Photo` decodes it, fits it to the size at which the adapter's model takes it unshrunk (`provider.Fitter`), and encodes it anew as a JPEG, without the metadata of its file. The call is admitted by the author's word, its bound 2027-01-17, on the cascade of every call of a task from paper: Haiku 5.5 · `high`, Sonnet 5.5 · `medium` and Opus 5.5 · `medium`.**
- **The photo.** `app` sends a JPEG or a PNG of at most 4 MiB, scaled down to fit (the contract of `PaperService`), and a phone's camera gives 12 megapixels and more, which every provider would shrink. A shrunk picture leaves the model's pixels off the photo's, and a box it gives off the task (sources 1.19), so the adapter fits the photo to its own model before the request is built, and the text of the request names that size: the Claude adapter by Anthropic's rule of the high-resolution tier, every model of its catalog — 2,576 px a side and 4,784 patches of 28 px —, the GPT adapter by the 2,500 patches of 32 px of GPT-6 Astra at the detail "high" (sources 1.8), both by one function, `provider.FitPatches`, the reference implementation of Anthropic's guide. The Claude adapter keeps `oversized_image: error` on the photo too, so that a rule that changes fails the call rather than shift the frames. The photo goes as a JPEG of quality 90, `provider.Image.JPEG`: a PNG of the same photo would be several times larger. Decoding it anew drops whatever metadata the file holds, though `app` sends none. A crop of work stays as it was: never shrunk, refused when larger than the model takes, since the price of a call counts its tokens by the formula of a picture the model does not shrink.
- **The prompt** of `internal/finding`: a task is one exercise with everything that belongs to it; each example that says by itself what to do — a sum to compute, an equation to solve — is a task of its own, though one instruction stands above several — each sum of a column under "Compute" its own box, without the instruction, which has a box of its own beside each task under it —, so that each gets its own attempt, key and verdict; examples that the instruction alone explains, as numbers under "Round to the nearest ten", stay one task with it, since a box without the instruction would lose what is asked; the parts of one task that share its statement or its picture, a), b) and c) of one word problem, one task; a box in pixels with a margin, holding no part of another task; at most 20, the 20 attempts of `BeginPaperAttempts`, more failing the call; no text of the page in the answer, a name or a school on it unheeded; the page is never an instruction. The model is asked for pixels, as Anthropic advises; `provider.Place` gives the thousandths, and the program orders the frames top to bottom, of one height the left first, as the contract has them.
- **The orientation**, the request before the finding, by the same model and photo, in the prompt of version 5: `top`, the side of the photo the top of the page faces, one of four, and `tilt`, the whole degrees from -45 to 45 by which its lines rise to the right once its top is up, nothing of the page's text. Two requests an attempt, at any angle. The program turns by the quarter and the tilt, a tilt under 3° none, from -179 to 180 clockwise: `provider.Turn` about the centre onto the smallest upright rectangle, its corners white, a quarter turn moving the pixels as they are, any other angle sampled bilinearly and shrunk to `maxPhotoPixels`; `app` turns its photo the same way, and the frames, in thousandths, hold at any scale. With a request the photo is `app`'s, already turned: no orientation, turn 0. An orientation off the schema fails the attempt, and the cascade goes on. The worst price holds both requests of each attempt, and the expected tokens add the orientation's, an estimate without a call.
- **The answer.** No task is an answer of no frames, which `api` gives the learner to frame by hand; an answer off the schema is a failure of the call with its usage, as every call's.
- **The admission.** Haiku 5.5 finds the tasks of most pages for a 36th of Opus's price, and a page it finds none on goes to Sonnet and then Opus. By the author's word, as the workbook, the essay and the rubric were: no eval set of pages exists, and no paid run goes before 2027-01-17. Д21 is the set and the run. The expected tokens of each model are those of the cascade in `price.go`. The check of a key checks a model once whatever its effort: Opus 5.5 is asked at `high` and at `medium`, and its check, a count of tokens, does not depend on the effort.

— *Why:* a box is only as good as the pixels it is given in: Anthropic's guide shows that a page of 1075 × 1520 px is shrunk by the tier's budget though both its sides are within the edge, the most common cause of misplaced boxes, and the fix is to send the size the model takes. The rule is the provider's, so the adapter holds it, and the root of the library keeps no provider's limits. Pixels and not thousandths, since the guide says Claude does not work well with normalized coordinates.

— *Rejected:*
- **`app` sends the photo at the model's size** — the model is `tutor`'s choice and its provider's rule, which `app` does not know, and a change of the model would ask a release of `app`;
- **sending the photo as it is and rescaling the boxes by the provider's rule** — it holds only while the rule is the one the adapter assumes, and `oversized_image: error` would refuse the photo instead;
- **boxes in thousandths asked of the model** — the guide advises against it;
- **a new dependency for the scaling, `golang.org/x/image/draw`** — the mean of the pixels each one covers, in the standard library, keeps the strokes of print, and the module stays as it is;
- **a PNG of the fitted photo** — five to ten times the bytes of the JPEG, against a limit of 10 MB of base64 at Anthropic (sources 1.19);
- **an eval set before the code** — no paid run goes before 2027-01-17, and a missed task costs the learner a frame by hand;
- **the photo as it came** — the models found no task on a page lying on its side, upside down or askew, and `app`'s EXIF orientation turns the camera, not the paper;
- **one request for the side and the boxes** — the boxes would be on a page the model reads askew, the very failure;
- **only quarter turns**, or a request at each of the four — a page photographed at 30° stays askew, and four findings cost four times one;
- **Opus 5.5 · `medium` alone** — it found the tasks on every page of the author's check, but at 36 times Haiku's price, which found them on most;
- **a column of examples under one instruction as one task**, version 1's rule — each example of its own frame is a task of its own on the learner's list, with its own attempt, though a frame of several is checked task by task too;
- **the instruction inside each task's box**, or its text in the answer — the boxes of a column would overlap on it, and the finding gives no text of the page; the Check gets the instruction's crop, which a grid's rule may stand in.

— *Consequence:* `provider` — `Image.JPEG`, `Fitter`, `Photo`, `FitPatches`, `Turn`, `Photos.Turned`; `provider/claude` and `provider/gpt` — `Fit`; `internal/finding`; `finding.go`, `price.go`, `key.go`; `admission/models.go`; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs-budgets.json`; `docs/sources.md`; `run/DEBT.md`.

## The Check of a task from paper

**R135. A task from paper has two calls of its own beside the finding: `CallStatement`, the statement read from the task's crop into the list of its tasks — one, or up to 20 of a frame round several —, each with its statement as printed and a formula of the grammar the solver of `module-kit` takes, or none (`statement_reading`), for Recognise; and `CallChecking`, the Check (`task_checking`), one call at each press of Check in which a model judges the work with no key: from the task's crop as printed, the work and the crop of the instruction above the frame, it gives each task's verdict, how sure it is, what it read and where, what it took for given, the learner's errors with their places, and its own solution and answer, hidden, and for the frame the tutor's words and a ladder of 2 or 3 hints. Both are admitted by the author's word on the cascade of every call of a task from paper — Haiku 5.5 · `high`, Sonnet 5.5 · `medium` and Opus 5.5 · `medium` with thinking —, and their eval set, `eval/paper-gen.json`, ran on the stub alone.**

- **The statement**, version 3 of `internal/statement`: the tasks on the crop in the order of the page — top to bottom, and left to right in a row —, each sum or equation with an answer of its own a task, though one instruction stands above several, the parts of one word problem one task, at most `MaxTasks`, 20, more off the schema; each with its statement as printed, without the instruction above several, the learner's writing, the task's label, as `1)`, and the punctuation that parts it from the next, as `;` — a fraction with a bar as `\frac{…}{…}`, nested as printed, and a raised power as `^{…}`, LaTeX's way and no more of it, which `canvas` draws built up —, which `Read` cuts at 200 bytes, as the key keeps it; a task with no statement is off the schema. A formula only of a statement that is itself an expression or an equation, as `37 + 48 =` and `□ + 5 = 12`; a word problem, a picture to count in, a comparison, a drawing or a proof is none, since to put words into a formula is to solve; the signs of the solver's grammar in [spec/contracts/README.md](../spec/contracts/README.md), written as it says — `×` as `*`, a box as `x`, the `=` at the end gone; a sign that cannot be made out gives none. The crop goes as a photo does, by `provider.Photo`. `statement.Read` gives `[]Task`, the spaces at the ends of each field gone: whether the solver takes a formula is `module-kit`'s, and no verdict rests on it.
- **The Check**, version 1 of `internal/checking`: Image 1 is the task's crop as printed, sent as a photo by `provider.Photo`; Image 2 the work, the PNG of the tracing paper at its place with the strokes on it and the sheet's strokes in sight, never shrunk, on which every box lies; Image 3 the crop of the instruction, as a photo, when the finding gave one. The text names each image's size, how many tasks the frame holds, the language of the interface by its name, the learner's age category, and the attempt's history — each Check before with its verdicts, its tutor's words and the rungs shown — inside `<history>` as data, each on one line, a tag of the block written in it neutralised. The model numbers the tasks in the order of the page, solves each itself before it reads the work, and judges each: `correct`; `incorrect`, a wrong answer or work that holds a mistake; `no_answer`, no answer yet, the work so far with no mistake; or `unreadable`; with `low`, `medium` or `high` confidence, `low` when it may have misread or the task may allow the answer. Each box is in pixels of Image 2, all four corners 0 for none, which `provider.Place` brings to thousandths of the work. The tutor's words and the hints name no answer, nor a number or a step the learner did not write, nor the learner's gender, and speak to the age category; the hints lead from a question to a pointer at the first task not yet correct. The schema is one object of required fields, as every schema of the library: `tasks`, each with `task`, `assumptions`, `solution`, `answer`, `read`, `read_box`, `errors` and `verdict` and `confidence` last, so that the model writes its solution before its verdict; `tutor_words`; `hints`. `checking.Read` holds the answer to it strictly: every task of the frame once, from 1 to its count, each box within Image 2, 2 or 3 hints.
- **The library** — `PaperCheck{Route, Statement, Work, Instruction, Tasks, Language, AgeCategory, History}` with `[]CheckTurn{Verdicts, Words, Rungs}`, `Tutor.CheckPaper` → `PaperChecked{Slots, TutorWords, Hints, Outcome}`, each `SlotChecked{Task, Verdict, Confidence, Read, ReadBox, Assumptions, Errors, Solution, Answer}` with `[]CheckError{Box, Where, Kind, Note}`; a frame of no task or of more than 20, a crop that is no picture and a work that is no PNG fail before any call. Once the cascade is over, the program holds the tutor's words and each rung to the leak check of `module-kit` by `PaperBreach`'s search: the hidden answer of every task not yet correct, each of its numbers in any notation and an answer of words as a phrase, a value the learner wrote, as read in any slot, being no leak. Words that name one are left out, "", and such a rung, or an empty one, is `PaperFallback`'s text of its rung. `PaperTask{Route, Statement}` and `Tutor.ReadStatement` → `StatementRead{Tasks}` are the statement's. Their expected tokens in `price.go` are those of the cascade.
- **The eval set** — `tutor-eval gen paper` makes `eval/paper-gen.json` by code (`internal/gen`, `internal/paperset`): six kinds in turn — an expression, an equation, a word problem, a choice, an open task, which has no key, and a frame of two to four tasks `□ + p = q` under one instruction, each with the solver's key —, the work of each task right, wrong and blank in turn. A case's pictures are drawn from it when a run asks them, the statement in synth's hand and the work under it, so the set is one file and no folder of samples. `run -kind statement|check -paper F` asks the cases of a part by each model of the cascade alone, `haiku-high`, `sonnet-medium` and `opus-medium`, unless `-readers` names others, and the program judges each answer: the statement right when it lists the case's tasks and the solver gives each true formula's answer, or both are none; the Check right when each task's verdict is the one its truth comes to — right work `correct`, wrong `incorrect`, blank `no_answer`, an open task any —, its record keeping each verdict and value read. The Check of the set asks in English for an adult, as the author's profile is. `report` counts the share right by configuration and kind of case.

— *Why:* a page holds tasks of any form, whose kinds no program knows all, and a model judging the picture whole checks what a key leaves "Couldn't check this one", as a grid whose rows and columns share their cells; a mistake of the model now and then is better than many tasks unchecked. One call gives the verdict, the places and the hints, so a hint reads the work no second time; the instruction goes too, since a grid's rule may stand there. The schema's order makes the model work the task out before it reads the learner's answer. The model is asked not to give its answers away, and the leak check is what holds. A frame round several tasks is the most common case on paper, and each of its tasks gets a verdict of its own. The statement stays for Recognise, which shows the learner what was read; nothing of the Check rests on it. The pictures of the set are drawn at run time, so that a set of hundreds is one file; their print is synth's hand, which measures the plumbing and not a printed page.

— *Rejected:*
- **the program's verdict by a key** that the solver or a model's solution derives, with the work read by `handwriting_reading` — no list of a key's kinds covers a page, and three calls cost more than one;
- **a second call for the hints and the place of the mistake** — it reads the work again for what the Check already saw;
- **the strokes or the writing history in the request** — the picture holds what the Check needs, and the history is biometric data;
- **the numbers the statement prints exempt from the leak check** — the Check carries no text of the statement, and Recognise may have read none; a hidden answer the page prints only gives a rung its fallback text, which costs words, not the answer;
- **the leak check of a correct task's answer** — the answer is the learner's own, written on the page;
- **samples of the protocol for the set** — the protocol's sample is a sheet of strokes, and a task from paper has a photo; a format of a photo's sample is `spec`'s to decide when a set of real pages comes (Д22);
- **a paid run before the code** — no paid run goes before 2027-01-17, and the author checks the calls by hand on `dev-ai`;
- **a label or a separator cut from the statement by code** — the prompt alone leaves them out.

— *Consequence:* `internal/statement`, `internal/checking`; `paper.go`, `check.go`, `paperwords.go`, `price.go`; `admission/models.go`; `internal/paperset`, `internal/gen` — `paper.go`, `cmd/tutor-eval` — `gen paper`, the kinds `statement` and `check` of `run`, and their report; `eval/paper-gen.json`; `docs/architecture.md`; `run/DEBT.md`.

**R136. The platform's debug mode is `Route.Debug` of a call, which api sets for an account with the right to it, on a local run or on the deployment, and the library takes only when `Config.Debug` is on: its first model serves the three calls of a task from paper and their tutor's words in place of those of the list, one attempt with no cascade, and its record gets each call of a model — the model as asked, its answer as it returned it, its usage, what failed. It bypasses the list and the admission's refusal; on any other call it changes nothing. A Check the mode exports, `DebugCase` of the contract, is a case of the set of a task from paper: `tutor-eval run -kind statement|check -paper` takes a folder of them, and they are kept in `eval/paper-cases/`.**
- **Where it hooks.** `chooseCall`, the one choice of a call, so that `Price`, the reserve of api's quota and the call itself follow the same models; the effort and the thinking go with each model as they go with one of the list. A route with the mode to a library with it off fails before any model.
- **The exported cases** (`internal/paperset`, `exported.go`). Each file of the folder, in the order of their names, is a case of the part `dev`: its pictures those the Check sent — the task's crop and the instruction's through `provider.Photo`, as the library sends them, and the PNG of the work —, its truth the developer's — for each task of the frame the value read and the verdict, `so_far_correct` among them for work with no answer and no mistake, and its statement when Recognise read one —, and its kind follows from that truth, a frame of more than one task `several`, one with no key `exported`. The Check's truth comes to a verdict as a drawn case's does, `so_far_correct` and `unchecked` to `no_answer`; the statement's run asks only the cases whose every task has a statement. The run's log names the folder by the SHA-256 of its files' names and bytes (`paperset.Digest`). So a task a model misread on the developer's paper is measured again by any model, where a drawn case measures the plumbing alone (Д22).
- **Where they are kept** — `eval/paper-cases/`, in git: the cases are the tests of the reading of real paper, and each run measures the same files on any machine. A file holds the pictures of the work of the profile it was made in, so the folder keeps only those of the developer's own paper, never a learner's, whatever the profile, since no learner's work goes into a repository; the mode is open only to an account with its right.
- **The record** is `Debug.Record`, called once the models have answered, each attempt of a cascade with its place, `DebugCall.Attempt`, not `Usage` or `Outcome`: those hold no content and may be logged, and the answer is the model's reading of the learner's work. api keeps the record in memory for the account that set the mode alone.

— *Why:* the author finds the errors of reading and chooses models, on a local run or on the deployment, without changing the list for each try; a choice that passes `chooseCall` keeps the price and the quota true.

— *Rejected:*
- **an adapter of api that wraps the dial** — the clients are kept by model, so it would read the choice at each call, and lose `Fit` of the adapter, which `provider.Photo` needs;
- **the answer in `Usage`** — a log would get it;
- **the cases outside the repository**, as the author's samples in `samples/` — a run elsewhere would not find them;
- **the exported case in the file of `paper-gen.json`** — its pictures are no case's to draw, and a file a case lets the developer add and drop cases by hand.

— *Consequence:* `debug.go`, `tutor.go`, `internal/paperset`, `cmd/tutor-eval/paper.go`, `eval/paper-cases/`; `docs/architecture.md`.

## The attempts of a call of a task from paper

**R137. Each of the three calls of a task from paper — `CallFinding`, `CallStatement` and `CallChecking` — is a cascade: its entry names Haiku 5.5 · `high`, Sonnet 5.5 · `medium` and Opus 5.5 · `medium`, all with thinking, and `cascade` asks them one at a time, a later one only when the one before did not do by the program's rule of its call. The author's word admits the three at the rung "author" until 2027-01-17, the last day of every admission by the author's word; `price.go` expects the tokens of each, and `Price` sums them, which `api` reserves.**
- **Did it do.** A failure of the call — no answer, an answer off the schema, more than 20 tasks — goes to the next model in every call; besides, the finding when it found no task, and the Check when a verdict is of `low` confidence or a task `unreadable` (`sure`): `no_answer` is what the work holds. The statement goes on by a failure alone: no formula is an answer.
- **How it ends.** A provider's refusal for a limit or of the key ends the cascade as its outcome: the next model is of the same provider and key. Otherwise the result is that of the last model that answered, though it did not do, or, when none answered, the last failure. `cascadeFloor`: a later attempt is not begun with less than 10 s before the deadline of its context. `Outcome.Usage` holds every attempt's usage, since each is billed.
- **Where.** `prepare` marks each `call` of a cascading kind with its `attempt`, and `kinds` has `cascade: true` and `models: 3` for the three; the module's calls keep their reading mode, the pair or `single`. The word admits the entries of the finding, the statement and the Check on the versions of their prompts that `admission/models.go` fingerprints — the finding's 5, the statement's 3 and the Check's 1 —: a new version changes their rules, not the models. The photo of the finding and the crops of the statement and of the Check fit to each model in turn, and the work goes as the PNG it is. In the debug mode a first model chosen is no cascade: the call asks what was chosen.
- **The expected tokens** — of the finding and the statement, measurements 3.25: of the author's calls on `dev-ai` in the debug mode, the input of Opus and Haiku for the finding and of Haiku for the statement, the output by model; Sonnet's input as theirs and its output as Opus's. Of the Check, an estimate of its three pictures and its answer, which no call has measured yet. They are an estimate, not an eval's.
- **Eval.** `runner.Configs` gains `haiku-high` and `sonnet-medium`; the set of a task from paper measures each model of the cascade alone, so that a run tells which does how often. A run of the cascade as it serves, its later models on the cases the earlier missed, is Д23.

— *Why:* the author saw on the debug mode that Haiku 5.5 sometimes finds no task on a page where Opus 5.5 always does, at 36 times the price ($0.0108 against $0.0003). A cascade pays Haiku's price where Haiku does and Opus's where only Opus does. Whether a call did is the program's judgement, and each call has its own: a missed page is no frames, a Check one with no verdict unsure and no task unreadable.

— *Rejected:* **Opus alone** — the price; **Haiku alone** — the pages it misses; **the statement going on when it gives no formula** — a word problem has none, and Sonnet and Opus would be asked for every one; **the Check going on by a failure alone** — an unsure verdict or an unreadable answer is what a stronger model judges or reads; **the cascade on the module's calls** — their pair or `single` are admitted by eval; **the expected tokens of Haiku alone** — `api` would reserve too little for a call that goes to Opus; **a run of the cascade as it serves now** — its configuration asks steps of several calls, beyond the cascade's code (Д23).

— *Consequence:* `cascade.go`, `tutor.go`, `debug.go`, `paper.go`, `check.go`, `finding.go`, `sheet.go`, `price.go`; `admission/models.go` — `paperCascade`, `paperWord`; `internal/runner`, `internal/hint`, `internal/workbook`, `internal/essay`, `internal/measure`, `internal/reading` — the outputs of the two configurations; `cmd/tutor-eval/paper.go`; their tests; `docs/architecture.md`; `docs/measurements.md`; `run/DEBT.md`.

**R138. The finding of the tasks takes the learner's request about the page: `TaskFinding` carries the request and the frames on the page now, those the AI found and those the learner drew, and the prompt gives the request to the model as data in `<request>`, with the frames numbered as `app` numbers them in `<frames>`; the answer stays boxes alone, and the program drops one that covers a frame the learner drew. The author's word admits the prompt on the cascade until 2027-01-17, at version 5, which first asks which way up the page lies and gives each task's instruction a box of its own.**
- **The prompt.** Rules 6 and 7 of the instructions, and rule 8 the box of each task's instruction: the request says which tasks to take and how to split them, and nothing else is taken from it; the boxes take the place of the found frames, and none repeats a drawn one. The instructions stay one text for every request, so they are cached; the request and the frames follow the size in the text. The request goes on one line, and a tag of either block written in it — in any case, with any spaces in it — is neutralised, as `inBlock` of the essay does. Without a request the text is that of the finding alone.
- **The frames** go in pixels of the photo the model sees, converted after `provider.Photo` fits it, in the order of the page — top to bottom, of one height the left first —, which is the order of `app`'s numbers.
- **The program's check**: a frame found whose overlap with a drawn frame is at least 0.8 of their union is dropped, `sameAsDrawn`, once the cascade is over: a model that gave only such frames found tasks, and the next is not asked for them. `CoversDrawn` is the check, which `api`'s stub calls too.
- **Eval.** The set of a task from paper has a run of the finding, `finding`: the cases of several tasks, each a column of examples under one instruction, with the request "each example a task of its own"; right when each example has a box of its own on the picture, apart from the others, the middle of its line in that box alone. It ran on the stub alone; a set of whole pages and a paid run stay Д21.

— *Why:* the learner names which tasks they were set and how a book groups them; a program still checks what comes back. The cached instructions keep the request's price at a few hundred tokens. The cases of several tasks are the nearest the set holds to a column of a page.

— *Rejected:* the request in the instructions, which would break their cache and mix the learner's words with the platform's; the frames in thousandths, which the model does not see; a set of whole pages with the request (Д21).

— *Consequence:* `finding.go`, `internal/finding` and their tests; `admission/models.go` — the entry of the finding; `internal/paperset`, `cmd/tutor-eval`; `docs/architecture.md`; `run/DEBT.md`.

## The tutor's words of a task from paper

**R139. The tutor's words of a task from paper beyond the Check: `Tutor.PaperWords` words a rung of the Check's ladder put another way, "Explain differently", with no picture — `PaperAgain`, by `internal/paperwords` version 2, `CallPaperHint`, `hint` —, on the cascade of every call of a task from paper, admitted by the author's word, its bound 2027-01-17. The Check gives the tutor's words and the ladder itself. `PaperBreach` is the program's rule of the cascade and `api`'s check of the words of a task from paper; `PaperFallback` the platform's fallback texts.**
- **The prompt.** `<tasks>` lists every task of the frame by its number, with its statement as Recognise read it, the Check's own answer and solution, which the learner never sees, and the value read of the learner's answer, each `none` when absent; `<asked>` the hints given on the task, the last the one to put another way. The request names the rung's task, the rung, the language, its limit of words from `words.HintWords` and the style. The rung goes no nearer the answer: a question stays a question, and a pointer points at the same place. The answer is `{hint}` alone. `RequestOf` refuses a rung with no tasks, a rung beyond 3 and a task the frame has not; `TestFrozenV2` keeps the fingerprint, which `admission/models.go` names.
- **The cascade.** `PaperWords` asks Haiku 5.5 · `high`, Sonnet 5.5 · `medium` and Opus 5.5 · `medium` one at a time; a later one only when the one before failed, answered off the schema, or its words broke `PaperBreach`. The answer is the last model's that answered, whether or not its words passed: `api` checks them again and gives the fallback text in their place.
- **The checks.** `PaperBreach(text, language, tasks)` is `BreachLeak` when the leak check of `module-kit` finds the hidden answer of a task given — each number of it in any notation, unless the statements print it or the learner wrote it, and an answer of words of two letters or more as a phrase, unless the statements or the learner's answers hold it —, and `BreachForm` when the words are empty or break `words.Hint`, with the statements' letters allowed. A task already correct goes with no answer: its answer is the learner's.
- **The fallback texts.** `PaperFallback` gives at rung 0 the opening question of the platform, which holds no number, in English and Russian, English for any other language, and at rungs 1 to 3 the templates of `hint.Fallback`; the Check gives a rung that failed its rung's text.
- **The price** is the expected tokens of each model in `price.go`, an estimate of the text alone, and the debug mode records the call as the tutor's words.

— *Why:* a task from paper has no module, so no author's question, traps, techniques or solution: the words are the model's from what the Check worked out. The Check gives the words and the whole ladder in its one call, so only "Explain differently" asks again, and it needs no picture: the rung it puts another way already says where. The Check's hidden answers are the keys the leak check searches for, as a module's key is.

— *Rejected:* Opus 5.5 · `high` alone, as the hint of a module, which costs the most on every hint; Haiku alone, which leaves no model to take over when its words fail; the opening question and a rung's words as calls of their own, which the Check gives; the crop of the work sent again for another way, which reads the work for nothing the rung lacks; a single letter as a leak phrase, which the words of any hint hold.

— *Consequence:* `internal/paperwords`, `paperwords.go`, `check.go`, `tutorwords.go`, `price.go`, `debug.go`, `tutor.go`, `admission/models.go` and their tests; `run/DEBT.md`; `docs/architecture.md`.
